## 1. [[Perf][MoE] Skip the combine staging copy in flashinfer_nvlink_one_sided](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65498)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65498
- **Author:** [vadiklyutiy](https://github.com/vadiklyutiy)
- **Merged By:** [arpera](https://github.com/arpera)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 23:46:54
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65498/files) (2):**
  - `tests/distributed/test_mnnvl_alltoall.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/prepare_finalize/flashinfer_nvlink_one_sided.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR overrides allocate_fused_expert_output in FlashInferNVLinkOneSidedPrepareAndFinalize so experts write their reduced output directly into FlashInfer's symmetric combine workspace, letting the one-sided combine detect a workspace-backed payload and skip the moeA2APrepareCombineKernel staging copy. It adds a multi-GPU test that verifies payload_in_workspace=True and exact output. This is a performance optimization for the flashinfer_nvlink_one_sided MoE all-to-all backend.

**Technical impact**  
Removes a per-layer HBM read/write copy of every received row before combine, yielding measured microbenchmark gains of up to ~161 us at 16K tokens/rank and ~1.4-3.2% end-to-end prefill throughput improvement on B200 EP4. The hook is guarded by TopKWeightAndReduceNoOP, bf16/fp16 dtype, and shape/device match, falling back to prior behavior otherwise. It reuses the existing allocate_fused_expert_output hook already used by AGRS, so no new synchronization or graph-capture concerns are introduced.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness depends on subtle lifetime reasoning: peers may still read a rank's combine region after combine returns, and safety relies on the next layer's dispatch waiting on completion flags before experts overwrite the region. A regression in that ordering could cause data races or corruption that the single new test may not fully catch. The multi-GPU test is skipped in CI due to requires_ptrace, so coverage in automated CI is limited. Fallback conditions (dtype, NoOP reducer, shape/device) must remain aligned with FlashInfer's workspace sizing assumptions.

**Key insights**  
The change is small (22 lines of production code) but behaviorally meaningful, eliminating a measurable copy in a hot path. The guard conditions correctly preserve old behavior for unsupported configurations, and the PR provides strong empirical validation (microbenchmark, nsys, end-to-end, gsm8k). The main review focus should be the workspace lifetime/ordering argument and ensuring the new test runs in environments with ptrace capability.

## 2. [[Test][UMBP] Cover end-to-end restore while the embedded DRAM pool is evicting](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64658)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64658
- **Author:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged By:** [tjtanaa](https://github.com/tjtanaa)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 23:12:12
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64658/files) (1):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/umbp_integration/test_mori_token_e2e.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The umbp_llm fixture now accepts a dict of pool options via indirect parametrization (extracting tensor_parallel_size and forwarding the rest into the embedded connector config), and a new test_restore_under_pool_eviction test uses a 64 MiB pool with explicit watermarks to exercise end-to-end restore while the DRAM pool is evicting. This fills a coverage gap where existing UMBP e2e tests never triggered the connector/scheduler eviction path.

**Technical impact**  
Test-only change with no production code impact. It broadens fixture flexibility (pool capacity and watermark tuning per test) and adds a deterministic LRU-eviction scenario that asserts the newest prompt restores exactly from external tokens while the oldest is evicted (no external token growth).

**Potential risks**  
The test is sensitive to hardcoded assumptions: pool size depends on Qwen3-0.6B KV layout on MORI's 64 KiB pages, and determinism relies on prompts running one at a time for LRU commit order. The description notes that equal watermarks or a high watermark at or below MORI's default low of 0.7 silently disable eviction, so the chosen 0.75/0.5 values are load-bearing and could make the test silently pass without eviction if defaults change. It also depends on a specific MORI build with UMBP.

**Key insights**  
The fixture refactor from a scalar param to a dict is a clean, backward-compatible way to expose pool knobs, but it changes the parametrization contract for existing tp1/tp2 cases. The known watermark bug (eviction silently disabled) is a real hazard for this test's validity and is correctly deferred to a separate PR; reviewers should confirm the test actually fails when eviction is disabled to avoid a false-positive guard.

## 3. [[Bugfix] Honor add_generation_prompt and continue_final_message in the DeepSeek V4.1 tokenizer](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62729)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #62729
- **Author:** [JohnZhao01](https://github.com/JohnZhao01)
- **Merged By:** [chaunceyjiang](https://github.com/chaunceyjiang)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 23:09:50
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62729/files) (5):**
  - `tests/parser/engine/test_deepseek_v41.py`
  - `tests/tokenizers_/test_deepseek_v41.py`
  - `vllm/parser/deepseek_v41.py`
  - `vllm/tokenizers/deepseek_v41.py`
  - `vllm/tokenizers/deepseek_v41_encoding.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes the DeepSeek V4.1 Python tokenizer so apply_chat_template honors add_generation_prompt and continue_final_message, which were previously dropped. The encoder now appends an assistant turn prefix after a final assistant message by default, leaves the final assistant message open (no EOS) when continue_final_message is set, and rejects continue_final_message without a final assistant message. The V4.1 parser also declares turn_boundary_tokens so prompt-closed reasoning is scoped to the current turn, making continued answers after </think> return as content rather than reasoning.

**Technical impact**  
Changes prompt rendering for conversations ending in an assistant message: they now get a new <｜Assistant｜> turn (with <think> or </think>) matching the DeepSeek reference encoder, and add_generation_prompt=false now actually suppresses the assistant prefix for user/developer/system-final conversations. This aligns the Python frontend with the Rust frontend fix (#62189) and fixes widespread empty/garbled replies (210/280 empty before, 2/280 after in E2E). The parser change affects how reasoning vs content is split when the prompt already closed reasoning.

**Potential risks**  
Behavior change for clients sending an assistant prefill without continue_final_message: they now get a new turn instead of a continuation after EOS, which drops lm-eval humaneval_instruct to 0.0 (0.9573 with the flag). Callers passing add_generation_prompt=false for user-final conversations now get a prompt without the assistant prefix, which can produce continuations of user text and special-token leakage in generation (intended for /tokenize or scoring). The wo_eos path is only reachable via direct encode_messages calls, and the final-system case is intentionally left to #62459, so scope boundaries must be respected.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-validated: token-for-token comparison against the reference encoder across seven conversation shapes, unchanged rendering for 335/342 saved request bodies, and a large E2E improvement in empty replies. The parser turn_boundary_tokens addition is essential for continue_final_message to work end to end, since without it continued answers after </think> are misclassified as reasoning. Reviewers should focus on the intentional behavior change for assistant-prefill requests and the lm-eval regression, which is a client-usage issue rather than a correctness bug.

## 4. [[Core][CUDA Graph] Separate prefill (token) and decode (request) CUDA graph capture sizes](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63406)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63406
- **Author:** [Pb314314](https://github.com/Pb314314)
- **Merged By:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 22:50:28
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63406/files) (17):**
  - `docs/design/torch_compile.md`
  - `tests/compile/test_config.py`
  - `tests/v1/cudagraph/test_cudagraph_manager.py`
  - `tests/v1/spec_decode/test_dynamic_sd_cug.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_ubatch_slicing.py`
  - `vllm/config/compilation.py`
  - `vllm/config/vllm.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/config.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/decoder_replay_cudagraph.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/specialized_decode/runtime/metadata_graphs.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/cudagraph_utils.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/model_runner.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/autoregressive/speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/dflash/speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/multi_module_mtp/speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/standalone_ar/speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/target_dependent_ar/speculator.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds five CompilationConfig fields that split CUDA graph capture sizing into prefill (token-keyed) and uniform decode (request-keyed) kinds, plus a decode token budget, with new default grids on SM100 and a deprecation path for cudagraph_capture_sizes/max_cudagraph_capture_size on model runner V2. CudaGraphManager now takes a decode_query_lens mapping and builds separate prefill and decode capture descriptors, and each speculator (EAGLE, DFlash, multi-module MTP, standalone/target-dependent AR) passes its own query length. The motivation is that a single token list under-covers wide uniform decode batches at large K, forcing eager execution and large throughput losses at high concurrency.

**Technical impact**  
Substantial behavior change to graph planning: prefill graphs now reach 8192 tokens (54 sizes) and decode graphs reach up to 1024 requests within an 8192-token budget on SM100, with per-runner query-length-aware shape computation via decode_cudagraph_capture_shapes and decode_cudagraph_capture_max_tokens. Reported gains are large at high concurrency (up to ~3.7x) with graph memory growing from ~4.7 to ~12.5 GiB and KV cache shrinking to 0.82-0.98x of main. Legacy fields are deprecated but still seed both kinds with a warning, and V1 rejects the new fields.

**Potential risks**  
Graph memory growth reduces KV capacity (notably 0.821x on GLM-5.3 and 0.879x on DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash with AV), and the known MiMo DFlash limitation makes the --max-model-len auto recipe fail at startup on 4x GB200. Off-grid padding can slow dense single-GPU models by 4-11% at some sizes, and the deprecated-field path does not reproduce main's graphs for adaptive verification, DSpark, or one-token draft decodes. Correctness deltas are within run-to-run noise but some single-pair GSM8K drops (e.g. K3 1283->1279) are retained, and off-SM100 defaults are computed rather than measured.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is that batch size means tokens for prefill/mixed and requests x q for uniform decode, so one shared token list cannot cover both well; separating units and adding a token budget fixes decode coverage without unbounded memory. The 8192-token budget is justified by measurements showing no steady-state throughput gain above it while graph memory grows sharply and FlashMLA sparse decode fails past ~14.5K rows. The PR is well-tested (new config and manager tests, 13/13 GPU cases) but is a large, high-risk change to core graph planning that warrants careful review of the deprecation path and memory tradeoffs.

---

## 5. [[ROCm][Perf][MiniMax-M3.1] Score DSpark 7 decode locally below 3 requests instead of via indexer CP](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65432)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65432
- **Author:** [Fangzhou-Ai](https://github.com/Fangzhou-Ai)
- **Merged By:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 22:34:49
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65432/files) (2):**
  - `tests/kernels/attention/test_minimax_m3_nvfp4_index.py`
  - `vllm/models/minimax_m3/amd/indexer_nvfp4.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a CP_MIN_REQS = 3 gate in the MiniMax M3 NVFP4 indexer so that DSpark 7 decode steps (query_len == 2 * CP_QUERY_LEN, i.e. 8 rows per request) with fewer than 3 requests bypass the context-parallel push kernel and use the single-head scorer instead. The motivation is that CP's cross-rank handshakes cost a fixed ~13 us per layer regardless of batch size, while the single-head scorer takes 6-9 us at 1-2 requests, yielding ~4% and ~3% decode-step p50 improvements at N=1 and N=2. A new unit test test_decode_cp_gate covers the gate boundary and the DSpark 3 (4-row) path.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior change is confined to scorer selection: the two scorers are documented as bit-identical, so top-k ids and attention output are unchanged. The gate is keyed on request count rather than context length because under CUDA graphs max_seq_len is the model maximum at capture time, making a context-aware choice infeasible without per-batch-size graphs. DSpark 3 (query_len == CP_QUERY_LEN) and batches of 3+ requests retain the existing CP path, and the change is a single added condition in _decode_cp plus a constant.

**Potential risks**  
The gate is conservative for short contexts (single-head wins up to 16 requests at 30K) and regresses for very long contexts (~800K tokens at 1-2 requests, where CP is 10-30% faster), so workloads with rare ultra-long single-stream decodes could see a slowdown. The threshold was tuned only on MI355X TP4 with DSpark 7 at ~150K contexts; the 4-row (DSpark 3) crossover was not measured, and DBO-on behavior for this change alone was not benchmarked. End-to-end gains are small (2-4%) and near the reported +-0.6% server-to-server noise, so the benefit is modest and measurement-sensitive.

**Key insights**  
This is a well-scoped micro-optimization backed by detailed kernel-level profiling and a 28-cell bit-identical correctness sweep, with a clean unit test for the gate. The main design tradeoff is that CUDA graph capture prevents context-aware dispatch, forcing a request-count heuristic that is optimal around the target 150K workload but suboptimal at both very short and very long contexts. Reviewers should confirm the gate condition ordering and that the new test's monkeypatching of index_cp.get is robust to future refactors of the CP host module.

---

## 6. [[ROCm][Step5] Exercise the real CSA decode and engine paths in tests](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64836)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64836
- **Author:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 21:40:10
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64836/files) (3):**
  - `tests/models/step5/test_amd_csa_paged.py`
  - `tests/models/step5/test_amd_fp8_kv_init.py`
  - `tests/models/step5/test_amd_model.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a numerical kernel test in test_amd_csa_paged.py that exercises the real CSA decode chain (summary scores, region selection, gated sparse decode attention) against FP32 references across TP1/TP8 layouts, BF16/FP8 KV, and single-token plus 4-row MTP verify. Adds test_amd_model.py, an end-to-end engine test on the reduced registry checkpoint that runs scored prefill and sparse decode, verifies the scored-prefill warmup fires, and compares decode logprobs to a teacher-forced re-prefill. Removes test_amd_fp8_kv_init.py since the new engine test subsumes its FP8 KV and block-size checks.

**Technical impact**  
Replaces mocked/dispatch-only Step5 ROCm tests with real numerical and engine-path coverage, closing a gap where the CSA decode chain was never validated against a reference or driven through vLLM. The kernel test pins scores, scale, masks, paging, and the gate to FP32 references, while the engine test validates decode wiring end to end. Net effect is stronger regression protection for the ROCm Step5 path with modest added test runtime.

**Potential risks**  
The engine test's logprob tolerance (1e-2) is explicitly loose because dummy weights yield near-flat logits, so it only catches gross breakage and may miss subtle decode wiring bugs. The test relies on monkeypatching warmup.run_step5_scored_prefill_warmup and on internal config attributes (sparse_config topk, cache_config), which could break on refactors. Removing test_amd_fp8_kv_init.py drops a standalone initialization check, so coverage now depends on the new engine test running successfully. Hardware-specific skips (on_mi3xx) mean the tests won't run on non-ROCm or older ROCm CI.

**Key insights**  
This is a meaningful test-quality improvement: it converts shallow Step5 ROCm tests into real numerical and integration checks, which is the right direction for a complex sparse-attention path. The author is transparent about the engine test's limited sensitivity, correctly deferring precise numerical validation to the kernel test. Reviewers should confirm the removed FP8 KV init test is fully redundant and that the monkeypatch targets and config attribute accesses are stable.

## 7. [[Bugfix][Frontend] Accept an empty tools array like OpenAI](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65547)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65547
- **Author:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 21:08:53
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65547/files) (4):**
  - `tests/tool_use/test_chat_completion_request_validations.py`
  - `tests/tool_use/test_chat_completions.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/openai/chat_completion/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/serve/tokenize/protocol.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR changes the OpenAI-compatible chat completions validation so that `tools: []` is treated like an omitted tools field (matching current OpenAI behavior), instead of being rejected with a 400. It normalizes empty tools to None and downgrades `tool_choice` of `auto`/`required` to `none`, while still rejecting a named function that is not in the (empty) tools list. It also improves the mismatch error message to name the function, updates tests, and removes the now-unneeded `/tokenize` workaround.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior of the Python frontend's request validation changes: empty tools arrays now yield normal completions, and `tool_choice` normalization differs for empty lists. The named-function error now reports parameter `tool_choice` with the function name, which is a user-visible message change. The Rust frontend still rejects `tools: []`, so the two frontends diverge until the follow-up lands, and the new integration test is skipped under the Rust frontend.

**Potential risks**  
Frontend inconsistency: Python accepts `tools: []` while Rust still returns 400, which could confuse clients and CI depending on which frontend is active. The normalization logic only special-cases dict `tool_choice` (named function) and string values; other shapes or future tool_choice variants could behave unexpectedly. Removing the `/tokenize` workaround relies entirely on the new validation path, so any regression there would surface in tokenize. Error message/parameter changes may break tests or clients asserting on the old text.

**Key insights**  
The change reverses an earlier check (#39780) to track upstream OpenAI semantics, with live before/after evidence provided. The implementation is compact and localized to `check_tool_usage`, but the cross-frontend mismatch is explicitly acknowledged as a follow-up, meaning the fix is only partial for deployments using the Rust frontend. Test coverage is reasonable (unit parametrization plus an integration test), though the integration test is gated off for Rust.

## 8. [[Rust Frontend] Support --skip-tokenizer-init](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64697)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64697
- **Author:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 20:59:47
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`

  - `rust/src/server/examples/external_engine_openai_qwen.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/config.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/lib.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/render.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/completions.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tokenize.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/backend/hf/mod.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/backend/hf/model_files.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/backend/mod.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/error.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/lib.rs`
  - `rust/src/tokenizer/src/error.rs`
  - `rust/src/tokenizer/src/hf.rs`
  - `rust/src/tokenizer/src/hf/added_tokens.rs`
  - `rust/src/tokenizer/src/lib.rs`
  - `rust/src/tokenizer/src/tekken.rs`
  - `rust/src/tokenizer/src/test_utils.rs`
  - `rust/src/tokenizer/src/tiktoken.rs`
  - `rust/src/tokenizer/src/unavailable.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Implements --skip-tokenizer-init in the Rust frontend so token-ID-only serving works without loading a tokenizer, matching Python's skip_tokenizer_init. The flag moves from unsupported markers into ModelArgs, is forwarded to the managed engine, and a null-object UnavailableTokenizer plus a TokenizerlessChatBackend reject text/chat operations with a 400. Loading skips all tokenizer-side files, and structured outputs, stop strings, bad_words, echo, and string-form logprobs are rejected.

**Technical impact**  
Touches the tokenizer crate (TokenizerError becomes an enum with Backend/Unavailable variants), text backend loading and request preparation, chat backend selection, CLI/config plumbing, and server routes for completions, /tokenize, and /detokenize. The null-object design keeps the Tokenizer interface and consumers unchanged, with only loading and a has_tokenizer flag aware of absence. Tokenizer errors on /tokenize and /detokenize now map through text_submit_error, so validation errors become 400 instead of 500.

**Potential risks**  
Behavioral divergences from Python are intentional but notable: stop strings and bad_words are rejected rather than silently ignored, gpt-oss Harmony chat is rejected, and structured outputs are rejected even when an independent engine has a tokenizer. The TokenizerError enum change is a breaking API change for any external consumers of vllm-tokenizer. EOS handling now falls back to the first config.json EOS id, which differs from Python's None primary EOS. The completions logprobs check depends on both request and server-default return_tokens_as_token_ids, which is subtle.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-tested with unit tests across vllm-text, vllm-chat, vllm-server, and vllm-cmd, plus an E2E run on GB200 confirming the target test passes where main fails. The design cleanly isolates tokenizer absence to loading and a single has_tokenizer flag, minimizing blast radius. The documented differences from Python and the explicit note that a tokenizer-optional Harmony chat backend is a possible follow-up show good awareness of scope boundaries.

  - `rust/src/parser/src/unified/muse.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests/responses.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds typed IncompleteToolCall errors to ToolParserError and UnifiedParserError, replacing generic ParsingFailed at every site where output ends inside a tool call across all Rust parser families. UnifiedParserState::finish now drops the parser buffer on that specific error instead of re-emitting truncated markup as assistant text, matching Python's parser engine behavior for max_tokens, stop strings, and EOS.

**Technical impact**  
Changes user-visible output for truncated tool calls: streaming and non-streaming (chat completions and Responses API) now omit incomplete tool-call markup and keep only preceding text, while other finish errors still fall back to text. The is_incomplete_tool_call helper covers both direct unified errors and tool-parser errors carried through CombinedParser, and the drop policy is centralized in the single consumer of UnifiedParser::finish.

**Potential risks**  
Behavior diverges from Python for legacy parsers (Rust drops markup for all families, Python keeps raw content in non-streaming for some). Calls whose header already streamed remain with truncated arguments, which Python's non-streaming path would drop, so clients may see partial calls. The change relies on every 'ended inside a call' site being converted to the typed error; any missed site silently retains old fallback behavior. Snapshot updates and the modified Responses API test reduce coverage of the previously leaked trailing text.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly separates parser responsibility (reporting typed incompleteness) from output policy (deciding what to show), placing the drop in UnifiedParserState::finish so streaming and non-streaming agree by construction. Tests cover both unified and tool-parser paths with empty and non-empty prefixes, and red-green validation confirms the new match arm is load-bearing. The hf and Beam template parsers are intentionally untouched since their content rules already prevent markup leakage.

## 10. [[KV Offload] Describe complete stores from request context](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63696)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63696
- **Author:** [Change72](https://github.com/Change72)
- **Merged By:** [orozery](https://github.com/orozery)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 20:33:52
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63696/files) (5):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/offloading_connector/test_events.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/offloading_connector/test_scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/offloading/events.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/offloading/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/cpu/manager.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR changes how the offloading connector's events tracker builds BlockStored payloads: instead of relying solely on metadata snapshotted at record_store time, complete chunks are now described from request context retained via a new on_new_request hook and _RequestEventContext state. It also sets removal_expected=True on complete_store events and adjusts residency-bit handling so removals still expand to block hashes from saved metadata. Tests were added for snapshot vs request-context paths and Mamba stores.

**Technical impact**  
The tracker now stores a lightweight request-derived context (block hashes, token ids, LoRA, resumable, transfer params, group configs) on ReqContext, and _metadata_for reconstructs complete-chunk metadata lazily at event emission. This decouples complete-chunk store payloads from the Request object lifetime, while partial tails and removals continue using the existing snapshot path. The scheduler wires on_new_request into its request lifecycle, and the CPU manager now marks complete stores as removal_expected.

**Potential risks**  
The change introduces a dual-path metadata model (request context for complete chunks, snapshots for partial tails/removals) that must stay consistent; the temporary re-insertion of metadata into _pending_event_metadata for removals is a stopgap that could leak or diverge if removal semantics change. The new on_new_request call in the scheduler is not wrapped in the existing try/except, so failures there could affect request setup. Tests note three partial-tail/EAGLE failures (pre-existing) and the PR is still draft pending #59599 alignment, so downstream consumers of removal contracts should be validated.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly separates store description (from request context) from removal expansion (from saved metadata), enabling future chunk-key removals without breaking current consumers. The weakref-based test confirms the tracker no longer holds the Request alive, which is a meaningful memory-safety improvement. The explicit removal_expected flag on complete_store is a small but important contract change that downstream event consumers must honor.

## 11. [[Model] Canary: require `language` and test translation](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65445)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65445
- **Author:** [Alex-Wengg](https://github.com/Alex-Wengg)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 20:29:50
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65445/files) (3):**
  - `docs/serving/online_serving/speech_to_text.md`
  - `tests/entrypoints/speech_to_text/translation/test_translation_validation.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/canary.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Canary model's validate_language now raises a ValueError when `language` is omitted instead of silently defaulting to 'en' with a warning, so transcription requests without a language return HTTP 400 rather than mislabeling non-English audio as English text. The change also removes the now-unused logger, adds a Canary translation test covering en->de success plus 400s for missing language and non-English pairs, and updates the speech_to_text docs to note Canary's language requirement and English-only translation.

**Technical impact**  
This is a behavior change for the nvidia/canary-1b-v2 model: clients that previously omitted `language` on transcription will now receive HTTP 400 and must pass an explicit language. It diverges from Cohere ASR, FunASR, and FireRedASR2 which still default to 'en', so Canary now has stricter validation semantics. The new test exercises the platform default attention backend because ROCM_AITER_FA rejects Canary's cross-attention.

**Potential risks**  
Existing users relying on the implicit 'en' default for Canary transcription will break with 400 errors, which is a backward-incompatible change that may warrant a release note. The new test spins up a separate RemoteOpenAIServer for Canary and adds ~45s runtime, and it depends on the platform default backend rather than the file's ROCm helper, so backend-specific coverage is limited. The error message embeds the full supported language list, which could be verbose.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was that a missing language defaulted to 'en', causing non-English audio to be returned as English text with HTTP 200, effectively an English translation mislabeled as transcription. Raising an error aligns transcription behavior with translation's existing validation from #64215. The test correctly avoids the foscolo it->en clip since canary-1b-v2 leaves most of that poem in Italian even in Transformers, and the WER test is unaffected because it already passes language='en'.

## 12. [[XPU] fix case test_p2p_lifecycle_e2e.py and test_mm_prefix_lm.py](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65416)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65416
- **Author:** [yma11](https://github.com/yma11)
- **Merged By:** [jikunshang](https://github.com/jikunshang)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 20:28:03
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65416/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/tiering/test_p2p_lifecycle_e2e.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/ops/triton_prefill_attention.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR fixes two independent failures in Intel CI. In test_p2p_lifecycle_e2e.py it pins block_size=16 in the shared engine kwargs so the native-LLM producer and VllmRunner consumer use matching block layouts, avoiding the P2P handshake rejecting incompatible offloaded block byte sizes. In triton_prefill_attention.py it pads BLOCK_DMODEL to at least 16 on XPU so tl.dot output tile width satisfies the XPU Triton compiler requirement for tiny head dims (e.g., Gemma3 vision head_dim=8).

**Technical impact**  
The test change only affects test configuration and makes the P2P lifecycle test exercise its intended path rather than failing on a layout mismatch. The kernel change alters the prefill attention tile width on XPU for head dimensions below 16, which is a behavior change in a production code path; existing masks are relied upon to zero-fill padded loads and restrict stores to the real head dimension. Head dims of 16 or greater are unaffected.

**Potential risks**  
The kernel change is gated to XPU but touches a shared attention op, so correctness depends on the existing masks properly handling the padded tile width; any masking gap could produce incorrect attention outputs for small head dims. Performance may shift slightly for small-head-dim XPU workloads due to the wider tile. The test fix hardcodes block_size=16, which could become stale if defaults change, and the PR description mentions asserting the injected disconnect but the shown diff only adds the block_size line, so that claim is not evidenced in the provided changes.

**Key insights**  
The two fixes address distinct root causes: a test-configuration mismatch versus a compiler constraint on tile width. The XPU-specific max(16, ...) guard is a targeted workaround for a Triton backend requirement and is scoped to avoid impacting other platforms or larger head dims. Reviewers should verify the masking logic handles the padded dimension and confirm whether the disconnect assertion described in the PR is actually included, since it is not visible in the diff.

## 13. [[CI] Enroll RL Entrypoints in automatic sharding](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65482)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65482
- **Author:** [Thangnguyenvn98](https://github.com/Thangnguyenvn98)
- **Merged By:** [khluu](https://github.com/khluu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 20:07:54
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65482/files) (1):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/rl.yaml`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Enrolls the RL Entrypoints Buildkite step and its AMD mirror in automatic_shard, and rewrites the second pytest command to set VLLM_WORKER_MULTIPROC_METHOD=spawn inline instead of via a separate export line. The inline form is required because the sharding planner only accepts plain pytest commands after the first one, so a standalone export would break enrollment.

**Technical impact**  
CI configuration change only; no product code is touched. The step and its mirror now become shardable, currently planning as a single shard each, and the per-command environment is preserved: the weight_transfer command still runs without the variable and the second command still runs with it. Timeouts remain per job and are unchanged.

**Potential risks**  
The second command's text changes, so main's per-command timing label for it is new and may be missing until recorded, which could temporarily affect shard planning (the PR notes it would still run as one job, matching the plan). Any hidden consumer of the exported variable later in the step would break, though the PR asserts nothing downstream read it. Sharding behavior depends on ci-infra's split_commands and the enrollment gate accepting the new form.

**Key insights**  
The core subtlety is that the sharding planner's command parsing constrains how environment variables can be set, forcing an inline prefix rather than an export. The change is small in diff size but behaviorally meaningful for CI scheduling, so it is correctly classified as substantial rather than trivial. Validation claims are reasonable but rely on external tooling and a /ci run comparison that reviewers should confirm.

## 14. [[Rust Frontend] Stop binding --data-parallel-rank to VLLM_DP_RANK](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65369)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65369
- **Author:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 20:00:26
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65369/files) (1):**
  - `rust/src/managed-engine/src/data_parallel.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust frontend's `--data-parallel-rank` clap argument previously had `env = "VLLM_DP_RANK"`, so an exported env var silently enabled external DP load balancing. This PR removes the env binding so only the explicit CLI flag selects external LB, matching `vllm serve` behavior, and adds a test asserting the arg has no env binding.

**Technical impact**  
Changes runtime behavior of `vllm-rs serve`: with `VLLM_DP_RANK` set in the environment and `--data-parallel-size 4`, the frontend now selects internal LB instead of external LB. Explicit `--data-parallel-rank` continues to work. The change is localized to one file in the managed-engine crate and aligns Rust frontend semantics with the Python entrypoint.

**Potential risks**  
Deployments that relied on exporting `VLLM_DP_RANK` to trigger external LB under `vllm-rs serve` will silently switch to internal LB and must now pass `--data-parallel-rank`. The new test only inspects clap metadata rather than end-to-end behavior, and process env is shared across tests, so it cannot fully validate env isolation; the PR notes separate-process manual verification.

**Key insights**  
The fix is at the correct layer (clap declaration) since that is where the environment variable entered the argument. The behavior change is intentional and documented, but it is a breaking change for env-based external LB users and should be called out in release notes. Test coverage is reasonable given the crate lacks an env-isolated harness.

## 15. [[Rust Frontend] Accept Python underscore and dotted flag forms under vllm-rs serve](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65370)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65370
- **Author:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 19:59:45
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65370/files) (4):**
  - `rust/Cargo.lock`
  - `rust/src/cmd/src/cli/tests.rs`
  - `rust/src/managed-engine/Cargo.toml`
  - `rust/src/managed-engine/src/cli.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust frontend's argv repartitioner (rust/src/managed-engine/src/cli.rs) now mirrors Python's FlexibleArgumentParser rewrites: underscore long-flag names are normalized to dashes up to the first '.' or '=', and dotted forms of flags that vllm-rs serve declares are merged into a single --<flag> <JSON> argument using Python's value-parsing and recursive merge rules. This fixes the bug where such spellings silently reached only the managed Python engine and left Rust-side defaults in place. New tests in rust/src/cmd/src/cli/tests.rs cover underscore names, dotted merging (nested keys, inline values, human-readable ints, list append, shared-flag forwarding, engine-only passthrough), and the missing-value error.

**Technical impact**  
Adds serde_json as a dependency of the managed-engine crate and introduces merge_dotted_frontend_args, split_dotted_flag, parse_dotted_value, parse_human_readable_int, merge_json, and normalize_python_long_flag_name. Behavior changes only for vllm-rs serve argv; vllm-rs frontend/render and the Python-supervised path (VLLM_USE_RUST_FRONTEND=1 via --args-json) are unaffected. Shared flags still forward to the engine through existing re-serialization, preserving unknown keys.

**Potential risks**  
The Rust reimplementation of Python's human_readable_int and recursive_dict_update must stay in sync with Python; edge cases like int() forms not valid JSON (e.g. 1_000) intentionally diverge and stay strings. A dotted form combined with the full JSON flag becomes a clap 'used multiple times' error rather than Python's last-wins behavior, and a dotted flag without a value errors instead of Python's IndexError traceback. Duplicate dotted keys overwrite silently without Python's warning. The merge runs on every serve invocation, so any misclassification of a flag as frontend-declared could reroute engine-only args.

**Key insights**  
Placing both rewrites in the repartitioner is a sound layering choice since it is the single point converting Python-style argv into clap input and already handles multi-character aliases. Test coverage is strong, including a negative test proving the underscore fix is required and assertions that engine-only dotted flags still reach Python. The documented divergences from Python are acceptable but should be noted for users relying on last-wins semantics or duplicate-key warnings.

## 16. [[Rust Frontend] Coerce replayed tool-call arguments that are not an object](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65371)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65371
- **Author:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 19:59:03
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65371/files) (8):**
  - `rust/src/chat/src/renderer/deepseek_v32/encoding.rs`
  - `rust/src/chat/src/renderer/deepseek_v32/tests.rs`
  - `rust/src/chat/src/renderer/glm5_next/encoding.rs`
  - `rust/src/chat/src/renderer/glm5_next/tests.rs`
  - `rust/src/chat/src/renderer/hf/mod.rs`
  - `rust/src/chat/src/renderer/inkling/mod.rs`
  - `rust/src/chat/src/renderer/inkling/tests.rs`
  - `rust/src/chat/src/renderer/mod.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a shared helper tool_call_arguments_or_empty in the Rust renderer module that decodes replayed assistant tool-call arguments and coerces malformed or non-object JSON to {} with a size-only warning, matching Python's _postprocess_messages. The HF, DeepSeek V3.2, Inkling and glm5_next renderers opt in, while DeepSeek V4/V4.1, Kimi K3 and Harmony keep their reference encodings. This fixes a 500 on /v1/chat/completions (400 on the render route) whenever a conversation history contains a malformed arguments string, such as a call truncated at max_tokens.

**Technical impact**  
HF's to_template_tool_calls no longer returns a Result and now yields Option, and glm5_next coerces arguments when building template messages so its native prompt matches the HF renderer. Behavior changes for non-object JSON like 42, which previously reached HF templates as a number and could break .items() calls. Tests were added or rewritten across HF, DeepSeek V3.2, Inkling and glm5_next, and the full nextest suite (2513 tests) passes.

**Potential risks**  
Coercing to {} discards the original payload, causing a one-time prefix-cache miss from the coerced call onward, though later turns hit since {} renders deterministically. The change is opt-in per renderer, so future renderers may silently miss the coercion. Warning text and counts are not asserted, and the dict passthrough case is untested because the DTO carries arguments as a string. glm5_next encoder behavior for non-object input is pinned by fixtures but depends on the template's own rejection.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is that a malformed history entry is not a request-validation error, so it surfaces as a 500 and poisons every subsequent turn, making coercion the pragmatic recovery path. Centralizing the logic in one helper with explicit per-renderer opt-in respects differing reference encodings (DSML parameters, Kimi raw-text blocks, Harmony verbatim). The glm5_next fix is notable because Python serves it via the HF renderer, so the native renderer had to mirror that coercion point to stay prompt-equivalent.

## 17. [[Rust Frontend] Drop tool calls under tool_choice=none for split tool parsers](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65377)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65377
- **Author:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 19:58:22
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65377/files) (1):**
  - `rust/src/chat/src/output/default/mod.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust frontend's DefaultChatOutputProcessor now builds the configured tool parser even when tool parsing is disabled (tool_choice=none), so that parsed tool calls are dropped via the existing max_tool_calls=Some(0) mechanism instead of leaking raw call markup into content. resolve_tool_parser was refactored to take a ResolvedToolContext and return Option<Box<dyn ToolParser>>, returning None when there are no tools or no parser is configured under none, while preserving existing errors when tool parsing is on. Tests were expanded to cover unified, split, and Llama JSON parsers under auto and none.

**Technical impact**  
Aligns the split reasoning-plus-tool-parser path with unified parsers and Python's parser engines: under tool_choice=none, calls are parsed and suppressed, and content is cleaned of markup. Parser construction in vllm-chat now consistently defers tool_choice handling to structured assembly's max_tool_calls. Output grammar under none with a response_format now uses final_output_only(answer), matching the shape CombinedParser already builds. Behavior for auto, required, and named choices is unchanged.

**Potential risks**  
Behavior diverges from Python for some parsers (legacy tool parsers, HF ResponseTemplateParser, Llama JSON), where raw calls remain content; Rust now drops bare JSON calls for llama3_json/llama4_json, which could surprise users relying on that output. Parsers preserving special tokens (kimi_k2, internlm2, DSML) now decode with skip_special_tokens=false under none, a subtle decoding change. The grammar shape change for response_format under none is asserted equivalent but is a structural shift worth watching. Only one file changed and tests are extensive, but cross-parser edge cases may not all be covered.

**Key insights**  
The fix is small and well-targeted: it reuses the existing max_tool_calls=Some(0) suppression rather than adding new drop logic, keeping layering clean. The PR is transparent about intentional divergence from Python and justifies it via OpenAI semantics for tool_choice=none. Test coverage is strong, including a revert-detection case, and validation ran the full Rust CI suite (2510 tests passing). The main review focus should be the llama JSON bare-call behavior change and the skip_special_tokens decoding shift.

## 18. [[Rust Frontend] Accept FastAPI boolean spellings on dev route query parameters](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65372)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65372
- **Author:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 19:57:41
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65372/files) (5):**
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/cache.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/pause.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/world_size.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/utils.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a deserialize_query_bool helper in vllm-server utils and applies it to the boolean query fields of PauseParams (clear_cache), ResetPrefixCacheParams (reset_running_requests, reset_external), and WorldSizeParams (include_dp). This makes the Rust frontend accept FastAPI/pydantic boolean spellings (true/t/yes/y/on/1 and false/f/no/n/off/0, case-insensitive) so Python clients like requests, which send True/False, no longer get a 400. Tests cover the parsing table and the three routes.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior change limited to query-string parsing on dev/RL routes; JSON bodies and engine calls are untouched. The helper is a small, reusable serde deserializer placed alongside existing utilities, and the change is backward compatible since lowercase true/false still parse. Invalid values still yield 400, matching existing Rust error semantics.

**Potential risks**  
Minor divergence from Python remains: invalid values return 400 vs 422, repeated keys take first vs last, and /pause reports param mode for clear_cache errors. The custom deserializer bypasses serde's default bool handling, so any future non-string deserialization path for these fields would break; also the accepted spelling set is hand-maintained and could drift from pydantic if it changes. No live-server or real-model validation was performed.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a client-library encoding mismatch (requests uses str(True)) rather than a logic bug, and the fix is correctly scoped to untyped query strings while leaving typed JSON bodies strict. The test table was probed against a live FastAPI app, giving good confidence in parity, and route tests assert the actual engine arguments. The PR is well-documented and low-risk, with the main residual gaps being error-code and repeated-key semantics that predate this change.

## 19. [[Rust][Benchmark] Map --output-len to the sharegpt and hf datasets](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64946)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64946
- **Author:** [ZhenchengLin](https://github.com/ZhenchengLin)
- **Merged By:** [esmeetu](https://github.com/esmeetu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 19:55:01
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64946/files) (2):**
  - `rust/src/bench/src/cli.rs`
  - `rust/src/bench/src/config.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust vllm-bench CLI previously ignored --output-len for the sharegpt and hf datasets, deriving output length from dataset reference answers instead. This PR maps --output-len onto sharegpt_output_len and hf_output_len (with --output-len taking precedence over the dataset-specific flags), extends the hf zero-length validation to also reject --output-len 0 for hf, and updates the help text. This aligns Rust behavior with Python vllm bench serve and the other Rust datasets.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change in config resolution: sharegpt_output_len and hf_output_len now use args.output_len.or(dataset_specific_flag). This also affects --multi-turn runs since they read the same config fields. The hf validation condition was rewritten to reject both --hf-output-len 0 and --output-len 0 when dataset is hf, with a combined error message. Two new unit tests cover mapping precedence and zero-length rejection.

**Potential risks**  
Users relying on the previous silent-ignore behavior for --output-len with sharegpt/hf will now see different output lengths, which is the intended fix but could surprise existing scripts. The error message text changed, so any test or tooling matching the old string '--hf-output-len must be at least 1' may break. The precedence rule (--output-len wins) is a deliberate design choice that should be documented consistently; the help text mentions it only implicitly. SPEED-Bench remains unmapped, which is intentional but could be a future inconsistency.

**Key insights**  
The change is small and well-tested, with clear before/after end-to-end evidence and parity with Python. The refactor of the hf zero-check into a single combined condition is cleaner than the original let-chain. The PR correctly scopes out SPEED-Bench and notes the multi-turn side effect. Overall a low-risk, high-value consistency fix, though the error message wording change is a minor compatibility consideration.

## 20. [[Rust Frontend] Return 501 for /generative_scoring and the opt-in Cohere v2 chat routes](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65376)

  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust frontend now returns 501 (shared not_supported handler) instead of 404 for POST /generative_scoring and, under the same gates as Python, for the opt-in Cohere v2 chat routes (/cohere/v2/chat and /cohere/v2/chat/render). A preparatory refactor replaces build_router_with_options' positional booleans and the test-only wrapper functions with a private RouteGates struct resolved once in build_router, adding a cohere_api gate read from VLLM_ENABLE_COHERE_API.

**Technical impact**  
Route registration behavior changes for the new placeholder routes: /generative_scoring is always registered, /cohere/v2/chat only when VLLM_ENABLE_COHERE_API is set to a non-zero integer, and /cohere/v2/chat/render additionally requires --enable-scale-out. The RouteGates refactor consolidates gate plumbing and removes three test-only builder wrappers, with tests updated to construct gates directly; the PR reports full clippy and nextest passes (2511 tests).

**Potential risks**  
The cohere_api_enabled parser accepts any non-zero i64 and silently treats unparsable values as disabled, which may diverge from Python's env handling (e.g. 'true' would be off). Tests bypass the env var by passing gates directly, so the env-parsing path itself is not covered by an isolated test. The 501 uses the shared error shape rather than Python's Cohere error envelope, a deliberate but observable client-facing difference. The refactor touches many test call sites, so any missed gate mapping could silently alter test coverage.

**Key insights**  
The change is a small, well-scoped parity improvement: it turns misleading 404s into explicit 501s for known-but-unimplemented Python routes and makes the Cohere opt-in meaningful on the Rust frontend. The RouteGates struct is a sensible cleanup that reduces positional-boolean mistakes and makes gate combinations explicit in tests. Main review focus should be the env-var parsing semantics and confirming the gate conditions match Python's attach_router logic.

## 21. [[Rust Frontend] Omit unset usage prompt_tokens_details and message annotations like Python](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65374)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65374
- **Author:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 19:54:02
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65374/files) (3):**
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/chat_completions/types.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/utils/types.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds serde skip_serializing_if = Option::is_none to Usage::prompt_tokens_details and ChatCompletionMessage::annotations in the Rust frontend so unset values are omitted rather than serialized as null, matching Python behavior and the OpenAI spec (which types these as optional but not nullable). Tests in usage_tests and routes/tests.rs are updated to assert the keys are absent.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the wire format of Rust frontend responses: prompt_tokens_details is now absent on all routes (chat, completions, generate; streamed and non-streamed) when the details flag is off, and annotations is always absent on non-streaming chat messages. This aligns Rust output with Python and improves compatibility with strict schema validators and the OpenAI SDK. Other unset fields retain explicit null.

**Potential risks**  
Clients that relied on the key being present with a null value (rather than absent) could break, though this matches Python and the spec. The change is field-level on shared Usage type, so it applies uniformly across all routes and stream modes, which is intended but broad. completion_tokens_details is intentionally left inconsistent with Python on some chunk kinds, a known follow-up gap.

**Key insights**  
The fix is minimal and well-scoped: two serde attributes plus test updates, with red-green validation and per-commit clippy/test cleanliness documented. The PR explicitly notes the remaining completion_tokens_details discrepancy and defers it, showing careful scoping. AI assistance was disclosed and human review of all changed lines is claimed.

## 22. [[Bugfix][Rust Frontend] Include error sources in HTTP 400 messages](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65144)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65144
- **Author:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 19:53:22
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65144/files) (3):**
  - `rust/src/server/src/error.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/lib.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust frontend's HTTP 400 validation error paths (text_submit_error and chat_submit_error) previously formatted messages with Display, which truncated the error chain at the non-transparent EngineCoreClient layer, hiding details like connected data-parallel ranks. The PR switches these to as_report() to include the full source chain, matching the 500 branch and gRPC behavior, and also uses as_report() for hyper connection errors in the trace log. A new integration test verifies the unconnected-rank 400 message now reports connected ranks.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change to HTTP 400 error message content for request validation errors: messages now include the full source chain rather than stopping at the first non-transparent wrapper. This improves debuggability and aligns the HTTP path with the gRPC path. The change is scoped to validation-error branches and the trace log; 500 responses and other error formatting sites are unchanged per the audit.

**Potential risks**  
Error message text changes could break clients or tests that assert on exact 400 message strings, though the PR notes existing error::tests still pass. The claim that InvalidDataParallelRank is the only non-transparent-wrapped validation error relies on an audit that could miss future error variants, so new wrapped errors may silently leak or truncate differently. The trace log change is low risk but alters log output format.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between Display (stops at non-transparent layers) and report formatting (walks the source chain); using as_report() consistently is the correct fix. The added test mirrors the existing gRPC test, giving good parity coverage. The PR is well-scoped and validated with nextest, clippy, and fmt, though the audit-based claim about other error sites is the main area to scrutinize.

## 23. [[Spec Decode] Clean up AR speculator decode path](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65528)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65528
- **Author:** [TheEpicDolphin](https://github.com/TheEpicDolphin)
- **Merged By:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 19:49:53
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65528/files) (4):**
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_target_dependent_ar_speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/autoregressive/speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/standalone_ar/speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/target_dependent_ar/speculator.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR renames the AR speculator decode methods to clarify their roles: _decode becomes _run_multi_step_decode, _generate_draft becomes _decode, _fused_multi_step_decode becomes _run_fused_multi_step_decode, and _generate_fused_drafts becomes _fused_multi_step_decode. It also moves the num_reqs = min(num_reqs, dp_sync_state.num_reqs) clamp from the target_dependent_ar propose path into the shared _run_multi_step_decode method, and reorders the specialize_spec_tokens call before _prepare_decode_inputs.

**Technical impact**  
The rename is mostly cosmetic but touches abstract method signatures and call sites across the base speculator, standalone_ar, target_dependent_ar, and tests, so any out-of-tree subclass or caller using the old names will break. Moving the num_reqs clamp into the shared path changes behavior for the standalone_ar speculator, which previously did not apply this clamp, and makes the clamp apply whenever dp_sync_state is present rather than only on dummy_run. The reordering of specialize_spec_tokens relative to _prepare_decode_inputs could subtly affect how batch descriptors are prepared for decode.

**Potential risks**  
The behavioral change from relocating the num_reqs clamp is the main risk: standalone_ar now clamps num_reqs against dp_sync_state.num_reqs, which may alter padding or batch sizing in DP-sync scenarios and could cause mismatches if downstream code assumes the unclamped value. The removal of the dummy_run condition means the clamp now applies on non-dummy runs too, which may change real decode behavior. The reordering of specialize_spec_tokens before _prepare_decode_inputs could affect input preparation if that method depends on the specialized descriptor. Renames also risk missed call sites in other modules not covered by the four changed files.

**Key insights**  
The rename improves readability by distinguishing the orchestration entry point (_run_multi_step_decode) from the actual draft generation (_decode), which is a reasonable cleanup. However, the PR bundles a semantic change (clamp relocation and condition removal) with a rename, making it harder to review and bisect. The test updates only cover the renamed symbols and do not appear to add coverage for the new clamp behavior in standalone_ar, so that behavioral change may be under-tested.

## 24. [[Bugfix][DeepEP] Upgrade to 2.1 and retain per-context GIN completion ordering](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59844)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #59844
- **Author:** [tlrmchlsmth](https://github.com/tlrmchlsmth)
- **Merged By:** [khluu](https://github.com/khluu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 19:36:07
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59844/files) (6):**
  - `docker/Dockerfile`
  - `docker/versions.json`
  - `docs/serving/expert_parallel_deployment.md`
  - `tools/ep_kernels/install_python_libraries.sh`
  - `tools/ep_kernels/patches/deepep-gin-barrier-data-visibility.patch`
  - `vllm/distributed/device_communicators/all2all.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Upgrades the vendored DeepEP pin from d4f41e4e93 to a56d6156 (DeepEP 2.1), which includes the upstream NVLink visibility fence, and replaces the prior custom deferred-flush patch with a smaller vendored patch that signals and waits on every GIN context at data completion while keeping arrival-only barriers on context 0. The installer now applies the patch conditionally with skip/already-applied/failure diagnostics, adds an NCCL library search path for SONAME linking, and passes num_allocated_qps=9 in direct mode to match automatic active-QP selection.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the native DeepEP build inputs, the barrier synchronization semantics in comm.cuh, and the all2all kwargs passed to DeepEP, so behavior of distributed EP communication is affected. The patch is gated to the exact pinned SHA and aborts on failed application, and the Dockerfile now copies the patches directory into the build context. Runtime NCCL version must match the build version for NCCL < 2.31.

**Potential risks**  
The PR explicitly states that distributed correctness, model execution, and overhead are unvalidated on this revision; GPU runs were blocked by infrastructure failures, so regressions in multi-node GDAKI paths could go undetected. The per-context signal loop multiplies barrier traffic by context count, which may add overhead, and the hardcoded nine-context allocation assumes vLLM's direct-mode QP selection stays aligned with DeepEP's automatic selection. The patch is tightly coupled to a single upstream SHA and will silently skip on any other revision.

**Key insights**  
The core fix relies on NCCL's per-context GIN ordering (NVIDIA/nccl#2302), so signaling only context 0 is insufficient for puts on other contexts. Validation is limited to build and offline patch checks; the historical three-node H200 reproducer evidence applies to the old pin and should not be treated as proof for this upgrade. The submitter flags that pre-merge GPU correctness and timing reruns are still required.

## 25. [[CI][Model] Fill the model info cache once in the model init and tensor schema tests](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65441)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65441
- **Author:** [aarushjain29](https://github.com/aarushjain29)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 18:57:06
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65441/files) (4):**
  - `tests/models/multimodal/processing/test_tensor_schema.py`
  - `tests/models/test_initialization.py`
  - `tests/models/test_registry.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/registry.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds ModelRegistry.fill_model_info_cache(), which inspects all lazily registered models missing from the model info cache in a single subprocess, and calls it once per module via autouse module-scoped fixtures in test_initialization.py and test_tensor_schema.py. The goal is to eliminate the ~10s per-case cache miss that these tests currently pay, cutting CI time substantially. A new unit test in test_registry.py verifies the cache is populated and used.

**Technical impact**  
Refactors inspect_model_cls by extracting the module hash lookup into _get_module_hash(), with no behavior change for existing callers. The new batch fill runs entirely in a subprocess, including cache lookups, to avoid importing model packages or initializing CUDA in the caller, which preserves fork-based NVIDIA test behavior. Cache entries are keyed identically to the existing per-model path, and failures are best-effort so individual model errors still surface in their own test cases.

**Potential risks**  
The batch fill adds a fixed ~17s cost per module per job, which is a net loss for jobs that run only one case from a module (noted for an Intel job). The subprocess-based cache lookup means a warm cache still incurs a subprocess startup (~10s). Validation was n=1 on a shared MI300X with no variability measurement, so the claimed savings are indicative rather than statistically robust. Best-effort skipping of failing models (3 of 418 on ROCm) is acceptable but means those models still pay the miss.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped and reuses existing cache save/load helpers, with a thoughtful design decision to move the cache lookup into the subprocess to avoid CUDA initialization in the caller. The new unit test meaningfully asserts cache usage by failing if _run_in_subprocess is invoked after fill. The main tradeoff is a fixed per-module cost that only pays off when a module runs multiple cases, which is worth monitoring in CI timing.

## 27. [[Bugfix][Spec Decode] Align speculator hidden norm in GLM-4.5/4.6 MoE and GLM-OCR MTP with GLM5Next to recycle post-final-hidden.](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62234)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #62234
- **Author:** [spandantiwari](https://github.com/spandantiwari)
- **Merged By:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 18:45:51
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62234/files) (9):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/spec_decode.yaml`
  - `tests/v1/e2e/spec_decode/mtp/glm/__init__.py`
  - `tests/v1/e2e/spec_decode/mtp/glm/test_mtp.py`
  - `tests/v1/spec_decode/test_llm_base_proposer_sampling.py`
  - `tests/v1/spec_decode/test_mtp.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/glm4_moe_lite_mtp.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/glm4_moe_mtp.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/glm_ocr_mtp.py`
  - `vllm/v1/spec_decode/llm_base_proposer.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Three GLM-family MTP drafters (glm4_moe_mtp, glm4_moe_lite_mtp, glm_ocr_mtp) previously returned the pre-final-norm hidden, so the speculator recycled a hidden missing one RMSNorm into the next draft step's hnorm. This PR applies the final norm once via the fused shared_head.norm(hidden, residual), returns the post-final-norm hidden as a tuple, drops the redundant norm in compute_logits, and registers the three architectures in SpecDecodeBaseProposer.model_returns_tuple() so Model Runner V1 can unpack the tuple. It aligns these models with the established #47448 / glm5next recycle contract.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the drafter's recycled hidden state for draft steps >= 1 (num_speculative_tokens >= 2), affecting both the single-module AR path and the multi-module path; k=1 is unaffected. Logits are unchanged since compute_logits now consumes the already-normed hidden. The tuple return requires the V1 registration to avoid a crash, and V2 unpacks tuples generically. Adds a real-object unit test, an e2e test on both model runners, and CI wiring for the new GLM e2e directory.

**Potential risks**  
The change alters drafter state, so any downstream consumer assuming a single-tensor return or pre-norm hidden could break; the V1 registration is load-bearing and any unregistered GLM MTP variant would crash on the first draft step. The e2e test adds ~2.5 min and a 2.7 GB checkpoint download to CI, and its acceptance thresholds are hardware-tuned (MI355X), which could be flaky on other GPUs. Acceptance impact is claimed statistically insignificant but was measured out-of-CI on a single checkpoint.

**Key insights**  
The fix is minimal and mirrors an already-merged pattern, with clear root-cause analysis and strong validation (unit test fails on base, passes here; e2e covers both model runners). The paired acceptance measurement showing no significant change supports the lossless claim. Main review focus should be confirming all GLM MTP architectures are registered and that the e2e thresholds are robust across CI hardware.

## 28. [[Rust][Benchmark] Record ramp-up runs in the result JSON and filename](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64308)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64308
- **Author:** [ZhenchengLin](https://github.com/ZhenchengLin)
- **Merged By:** [esmeetu](https://github.com/esmeetu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 18:33:46
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64308/files) (5):**
  - `rust/src/bench/src/benchmark.rs`
  - `rust/src/bench/src/cli.rs`
  - `rust/src/bench/src/config.rs`
  - `rust/src/bench/src/output/json.rs`
  - `rust/src/bench/src/rate_control.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust vllm-bench previously saved ramp-up runs as if they were fixed-rate (infqps filename, no ramp-up metadata). This PR adds ramp-up fields (ramp_up_strategy, ramp_up_start_rps, ramp_up_end_rps) and rps_change_events to the result JSON, encodes the ramp-up strategy and start/end RPS in the result filename, and adds validation rejecting --request-rate/--sweep-request-rate with ramp-up and a start RPS above the end RPS, matching Python vllm bench serve behavior.

**Technical impact**  
Adds RampUpStrategy::as_str, a RequestSchedule::rps_change_offsets helper that derives integer-rate change events from the precomputed per-request rates/delays, and a benchmark_start_local wall-clock anchor to timestamp events. build_result_json now conditionally inserts ramp-up fields, and compute_result_filename branches on config.ramp_up before the infqps case. New unit tests cover filename, validation, JSON insertion, and event offsets.

**Potential risks**  
rps_change_offsets casts f64 rates to u64, so fractional or sub-1.0 start rates and non-integer step behavior may diverge from Python's int-based parsing; the f64 vs int JSON representation (2.0 vs 2) is a known, documented difference. Timestamp computation uses (delay * 1e6) as i64, which could truncate or overflow for very large delays. The new validation is a behavior change that could break existing scripts passing --request-rate alongside ramp-up.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped and closely mirrors Python semantics, with clear tests and an honest note on the remaining f64/int discrepancy. The main review focus should be edge cases in rps_change_offsets (start_rps below 1, non-integer rates, exponential strategies) and confirming the new validation errors are surfaced clearly to users.

## 29. [[Bugfix][Rust Frontend] Reject --dataset-path with synthetic vllm-bench datasets](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64551)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64551
- **Author:** [MayaJShetty](https://github.com/MayaJShetty)
- **Merged By:** [esmeetu](https://github.com/esmeetu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 18:33:27
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64551/files) (3):**
  - `rust/src/bench/README.md`
  - `rust/src/bench/src/cli.rs`
  - `rust/src/bench/src/config.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust vllm-bench frontend silently ignored --dataset-path when used with synthetic datasets (random, random-mm, random-rerank, prefix_repetition), causing users who forgot --dataset-name to benchmark random prompts instead of their file. This PR adds a validation check in BenchConfig::from_args that rejects this combination with the same error message as Python vllm bench serve, and updates the CLI help text and README to document the restriction.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change: previously accepted invocations (synthetic dataset plus --dataset-path, including the default random dataset) now fail fast at startup with a clear config error. Because the check lives in BenchConfig::from_args, it covers all serve modes (single run, multi-turn, sweeps, --num-runs). File-based datasets (sharegpt, speed-bench, hf, custom, timed_trace) are unaffected, and parity with Python's error message is preserved.

**Potential risks**  
This is a breaking change for any existing scripts or users that passed --dataset-path alongside a synthetic dataset and relied on it being ignored; they will now get a hard error. The error message uses clap's to_possible_value with an expect, which is safe given no skipped variants but is a minor panic surface if DatasetName changes. Test coverage is solid but limited to config-level unit tests plus manual end-to-end validation.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was a missing validation present in Python's serve.py that the Rust port never replicated, combined with a default of random for --dataset-name that made the failure silent and plausible-looking. Placing the check centrally in from_args is the right design choice for covering all execution modes with one guard. The PR is well-validated with regression tests that fail without the fix and a clear before/after end-to-end table.

  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/cutedsl/moe/routing.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/cutedsl/moe/tail.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/cutedsl/oproj/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/cutedsl/oproj/all_reduce_dual_norm.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/cutedsl/oproj/batt.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/cutedsl/oproj/draft_all_reduce_boundary.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/runtime/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/runtime/cute_ops.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/runtime/decode.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/runtime/draft.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/runtime/expert_tp.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/runtime/flashinfer_ops.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/runtime/markov.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/runtime/providers.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds an opt-in (decode_backend=latency_specialized) specialized single-stream decode path for Muse Spark Open on GB300, claiming ~1.5x decode throughput. It introduces a large new package under vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/specialized_decode/ (CuTe DSL and CUDA C kernels for attention, MoE, boundaries, DSpark draft and Markov sampling), a MuseSparkModelState, model hooks in model.py/dspark.py, and three new CUDA C ops with their registration. The stock decode remains the default (auto).

**Technical impact**  
Behavior is gated behind additional_config and a long list of unsupported() checks (TP 4/8/16, EP=TP, DSpark with 7 draft tokens, FULL CUDA graphs, FlashInfer attention/all-reduce backends, SM100 family, BF16 KV cache, etc.), so default paths are unchanged. When enabled it replaces the decode forward with a custom executor, adds a column-parallel draft fc and vocab-parallel Markov head, and disables weight reloading. The change is very large (~19.5k lines, 42 files) and touches shared model code, so review and maintenance burden is high.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness relies on bitwise-matching many stock kernels (norms, all-reduce sum orders, routing, Gumbel sampling) and on collective symmetric-memory mailboxes with bounded spins; divergence or missing NVLink multicast can trap or hang. Several paths are only approximately equal (GEMM summation order, NaN argmax, aux state last bits). Unit/kernel tests are excluded from the PR, so CI coverage is absent, and the code depends on FlashInfer internals below the public op plus pinned tactics, which may break on upgrades. Unsupported-config detection must stay in sync with the kernels.

**Key insights**  
This is a highly specialized, hardware- and config-specific optimization that is carefully opt-in and guarded, with strong validation evidence (gsm8k parity, OCRBench/AIME accuracy, TP4/8/16 greedy determinism). The main review concerns are the sheer size and complexity, the reliance on private FlashInfer APIs and exact numerical reproduction, and the absence of committed tests; the checklist items for design fit and testing are unchecked.
