### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new opt-in preset b200_agentx_agg to the Kimi-K3 recipe YAML, documenting the InferenceX AgentX lane (pinned to 9fe1038). It defines a two-node TP8 x PP2 + DCP8 aggregated engine with the Inferact DSpark draft, an embedded RDMA Mooncake DRAM store, a pinned image, shared args/env, and eight per-concurrency points with their own max-num-seqs, CUDA graph sizes, attention backends, and Mooncake on/off toggles.

**Technical impact**  
This is a config/data-only change to a recipe file, but it materially expands the recipe's preset surface: new selectors (nodes 2, python frontend, kv_store_distributed_mooncake), remove_args, json_args for attention/compilation/kv-transfer configs, and per-point overrides. It will be consumed by the recipe build/validation tooling and rendered into vllm_command output, so downstream schema, preset, and guide-html tests and the generated public JSON are affected.

**Potential risks**  
The PR's Test Plan is marked TODO with no results, so the build, validate-source, schema, presets, and guide-html checks and the 8-point vllm_command comparison against the lane are unverified. Per-point divergence (K7 vs K4, backend switch at c10, Mooncake on/off, PP layer partition at c4, gpu-memory-utilization 0.915 at c96) is error-prone and could silently drift from the pinned lane. The diff is truncated, so the full runbook and any remaining points cannot be fully reviewed.

**Key insights**  
The preset is deliberately opt-in because it carries benchmark-only settings, and the runbook documents what was intentionally excluded and why (usage reporting, logging, cluster-specific fabric/peermem, env vars not read by the pinned image). The recipe lacks a DCP selector, so DCP8 flags are inlined, and the Blackwell baseline's --disable-flashinfer-autotune is swapped for the lane's --no-enable-flashinfer-autotune. Merging should wait on the TODO test results and a full diff review of the truncated runbook.

## 32. [[Bugfix][Frontend] Materialize DeepSeek V4.1 content iterators before deepcopy](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62647)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #62647
- **Author:** [ltllt1](https://github.com/ltllt1)
- **Merged By:** [chaunceyjiang](https://github.com/chaunceyjiang)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 18:21:49
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62647/files) (2):**
  - `tests/tokenizers_/test_deepseek_v41.py`
  - `vllm/tokenizers/deepseek_v41.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a bug where DeepSeek V4.1 chat requests with message content as an empty array (or other iterator-form content) failed before generation with a TypeError about pickling a pydantic_core ValidatorIterator. The tokenizer's _normalize_messages now materializes iterator content into a list before copy.deepcopy, and regression tests cover empty content in streaming and non-streaming modes, text content parts, repeated rendering, and JSON serialization.

**Technical impact**  
The change is localized to vllm/tokenizers/deepseek_v41.py and only alters behavior when content is an Iterator instance; string and list content follow the prior path. Materializing and storing the list back on the original message prevents one-shot iterator consumption and allows repeated rendering and model_dump_json. Tests add coverage for the previously failing request shapes.

**Potential risks**  
The fix mutates the input message dicts in place by replacing content, which could affect callers relying on the original iterator object, though this is likely intended. The isinstance(content, Iterator) check may not catch all non-picklable iterable types (e.g., generators are Iterators, but other lazy containers may not be), leaving edge cases. Test coverage is limited to the DeepSeek V4.1 tokenizer and does not verify broader request validation or tool_calls interactions.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is Pydantic leaving empty-array content as a ValidatorIterator that cannot be deep-copied, and the fix correctly materializes it before the deep copy. Storing the materialized list back on the message is important to avoid consuming a one-shot iterator on subsequent renders. The PR is well-scoped with targeted regression tests, though the iterator-type check could be broadened for robustness.

## 33. [[ROCm][HY4] Use breakable graphs without torch.compile](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64763)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64763
- **Author:** [zixi-qi](https://github.com/zixi-qi)
- **Merged By:** [ywang96](https://github.com/ywang96)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 18:12:57
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64763/files) (6):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/models_basic.yaml`
  - `docs/models/hy4.md`
  - `docs/models/supported_models.md`
  - `tests/models/test_hyv4_rocm.py`
  - `vllm/config/vllm.py`
  - `vllm/models/hy_v4/amd/model.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Removes @support_torch_compile from the HY4 AMD backbone and adds HYV4ForCausalLM/HYV4MTPModel to the default breakable-cudagraph architecture set on ROCm, so HY4 runs with breakable HIP graphs and no torch.compile. Docs and the ROCm regression test are updated to assert the new default (compile mode NONE, FULL_AND_PIECEWISE graphs, captured graphs > 0) and to exercise generation.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes for HY4 on ROCm: the backbone no longer enters torch.compile, but graph capture is retained for prefill and decode, allowing decode-specific MXFP8 GEMM tile selection at capture time. The PR reports ~18.7% higher throughput and ~15.6% lower median TPOT on C8 decode, with a small TTFT regression (~5.5%) on mixed workloads. The change is scoped to ROCm via the architecture allowlist in vllm/config/vllm.py.

**Potential risks**  
Accuracy parity is not fully established: only GPQA-Diamond was compared (+0.51 pp, within noise) and the PR itself notes other discrepancies remain under investigation. The default-on architecture allowlist affects all HY4 ROCm users, and the mixed-workload TTFT regression could matter for latency-sensitive prefill. Kernel-sum improvements are not wall-clock latency, so real-world gains may vary by workload.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is that torch.compile freezes prefill-sized MXFP8 GEMM tiles into small-batch decode, and removing it lets graph capture pick decode-appropriate tiles, explaining most of the kernel-time reduction. The test change is well-targeted: it asserts the new default and fails on the unmodified parent, guarding the regression. Broader accuracy qualification should be completed before treating this as a safe default flip.

## 34. [[Bugfix][Quantization] Fix online MoE weight conversion `KeyError: 'weight_scale'` with Humming](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56606)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56606
- **Author:** [waizuichougou](https://github.com/waizuichougou)
- **Merged By:** [mgoin](https://github.com/mgoin)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 18:08:37
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56606/files) (6):**
  - `tests/quantization/test_fp8.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_online.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/oracle/fp8.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/oracle/mxfp4.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/utils/humming/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/utils/humming/moe.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a deterministic KeyError: 'weight_scale' when generic online FP8/MXFP8/MXFP4 MoE quantization selects the Humming backend. Humming's converter reads tensors from layer.state_dict(), but online quantization had not yet registered the generated quantized weights and scales on the layer. A new helper stage_moe_weights_for_humming installs the canonical weights/scales (and optional input scales) before conversion, and unused MXFP8 full-size FP8 allocations were removed.

**Technical impact**  
Centralizes the previously inline staging logic from oracle/fp8.py into a shared humming utility and applies it to the MXFP4 path as well, so all generic online MoE formats (FP8, MXFP8, MXFP4) now present consistent tensors to Humming's layer-based converter. Removing the discarded MXFP8 allocations avoids roughly 576 MiB of transient device memory per layer for Qwen3-30B-A3B dimensions. Other MoE backends are unaffected.

**Potential risks**  
The staging mutates layer parameters via replace_parameter before conversion, so correctness depends on the subsequent Humming conversion and post-conversion replacement behaving as before; any failure mid-conversion could leave the layer in a partially staged state. The scale-name selection still relies on hasattr(layer, 'w13_weight_scale_inv'), which is a heuristic that could mis-select if both or neither scale attributes exist. New tests are CUDA-only, so non-CUDA CI provides no coverage of this path.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is an interface mismatch: Humming consumes layer state rather than converter arguments, so online quantization must stage tensors first. The refactor improves consistency by sharing one staging helper across FP8 and MXFP4 paths. Tests validate both the staging behavior and end-to-end forward equivalence against a fresh Humming layer, including requantization, which is strong regression coverage for a subtle conversion-boundary bug.

## 35. [[Perf][ToolParser] Scan Kimi K3 streaming tool calls incrementally](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62203)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #62203
- **Author:** [waizuichougou](https://github.com/waizuichougou)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 18:06:14
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62203/files) (2):**
  - `tests/tool_use/test_kimi_k3_tool_parser.py`
  - `vllm/tool_parsers/kimi_k3_tool_parser.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The KimiK3ToolParser streaming path previously rescanned the entire accumulated tools section and redecoded all completed calls on every delta, causing quadratic CPU cost for long tool arguments. This PR adds two parser offsets (_stream_call_close_idx and _stream_call_parse_idx) plus a call-close regex so parsing resumes from the last unfinished call boundary and each completed call is decoded exactly once. Tests add boundary/chunk-size parametrized streaming equivalence checks and a decode-once regression test.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior-preserving optimization localized to KimiK3ToolParser: the existing call regex and decoder still recognize and decode calls, while a new close-marker regex with partial=True handles close markers split across deltas. Emitted tool-call indices, names, argument bytes, and timing are claimed unchanged, and the reported CPU parser speedups scale from 1.57x to over 9x for large arguments. The change adds only two integer offsets and one compiled regex, no new scanner class or buffer.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness hinges on the close-marker regex and partial-match handling aligning exactly with the call regex boundaries, especially for whitespace variants, invalid openers, and close markers embedded in attributes; the differential replay and boundary tests mitigate but do not fully prove equivalence. Stateful offsets assume monotonic current_text growth and a single tools channel; reuse of a parser instance across requests or non-monotonic text could desynchronize offsets. Benchmarks are CPU-only and do not establish end-to-end serving gains.

**Key insights**  
The core win is eliminating redundant decoding of already-closed calls rather than changing the scanner, keeping the diff small and reviewable. The decode-once test with a monkeypatched _decode_call is a strong, targeted regression guard, and the chunk-size parametrization over edge cases (spaced close, invalid opener, close-in-attributes, missing name) covers the trickiest incremental-parsing boundaries. The PR is well-scoped and clearly separates itself from related PRs #60882 and #50627.

## 36. [[Rust][Benchmark] Keep transport failures visible in measured requests](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65284)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65284
- **Author:** [reidliu41](https://github.com/reidliu41)
- **Merged By:** [esmeetu](https://github.com/esmeetu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 18:05:38
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65284/files) (2):**
  - `rust/src/bench/README.md`
  - `rust/src/bench/src/benchmark.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust benchmark's measured request path previously retried connection reset/refused/timeout failures up to three times with backoff, which could mask overload and distort latency, throughput, and failure counts. This PR removes retries from the measured workload path so each scheduled sample performs exactly one HTTP attempt and reports its result, while leaving preparation-stage retries (endpoint readiness, tokenizer) intact. It also deletes the now-unused is_connection_error helper, adds a regression test, and updates the README.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change in the benchmark client: measured requests now match Python's one-attempt semantics, making failure counts, per-request latency, and throughput consistent with the configured workload and concurrency. The concurrency permit is no longer held through retry backoff, and start_time is captured once per request. The new test spins up a local TCP server that resets the first connection and asserts only one workload request is sent and reported as failed.

**Potential risks**  
Removing retries may surface more transient failures in benchmark results, which is intentional but could surprise users who relied on retry smoothing; this is mitigated by README documentation. The regression test relies on set_linger(Some(ZERO)) to force a deterministic reset, which is deprecated and platform-dependent, so it may be flaky on some OSes. The test also depends on tokenizer/dataset test support and a real local socket, adding some CI fragility.

**Key insights**  
The change correctly distinguishes benchmark semantics (one attempt per sample to preserve workload fidelity) from application-client semantics (retry for completion). Aligning with the Python benchmark improves cross-client comparability. The test is well-targeted, verifying both request count and failure reporting, though the connection-reset mechanism is the main reliability concern.

## 37. [[ROCm][Perf][MiniMax-M3.1] Four-wave decode top-k for the NVFP4 indexer](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65087)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65087
- **Author:** [chao-xu-spec](https://github.com/chao-xu-spec)
- **Merged By:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 18:04:25
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65087/files) (3):**
  - `tests/kernels/attention/test_minimax_m3_index_topk_decode.py`
  - `vllm/models/minimax_m3/amd/ops/index_topk_decode/host.py`
  - `vllm/models/minimax_m3/amd/ops/index_topk_decode/prefill_kernel.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR rewrites the decode path of the MiniMax-M3.1 NVFP4 indexer top-k kernel so each decode row is processed by four waves (16 strips each, 1,024-block windows) instead of one wave of 26 strips, then merges the four per-wave top-16 lists with an exact rank over (score, block id). It also widens the lane-bound threshold from 12 to 16 bits with a 32-bit fallback when too many keys pass, and updates the host to compile the decode kernel with 16 strips (kernel renamed _w26 to _w16). The goal is to remove the serialization and candidate-overflow slow path that the old single-wave kernel hits on real index scores, giving roughly 4x faster decode top-k and modest end-to-end throughput/TPOT gains.

**Technical impact**  
Decode top-k now uses 256 threads per workgroup (4 waves x 64 lanes) with per-wave shared-memory candidate and selection buffers, software-pipelined window loads, and a final exact merge that preserves AITER's ordering including ties, NaN and infinities. Rows of at most one window skip the merge, and prefill compiles to the same code as before. The change is confined to the decode top-k kernel and its host dispatch; no other kernels, flags or call sites change, and outputs are claimed bit-identical to the old kernel and AITER across large sweeps.

**Potential risks**  
The PR itself documents a regression: when every row is under about 213k tokens and no row overflows the old kernel's candidate buffer, a call is up to 12% slower (about 0.5 us on a 4 us call), and the host cannot choose between kernels because it only sees the graph's max_seq_len (1M in serving). One of 2,816 replayed decode rows differs, in a call with duplicate page ids where the old kernel's own output is nondeterministic, so exactness is not strictly bit-for-bit in all edge cases. Validation is gfx950-only, the microbench is not in the repo benchmarks, no Buildkite step runs the test file on gfx950, and E2E was run on a published image with overlaid files rather than a build of current main, so the measured gains may not transfer exactly.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is that the old kernel's slowness on real scores comes from a narrow score distribution causing more than 64 keys per window to pass the 12-bit bound, triggering candidate rebuilds and an exact bit search on a single wave; four waves plus a 16-bit bound with 32-bit fallback largely eliminates that path. The merge is designed to be order-exact, and the added tests cover the wave-meeting lengths and a 100-request shape. The main open trade-off is the small regression on short, non-overflowing calls, which the author notes could be mitigated by a 2-way merge for rows up to 2,048 blocks but left out of this PR.

---

## 38. [[Bugfix][Rust Frontend] Fail token-less streams in vllm-bench openai-chat](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59736)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #59736
- **Author:** [ZhenchengLin](https://github.com/ZhenchengLin)
- **Merged By:** [esmeetu](https://github.com/esmeetu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 18:02:55
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/59736/files) (3):**
  - `rust/src/bench/src/backends/mod.rs`
  - `rust/src/bench/src/backends/openai_chat.rs`
  - `rust/src/bench/src/backends/openai_completions.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Rust vllm-bench openai-chat backend previously marked any cleanly-ended 200 stream as successful even when no token was ever received, unlike the completions backend and Python bench serve. This PR gates success on the existing first_token_received flag, failing token-less streams with a shared NO_VALID_CHUNK_ERROR const that the completions backend now also uses to prevent drift. Two new unit tests cover a token-less SSE stream and a role-chunk-only stream.

**Technical impact**  
Aligns chat backend success semantics with completions and Python, fixing inflated output token counts (the 1 + output.itl.len() fallback), skewed Mean TTFT/E2EL, and false goodput SLO passes for empty responses. The shared const centralizes the error message across streaming backends. Multi-turn runs will now treat token-less turns as failed rather than appending empty assistant messages, matching existing behavior elsewhere.

**Potential risks**  
Behavior change for multi-turn benchmarks: previously token-less turns continued the run with empty assistant history; now they are reported as failed, which could alter multi-turn result counts and flow. Any downstream consumers relying on the old lenient success semantics for chat streams will see different success/failure tallies. The change is well-tested at unit level and validated end-to-end against a mock server, but real-server edge cases (e.g., streams with only usage chunks) depend on the prior role-chunk fix in #64626.

**Key insights**  
The fix is small but semantically meaningful: it corrects a metrics-correctness bug where phantom successes silently corrupted TTFT, throughput, and goodput. Reusing a shared error const is a good maintainability choice. The PR description is thorough, includes before/after e2e comparison, and explicitly flags the multi-turn behavior change, which is the main thing reviewers should scrutinize.

## 39. [[Step5] Allow MegaMoE with pipeline parallelism](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65504)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65504
- **Author:** [xsxszab](https://github.com/xsxszab)
- **Merged By:** [jeejeelee](https://github.com/jeejeelee)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 17:57:50
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65504/files) (2):**
  - `vllm/models/step5/nvidia/mega_moe.py`
  - `vllm/models/step5/nvidia/model.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Removes the startup guard rejecting MegaMoE with TP>1 and PP>1 in mega_moe.py, and drops the pipeline_parallel_size == 1 condition from the sequence-parallel gate in model.py. The rationale is that Step5's forward already all-gathers token shards before returning IntermediateTensors from a non-last PP stage and re-shards them on entry, so the PP transport sees full TP-identical tensors and the gate was unnecessary.

**Technical impact**  
Enables the deep_gemm_mega_moe backend under pipeline parallelism, activating the existing SP all-gather/shard path at PP stage boundaries (two TP all-gathers per boundary per step). PP=1 behavior is unchanged since the removed condition was already satisfied there. Validation reports matching GSM8K accuracy and ~13.5% decode/mixed throughput gains at PP2xTP2, with a small prefill regression and a decode regression at PP2xTP4.

**Potential risks**  
The change relies on the correctness of the pre-existing SP boundary gather/shard logic that previously never executed with PP; any edge case (e.g., variable token counts, partial shards, non-identical TP tensors) could produce silent numerical errors rather than a startup failure. The path cannot be covered by CI (SM100 MegaMoE plus ~650GB checkpoint exceeds available CI hardware), so regressions would only surface in manual validation. Throughput trade-offs are workload-dependent, and the PP2xTP4 decode regression is left to user discretion.

**Key insights**  
This is a small diff with outsized behavioral impact: it unlocks a previously blocked configuration by trusting existing code paths. The main review concern is the lack of automated test coverage and dependence on manual benchmarks; the accuracy parity and A/B throughput data are the primary evidence of safety. Consider documenting the PP+SP interaction and the hardware/CI constraints more prominently.

  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/single_type_kv_cache_manager.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/detokenizer.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/output_processor.py`
  - `vllm/v1/request.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR reduces CPython cyclic-GC cost in vLLM by untracking per-request containers that grow with context length (block id lists, block hashes, prompt/output token id lists, detokenizer buffers, API-server token accumulators) via a new gc_untrack/untracked_list helper in gc_utils. It also reworks BlockHashToBlockMap to store block ids instead of KVCacheBlock objects (with a side dict for duplicate ids), makes KVCacheBlock.pool a weakref, clears the prefix-cache map in place on reset, and sends a new request's prompt to MRV2 workers once by sharing the prompt_token_ids object through a new Request.prefill_token_ids property.

**Technical impact**  
Benchmarks claim full GC pass time drops from 258/459 ms to 0.1 ms with negligible admission/chunked-prefill overhead, and no change to scheduling decisions or block ids. The change relies on ctypes PyObject_GC_UnTrack and on the invariant that untracked containers can never participate in reference cycles; the weakref pool field and id-based cache are needed to keep that invariant. Behavior is otherwise intended to be identical, with tests added for untracking, pickle round trips, prefix-cache reset, and prompt sharing.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness depends on a subtle invariant: any future reference cycle through an untracked list or dict will leak memory silently, and any code that replaces an untracked list with a fresh one loses the benefit without failing. The ctypes-based untracking is CPython-specific, untested on free-threaded builds, and makes objects invisible to gc.get_objects()/VLLM_GC_DEBUG. The API server untracks caller-owned prompt lists, and the weakref pool change plus id-based BlockHashToBlockMap alter internal data structures that other code may depend on. The benchmark numbers cover only EngineCore lists and the prefix-cache map, not the later worker/API-server/reset changes.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is well identified: per-request lists created after startup gc.freeze() are still GC-tracked and dominate full-collection cost. The design is careful about cycle safety, Python-version differences in dict untracking (3.11-3.13 vs 3.14), and in-place mutation assumptions, and it is validated with a real Scheduler benchmark plus a broad test suite. The main review focus should be on the weakref pool refactor, the new BlockHashToBlockMap semantics (pop/contain/dup handling), and any code paths that reassign rather than mutate the untracked lists.

## 41. [[CI][Test] Skip multi-node-only pipeline-parallel cases at collection](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65443)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65443
- **Author:** [aarushjain29](https://github.com/aarushjain29)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 17:47:31
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65443/files) (1):**
  - `tests/distributed/test_pipeline_parallel.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
In tests/distributed/test_pipeline_parallel.py, PPTestSettings.iter_params now yields pytest.param objects carrying a skipif mark (multi_node_only and not VLLM_MULTI_NODE) instead of bare tuples. This moves the multi-node-only skip from inside the per-test fresh process to collection time, avoiding interpreter startup cost for cases that would only skip on single-node CI.

**Technical impact**  
Test collection behavior changes: multi-node-only cases are now deselected/skipped at collection rather than executed and skipped at runtime. The PR claims test IDs, the -k ray selection, and VLLM_MULTI_NODE=1 multi-node runs are unchanged, and the existing runtime check in _compare_tp remains as a fallback. Only test infrastructure is affected; no production code or model behavior changes.

**Potential risks**  
If the skipif mark is applied incorrectly or VLLM_MULTI_NODE evaluation differs at collection vs runtime, multi-node-only cases could be silently skipped on multi-node runs, reducing coverage. The PR notes NVIDIA uses fork rather than spawn so savings are smaller there, and validation was n=1 on MI300X only. The claim that test IDs and -k ray selection are unchanged relies on pytest.param preserving IDs, which should be verified but appears validated via --collect-only.

**Key insights**  
This is a targeted CI performance optimization for test collection, saving roughly 2 minutes per MI250 Pipeline + Context Parallelism build and about 1 minute per RayExecutorV2 run. The change is low-risk since it only affects skip timing and keeps the runtime guard, but reviewers should confirm multi-node coverage is preserved and that the skipif condition is evaluated consistently with the existing in-test check.

## 42. [[Kernel] Accumulate ranks in absolute order in 2-stage custom all-reduce](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57042)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57042
- **Author:** [mosafariuk](https://github.com/mosafariuk)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 17:44:04
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57042/files) (4):**
  - `csrc/custom_all_reduce.cuh`
  - `docs/features/batch_invariance.md`
  - `tests/distributed/test_custom_all_reduce.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/determinism/batch_invariant.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The 2-stage custom all-reduce kernel now reads rank pointers in absolute order (ptrs[i] = _dp->ptrs[i]) instead of the rotated (rank + i) order, while tmps keeps the rotation for stage-2 scratch/gather. This makes 1-stage and 2-stage kernels produce bit-identical results and removes 2-stage output's dependence on tensor size, so the batch-invariance force-1-stage env override is dropped and docs updated. A new 4-rank differential test forces each kernel and asserts bitwise equality on adversarial inputs.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the float32 accumulation order in the 2-stage kernel for all runs, not just batch-invariant mode, so 2-stage output bit patterns shift (fp32 notably, ~30% of elements; fp16/bf16 mostly unchanged for ordinary data). Kernel selection by message size becomes irrelevant to the bits, and the batch-invariance path no longer needs to pin 1-stage. Only ptrs is de-rotated; tmps must stay rotated to keep stage-1 writes in each rank's own scratch buffer.

**Potential risks**  
The test has not been run on NVIDIA hardware (A100 CI job retired; AMD mirror is optional), so the CUDA path is validated only by CPU emulation and an AMD in-vitro run. Bit-level output changes could affect downstream reproducibility expectations or any consumer relying on prior 2-stage bits. The change is a single line with subtle correctness constraints (ptrs vs tmps rotation), so a future refactor could accidentally de-rotate tmps and corrupt scratch buffers.

**Key insights**  
The fix is minimal and well-reasoned: matching accumulation order across kernels makes kernel selection bit-neutral, and the test's adversarial cancellation inputs are necessary because plain random fp16/bf16 data cannot detect order changes. The PR is complementary to #50505 and overlaps with #52231, so whichever lands first should reconcile the force-1-stage gate. Main residual gap is lack of NVIDIA CI coverage for the new test.

## 43. [[Bugfix][KV Connector] Mooncake store: register the committed blocks of an extensible KV cache](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65331)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65331
- **Author:** [gcanlin](https://github.com/gcanlin)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 17:35:24
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65331/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_mooncake_store_worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/mooncake/store/worker.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a startup AssertionError in MooncakeStoreConnector when the extensible KV cache is enabled. The worker previously derived num_blocks from cache_config.num_gpu_blocks, which still holds the warmup size when the connector is created against committed views, causing group_kernel_blocks to assert. It now reads num_blocks from its own _kv_cache_config, consistent with the rest of the worker, and adds a unit test covering the committed-block-count scenario.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change limited to Mooncake store worker block accounting: registration now uses the committed KV cache block count rather than the stale warmup value, allowing startup to succeed and the cache to extend (validated to 1920 blocks / 105.38 GiB per rank). The new test asserts the registered block count and buffer registration match the committed cache, and reportedly fails without the fix.

**Potential risks**  
The fix assumes _kv_cache_config.num_blocks is always populated and authoritative at registration time; if any code path leaves it unset or inconsistent with the actual tensor shapes, the assertion could resurface or silently mis-register. Validation is limited to a single hardware/config (8x B200, DEP8, standalone store), so other topologies or non-extensible configurations are only covered by existing tests. The removed explicit None check on num_gpu_blocks is replaced by reliance on _kv_cache_config.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is an ordering issue between connector creation and cache_config updates during extensible KV cache setup, so sourcing the block count from the connector's own config is the correct fix. The change is small and well-targeted, backed by a regression test and end-to-end validation including GSM8K accuracy parity and needle read-back. AI assistance was disclosed, and the PR description is thorough about validation scope.

## 44. [[Bugfix] Fix DFlash draft acceptance of 0 with FULL CUDA graphs](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64059)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64059
- **Author:** [vadiklyutiy](https://github.com/vadiklyutiy)
- **Merged By:** [TheEpicDolphin](https://github.com/TheEpicDolphin)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 17:32:33
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64059/files) (4):**
  - `tests/v1/e2e/spec_decode/acceptance_rates/dflash/test_dflash.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/autoregressive/speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/dflash/speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/speculator.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a bug where DFlash-family draft models under FULL CUDA graphs read a stale kernel block table, causing zero draft token acceptance. The fix extracts the existing kernel block table mapping logic from TargetDependentARSpeculator into a shared _map_kernel_block_tables helper and calls it in DFlashSpeculator.propose before the FULL graph replay when the eager metadata build is skipped. A regression test row (qwen3.5-4b-dflash2-mrv2) is added to test_dflash_correctness.

**Technical impact**  
Restores correct draft acceptance (~0.185 vs 0.0) and roughly triples throughput (20.5-21.2 vs 6.6 req/s) for hybrid models like Qwen3.5 with DFlash drafts under default FULL_AND_PIECEWISE graphs. The refactor is behavior-preserving for TargetDependentARSpeculator since it calls the same mapping logic. Affects DFlash, DFlash2, DSpark, LiLiCorr, and XPress which share this propose path.

**Potential risks**  
The new test row uses a community checkpoint (nchapman/Qwen3.5-4B-DFlash2) rather than an official one, and thresholds were measured only on B200 while the -k dflash2 selector also matches H100 and MI355 CI steps, risking flaky failures on other hardware. The original issue's checkpoints and the 'no KV cache group could be identified' warning were not reproduced, so the fix may not cover all reported scenarios. The mapping is now invoked on every FULL replay, adding minor per-step overhead.

**Key insights**  
Root cause is that the in-graph _update_draft_decode_metadata only refreshes per-builder data and never maps the persistent kernel block table, so after capture the buffer retains null-block rows. The fix mirrors the pattern already used by TargetDependentARSpeculator, keeping the FULL draft path from #60692 enabled rather than disabling it. Validation is thorough with before/after metrics and a failing-then-passing regression test, though CI portability across GPU types remains a concern.

  - `docs/contributing/dockerfile/dockerfile.md`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a Buildkite release-pipeline step that publishes the nightly CUDA 13.0 EFA image to DockerHub via the existing push-nightly-builds.sh and cleanup-nightly-builds.sh scripts, and updates docker-build-metadata-args.sh so the EFA variant's VLLM_IMAGE_TAG uses the efa-nightly-<commit> prefix matching what is actually published. Docs and a new parametrized test are included.

**Technical impact**  
Changes CI behavior by introducing a new DockerHub publish/cleanup step gated on NIGHTLY=1, and alters the image tag/label emitted for the efa variant (and keeps cu*-efa behavior consistent). The prefix-based tagging avoids collision with the default nightly- cleanup selector, so EFA tags are not deleted by the standard nightly cleanup.

**Potential risks**  
The publish step only runs in the nightly pipeline, so the first nightly after merge is the real end-to-end validation; a misconfiguration would only surface then. The tag format change for efa (and cu*-efa) could affect any downstream consumers relying on the old nightly-<sha>-efa label. The known Rubin CUDA 13.4 aarch64 EFA failure is explicitly out of scope and remains broken.

**Key insights**  
Reusing the existing push/cleanup scripts with a variant prefix is a low-complexity approach, and the new test directly encodes the invariant that metadata tags match published tags. The deliberate choice of efa-nightly- over nightly-<sha>-efa to avoid cleanup prefix collisions is a subtle but important correctness detail worth preserving.

## 46. [[Bugfix] Step3 tool parser: coerce nullable and object/array parameters by schema](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65274)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65274
- **Author:** [0xDexe](https://github.com/0xDexe)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 16:34:23
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65274/files) (2):**
  - `tests/tool_parsers/test_step3_tool_parser.py`
  - `vllm/tool_parsers/step3_tool_parser.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Step3 tool parser's _cast_arguments previously only coerced parameters whose schema had a single string type, leaving nullable (Optional[...]) and object/array parameters as raw strings. This PR replaces the manual if/elif type dispatch with the shared extract_types_from_schema and coerce_to_schema_type helpers, and adds 13 parametrized tests covering nullable, object, array, untyped, and uncoercible values.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior now aligns with other tool parsers (Muse, MiniCPM5, Poolside V1) by reusing common helpers that handle type lists, anyOf/oneOf/allOf, enum inference, and non-finite numbers. Nullable params are coerced correctly, object/array params are JSON-parsed, and the unused contextlib import is removed. Two inherited behavior changes: whole numbers return as ints rather than floats, and '1'/'0' are accepted for booleans.

**Potential risks**  
The inherited helper behavior changes could subtly affect downstream clients expecting floats for number types or rejecting '1'/'0' for booleans, though both are valid JSON. The tests rely on a private method (_cast_arguments) and a mock tokenizer, which is acceptable but couples tests to internals. The pre-existing xfail markers for non-streaming extraction are untouched and remain a known limitation.

**Key insights**  
Reusing shared coercion helpers reduces duplication and fixes a class of bugs across parsers consistently. The PR is well-scoped, documents inherited behavior changes for reviewers, and includes targeted tests that run without model downloads. The root cause analysis is clear and the fix is minimal.

## 47. [[Perf][Spec Decode] Pad DFlash draft inputs on extra programs of the same launch](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65488)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65488
- **Author:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged By:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 16:26:18
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65488/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/dflash/speculator.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Refactors the padding logic in prepare_dflash_inputs so that instead of the last request's program serially padding all output buffers up to max_num_reqs/max_num_tokens, 32 extra programs are launched in the same grid and the padding loops are split across them. This reduces device time from ~5-8.5us to ~2.1us per call while keeping a single kernel launch and bit-identical outputs.

**Technical impact**  
The grid grows from (num_reqs, num_blocks) to (num_reqs + 32, num_blocks), and num_reqs is now passed as a kernel argument with do_not_specialize since it is no longer tl.num_programs(0). Padding is extracted into a helper _pad_dflash_inputs invoked only by extra programs (block 0), with each program striding over every 32nd block. Regular per-request programs no longer perform padding, and host time is unchanged since it remains one launch.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness depends on the strided partition covering exactly the same ranges as before; off-by-one errors in the range bounds (e.g., max_num_reqs + 1 for query_start_loc) or mask conditions could leave gaps or overwrite valid data. The do_not_specialize on num_reqs may affect Triton specialization/performance in edge cases, and the extra 32 programs add minor launch overhead for very small batches. Validation is limited to a single GB200 GPU and a microbenchmark, so behavior on other hardware or with different max_num_reqs/max_num_tokens is not demonstrated.

**Key insights**  
The change is a well-motivated micro-optimization that removes a serial padding bottleneck without adding a second kernel launch, addressing the main concern raised on the alternative PR #58633. The PR provides strong evidence (bit-identical outputs, device/host timing tables, unit tests, and end-to-end measurement) but the end-to-end gain is small (~0.2-0.4% of step time), so the value is primarily in the per-call device-time reduction. Reviewers should focus on verifying the strided padding ranges and masks match the original semantics exactly.

## 48. [[Perf][Spec Decode] Skip vocab blocks with no target token in the resample kernel](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65100)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65100
- **Author:** [zyongye](https://github.com/zyongye)
- **Merged By:** [zyongye](https://github.com/zyongye)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 16:12:16
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65100/files) (7):**
  - `tests/v1/spec_decode/test_rejection_sampler_utils.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_rejection_sampler_chunking.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_sampler_flags.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/sample/sampler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/sample/states.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/rejection_sampler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/rejection_sampler_utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds an opt-in fast path to the MRV2 _resample_kernel that returns early for vocab blocks whose target logits are all -inf, skipping the draft-logit load and Gumbel draw. A new per-request may_mask_logits flag in Sampler (set for min-p/top-k/top-p, thinking-budget forcing, or logits processors, but not temperature alone) gates the skip in RejectionSampler._verify, so unmasked chunks pay no cost. Tests cover bitwise identity with skip on/off and the new flag semantics.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior is unchanged for sampled outputs (claimed bitwise identical) but kernel execution changes: masked blocks now store block_idx*BLOCK_SIZE and -inf directly instead of computing residual and argmax. Reported 2-4x speedup on top-p-masked verify rows (e.g. 165.9 to 55.4 us at 1024 rows) with no regression on unmasked rows. Adds a new public parameter skip_masked_vocab_blocks to rejection_sample and a new Sampler attribute consumed by the rejection sampler.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness hinges on the invariant that an all -inf target block implies an all -inf residual and that reporting index 0 for such blocks matches the argmax tie-break; any future change to residual computation or argmax semantics could silently break this. The may_mask_logits gate is conservative but must stay in sync with every code path that can produce -inf (e.g. new logits processors or sampling features); a missed case would disable the optimization (perf only) while an over-broad case is safe. End-to-end serving throughput was not measured, and the change interacts with the follow-up on-load top-p PRs (#65102/#65103), so integration there needs verification.

**Key insights**  
The optimization is a well-scoped, gated micro-optimization with strong bitwise-identity tests across fp32/fp64 and with/without draft logits. Separating needs_logits_processing from may_mask_logits is the key design choice that avoids paying the ~5% block-max cost on temperature-only or unmasked workloads. The PR is part of a larger rejection-sampling stack, so reviewers should confirm the invariant holds under the subsequent on-load top-p changes.

## 49. [[MRV2][Spec Decode] Refactor AR speculators and enable CUDA Graph for draft model](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60989)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #60989
- **Author:** [wxsIcey](https://github.com/wxsIcey)
- **Merged By:** [TheEpicDolphin](https://github.com/TheEpicDolphin)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 15:59:24
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60989/files) (11):**
  - `tests/v1/cudagraph/test_cudagraph_manager.py`
  - `tests/v1/spec_decode/test_dflash_profile.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/cudagraph_utils.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/model_runner.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/autoregressive/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/autoregressive/cudagraph_utils.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/autoregressive/speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/multi_module_mtp/speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/standalone_ar/speculator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/target_dependent_ar/speculator.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR refactors the Model Runner V2 autoregressive speculators by extracting a shared AutoRegressiveSpeculator base class that unifies CUDA Graph management and multi-step decode logic, and it enables CUDA Graph capture/replay for standalone draft-model speculators (previously a no-op). It also moves the speculator cudagraph_utils into a new autoregressive package, replaces the num_extra_query_per_req attribute with speculative_config.max_num_new_slots_for_drafting, and adds a slot_mappings_buffer parameter so captured graphs reuse the runtime slot-mapping buffer.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes are significant: standalone AR speculators now capture prefill and multi-step decode CUDA graphs, and the target-dependent speculator's capture/decode paths are reorganized into shared hooks (_prefill, _generate_draft, _prepare_decode_inputs, lifecycle callbacks). The dummy-run token budget in model_runner now derives from speculative_config rather than the speculator attribute, and prepare_inputs_to_capture can bind a custom slot-mapping buffer with PAD_SLOT_ID padding. Reported validation shows ~23% throughput gain at concurrency 1 and ~2% at concurrency 8.

**Potential risks**  
The refactor touches shared capture/decode paths used by Eagle, MTP, Gemma4, and standalone drafters, so regressions could affect all spec-decode modes even though only standalone AR gains new functionality. The new slot_mappings_buffer assertions (shape[1] >= num_tokens) and the PAD_SLOT_ID fill could break if buffer sizing assumptions differ across configurations. The test change constructs the speculator via object.__new__ and manual __dict__ updates, which is brittle and may mask initialization issues. The truncated diff limits full review of the standalone speculator's new prefill/decode implementation.

**Key insights**  
The core design is a template-method refactor: AutoRegressiveSpeculator owns CUDA Graph capture, dispatch, and the multi-step loop, while subclasses supply prefill, draft generation, and decode-input preparation. Enabling CUDA Graphs for standalone draft models is the main functional win, backed by concrete benchmark numbers. Reviewers should focus on the new slot-mapping buffer aliasing in cudagraph_utils, the removal of num_extra_query_per_req in favor of speculative_config, and whether all subclass hooks preserve prior eager semantics.

## 50. [[Bugfix] Size the extensible KV cache from UMA-corrected free memory](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65413)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65413
- **Author:** [aijanai](https://github.com/aijanai)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 15:57:43
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65413/files) (6):**
  - `tests/utils_/test_mem_utils.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_extensible_kv_cache.py`
  - `vllm/platforms/cuda.py`
  - `vllm/platforms/interface.py`
  - `vllm/utils/mem_utils.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/extensible_kv_cache.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR fixes undersized extensible KV cache on integrated (UMA) GPUs by extracting the existing psutil-based free-memory correction from MemorySnapshot.measure() into a new device_memory_info() helper in vllm/utils/mem_utils.py, and routing the three extensible_kv_cache call sites (committable_blocks, measure_kv_cache_blocks, and the free-memory read in _release_for_weight_update) through it. Previously those paths read raw torch.accelerator.get_memory_info, which on UMA parts counts reclaimable page cache as used, causing up to 84% undersizing and zero-block failures in disaggregated serving. Platform signatures for is_integrated_gpu were widened to accept device_id: int \\| None to support the helper.

**Technical impact**  
Centralizes UMA free-memory correction so profiling and extensible KV cache sizing agree, and discrete GPUs are unaffected since the helper returns the raw get_memory_info values when is_integrated_gpu is false. The change is small (~30 lines of main code) and reuses existing behavior rather than introducing new configuration. Tests patch platform, psutil, and accelerator to simulate UMA readings in CI, and the new end-to-end test fails before the fix and passes after, confirming the behavior change is exercised.

**Potential risks**  
The helper now calls torch.device(device).index and passes None to is_integrated_gpu when device is None, which relies on the widened platform signature and on downstream implementations (e.g. CUDA) correctly handling None as the current device; any platform subclass overriding is_integrated_gpu with the old int-only signature could break. Sizing from psutil available memory assumes the OS will actually reclaim page cache under device allocation pressure, which the PR validates on GB10 but not across all UMA platforms. The test_memory_profiling failure is reported as a pre-existing UMA flake, but it means the modified test file is not fully green on the target hardware.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a measurement inconsistency: MemorySnapshot already applied the psutil correction, but the extensible cache re-read raw cudaMemGetInfo, so page cache was double-counted as engine memory and missing from headroom, driving the headroom term negative in P/D. Extracting a single device_memory_info helper is the right fix versus disabling the feature on UMA, and the reclaim experiment (committing 106 GiB against 59.2 GiB reported free) provides solid evidence the corrected figure is safe to size from. The PR also notes coordination needed with open PR #55828, which would hit the same raw reading on GB10.

## 51. [[Core][KV Connector] Start capped async KV loads while the batch is full](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65319)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65319
- **Author:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged By:** [ivanium](https://github.com/ivanium)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 15:47:05
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65319/files) (8):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_remote_prefill_lifecycle.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/utils.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/tiering/p2p/test_manager.py`
  - `vllm/config/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/base.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/multi_connector.py`
  - `vllm/engine/arg_utils.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new --max-num-inflight-kv-loads scheduler option and a new KVConnectorBase_V1.may_load_async hook so async KV loads (e.g. P/D NIXL pulls) can start while the running batch is full, capped by the number of requests holding KV blocks for in-flight or finished-but-waiting loads. The scheduler gains a dedicated async_kv_waiting queue, merges it with waiting in policy order when slots are free, and tracks aborted loads so cancelling requests cannot exceed the cap. Motivation is avoiding bursty load starts and idle slots on full decode batches.

**Technical impact**  
Touches core scheduler scheduling loop, request enqueueing, connector interface, MultiConnector, SchedulerConfig, and EngineArgs CLI wiring. Behavior is gated behind the new option (default None preserves existing behavior), but the scheduling loop is restructured (loads_only path, take_async_kv flag, new queue merge helper) and on_new_request now runs before enqueueing, which is a subtle ordering change affecting all connectors. New state (async_kv_waiting, _aborted_kv_loads) must be maintained across preemption, abort, and finish paths.

**Potential risks**  
The PR itself reports 9 failing tests in multi-connector/offloading suites with unconfirmed status on main, and no end-to-end P/D benchmark yet, so performance claims are unvalidated. The cap must be tuned to slot-free rate times load latency; too low starves loads, too high allows large first-fill bursts. A may_load_async false positive can stall async loads queued behind it, and the on_new_request ordering change could affect connectors relying on prior queue state. The scheduling loop rewrite is intricate and risks regressions in pause/preemption and priority/FCFS ordering.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly separates requests that can load without a slot into their own queue, bounding both per-step load bursts and KV-block pile-up via a single cap counting in-progress, completed-waiting, and aborted loads. The new connector hook keeps the feature opt-in and extensible (MultiConnector ORs, offloading can opt in later). Main gaps before merge: confirm the 9 failing tests also fail on main, and run the promised end-to-end P/D benchmark to justify the cap and default.

## 52. [[ROCm] Migrate Aiter sigmoid to topk_gating](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63419)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63419
- **Author:** [rjrock](https://github.com/rjrock)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 15:36:08
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63419/files) (3):**
  - `tests/model_executor/test_enabled_custom_ops.py`
  - `vllm/_aiter_ops.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/router/fused_topk_router.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR migrates ROCm AITER sigmoid top-k routing from the soon-to-be-deprecated rocm_aiter_ops.topk_sigmoid to rocm_aiter_ops.topk_gating (exposed as topk_sigmoid_gating), mirroring the existing softmax path. It adds a score_func parameter to the AITER topk_gating custom op, removes the old rocm_aiter_topk_sigmoid op, and updates dispatch_topk_sigmoid_func to select topk_gating only when gating is enabled and the launch is supported (contiguous, <=4096 tokens). This fixes a failing test where renormalization was not applied.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the sigmoid routing kernel selection on gfx942/gfx950 with AITER enabled, gaining renormalize support and forward compatibility with AITER's deprecation of topk_sigmoid. The dispatch function signature now accepts optional tensor arguments, and the custom op registry drops rocm_aiter_topk_sigmoid in favor of the generalized topk_gating op. Non-ROCm and unsupported launches fall back to vllm_topk_sigmoid, preserving existing behavior.

**Potential risks**  
The dispatch now depends on tensor shape/contiguity and token count, so behavior differs by launch; the 4096-token cap is tuned for softmax and may not be optimal for sigmoid. Removing the rocm_aiter_topk_sigmoid op could break any external callers referencing it. Correctness relies on AITER's topk_gating sigmoid path matching vLLM semantics, validated only by a single kernel test.

**Key insights**  
The change unifies softmax and sigmoid routing through one AITER kernel, reducing code duplication and enabling renormalize for sigmoid. Tests were expanded to cover gating-enabled/disabled and launch-shape edge cases via monkeypatching, which is good coverage. The PR is well-scoped but its performance and correctness claims rest on limited validation, so broader CI on ROCm hardware is advisable.

## 53. [[HiSparse] Remove dead code, unused custom-op arguments, and a stale connector check](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64559)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64559
- **Author:** [MatthewBonanni](https://github.com/MatthewBonanni)
- **Merged By:** [LucasWilkinson](https://github.com/LucasWilkinson)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 15:33:03
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64559/files) (17):**
  - `csrc/hisparse_kernels.cu`
  - `csrc/ops.h`
  - `csrc/torch_bindings.cpp`
  - `tests/test_config.py`
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_sparse_mla_backends.py`
  - `tests/v1/core/test_prefix_caching.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_hisparse_connector.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_utils.py`
  - `vllm/config/vllm.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/hisparse/worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/base_worker.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v32/attention.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backend.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/mla/flashmla_sparse.py`
  - `vllm/v1/hisparse/coordinator.py`
  - `vllm/v1/hisparse/runtime.py`
  - `vllm/v1/hisparse/types.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Removes dead HiSparse code paths, unused custom-op arguments, and stale connector checks across 17 files (+194/-394). This includes dropping SparseKVPageTransfer.after_forward, unused coordinator/worker attributes, the private-pool branch of allocate_hisparse_host_pools, redundant NIXL and DeepseekV32 branches, and several kernel arguments (miss_mask, resolved_global_indices, resident_null_block, attention stride args). It also adds a config-time validation that HiSparse requires HiSparseConnector with host_pool_gib, and changes the plugin cache-ops signatures.

**Technical impact**  
The custom-op signatures for hisparse_resolve_residency and hisparse_gather_plan shrink by three args each, and the kernel drops writes to removed buffers. The new config check moves a previously late KV-planning failure to config time, and the stale connector allowlist warning is removed. Hot-path tidy-ups (cached arange, is_host_writer reuse, shared load_hot_row_warp helper, dropped per-call pinned check) reduce overhead without changing kernel SASS. Out-of-tree plugin implementations of create_cache_ops would need to follow the new signatures, though none are known to exist.

**Potential risks**  
The behavior change is real: HiSparse without HiSparseConnector + host_pool_gib now fails at config time, including HiSparseConnector combined with --kv-offloading-size, which could break existing user configs that previously constructed successfully. The plugin cache-ops signature change is a breaking API change for any external implementers. Removing the pinned-memory check in hisparse_resolve_residency relies on the assumption it never reads host memory; if that invariant is ever violated, silent correctness issues could arise. The PR reports benchmark parity within run-to-run noise and matching greedy outputs, but the divergence analysis is somewhat nuanced.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-scoped and carefully justified, with each removal traced to its origin PR and a stated reason it is dead. The new test_hisparse_requires_hisparse_connector covers the three failure modes and fails on main, confirming the behavior change is intentional and tested. The GPU validation (forced host offload, GSM8K, greedy outputs) is thorough, though the greedy divergence discussion shows main itself is nondeterministic at near-tie tokens, which limits the strength of the correctness claim. The plugin signature change is the main compatibility concern and should be called out in release notes.

## 54. [[Kernel][DSA] Restore FP32 indexer MQA logits for non-DeepSeek models and add `indexer_logits_dtype`](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64580)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64580
- **Author:** [zyongye](https://github.com/zyongye)
- **Merged By:** [zyongye](https://github.com/zyongye)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 15:13:24
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64580/files) (18):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/models_basic.yaml`
  - `cmake/external_projects/deepgemm.cmake`
  - `docs/configuration/conserving_memory.md`
  - `tests/kernels/attention/test_deepgemm_attention.py`
  - `tests/kernels/attention/test_fused_mqa_topk.py`
  - `tests/kernels/test_top_k_per_row.py`
  - `tests/model_executor/layers/test_mla_short_prefill_indexer.py`
  - `tests/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/specialized_decode/test_numerics.py`
  - `tests/models/test_indexer_logits_dtype_config.py`
  - `vllm/config/attention.py`
  - `vllm/envs.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/sparse_attn_indexer.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/sparse_mqa_indexer.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/config.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v32/attention.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/specialized_decode/attention/indexer.py`
  - `vllm/models/glm5next/nvidia/sparse_indexer.py`
  - `vllm/utils/deep_gemm.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Bumps the DeepGEMM pin to ae4fd25, which restores FP32 output for the dense MQA-logits kernels behind a new logits_dtype argument, and adds a new --attention-config.indexer_logits_dtype option (auto/float32/bfloat16) for the DSA sparse indexer. The auto default is resolved per model via config hooks: DeepSeek-V3.2/V4/V4.1 stay on BF16 on SM100, while GLM-5/GLM-5.3/HY-V4 and hookless indexers go back to FP32. The change threads logits_dtype through the DeepGEMM wrappers, the sparse indexer, the GLM-5.3 kpool indexer, and the DeepSeek-V4.1 specialized decode indexer, and sizes the dummy logits allocation and decode chunk budget from the actual dtype instead of the architecture.

**Technical impact**  
Restores numerically exact top-k selection (FP32 logits match the FP64 reference top-2048) for non-DeepSeek DSA models on SM100 while preserving main's BF16 behavior for DeepSeek. The dtype now drives the reduction precision on SM100 (FP32 weights reduce in FP32, BF16 weights in BF16), so mqa_logits_mx_inputs casts weights to logits_dtype. Memory profiling and the decode chunk splitter now account for 2- vs 4-byte logits, and FP32 decode logits skip the as_fp32_logits upcast. SM90/SM120 behavior is unchanged by default, and the sparse MQA-logits path stays BF16.

**Potential risks**  
The per-model default is keyed on architecture name rather than layer, so any new indexer model class without a config hook silently resolves auto to FP32, which could change numerics or memory for future models. The DeepGEMM bump also pulls in unrelated MegaMoE/NVFP4/BF16 weight-layout changes, widening the blast radius beyond MQA logits. The upstream weights-dtype check relaxation lets sparse MQA-logits kernels accept FP32 weights on SM100; vLLM always passes BF16 there but this is a latent upstream bug. Long-context MRCR validation that actually exercises top-k selection is still pending, and the E2E GSM8K eval cannot distinguish the two paths since top-k never drops tokens. Decode timings are not CUDA-graphed, so the real decode cost of the upcast is unmeasured.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped: a single config knob with a per-model auto default, threaded consistently through every CUDA consumer of the dense MQA-logits kernels, plus parametrized kernel tests asserting output dtype and a CPU-only config-resolution test added to CI. The accuracy table shows FP32 logits are exact against FP64 while BF16 changes 0.4-0.75% of selected tokens, justifying the FP32 default for GLM/HY-V4. The main open gaps are the missing long-context eval and the architecture-name-keyed default, which is fragile if new indexer models are added without a hook.

## 55. [[Spec Decode][Perf] Fuse DFlash2 candidate edge scoring into a Triton kernel](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64916)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64916
- **Author:** [TheEpicDolphin](https://github.com/TheEpicDolphin)
- **Merged By:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 15:10:28
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64916/files) (4):**
  - `tests/v1/spec_decode/test_dflash2.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/qwen3_dflash2.py`
  - `vllm/models/beam/nvidia/dflash2.py`
  - `vllm/models/beam/nvidia/dflash2_ops.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Replaces two separate DFlash2 candidate edge scoring implementations (a compiled torch graph in Qwen3 and a bespoke Triton kernel in Beam) with a single shared Triton kernel dflash2_score_edges, registered as a custom op and dispatched via _score_edges, with the old torch path retained as _score_edges_reference for CPU and tests. Beam's dflash2_ops.py is deleted and its selector now calls the shared _score_edges. The goal is to cut kernel launches and improve performance while unifying numerics.

**Technical impact**  
The new kernel uses one program per (request, step), gathers anchor/candidate rows, forms A*H in FP32, and computes the KxK edge block with tl.dot, adding the unary term. Output is now always FP32 ([num_reqs, steps, K, K]) regardless of unary dtype, and the edge term is no longer rounded to bf16, improving accuracy (MAE vs FP64 drops from 7.2e-5 to 5.2e-5). Microbenchmarks show 2.7x-5.1x speedups for Qwen and up to 3.9x for Beam at higher concurrency, with modest end-to-end gains and unchanged acceptance lengths.

**Potential risks**  
The dtype contract changed: callers that previously relied on scores matching unary_logits dtype (e.g., bf16) now receive FP32, which could affect downstream consumers expecting the old dtype or memory layout. The kernel assumes contiguous candidate/unary/hidden inputs (it calls .contiguous()) and relies on anchor_token_ids.stride(0) for strided anchor views; unusual strides or non-CUDA tensors fall back to the reference path. Anchor clamping to [0, vocab_size-1] is now done in-kernel, so behavior for out-of-vocab anchors must match the reference exactly, which the updated tests attempt to cover but only on CUDA.

**Key insights**  
This is a solid consolidation: one kernel serves both model families, removes duplicated code, and improves both speed and numerical fidelity. The main review focus should be on the FP32 output dtype change propagating safely through all consumers, the correctness of the in-kernel anchor clamp versus the reference, and ensuring the custom op's fake impl and contiguity handling are robust under torch.compile and CUDA graphs. Test coverage was updated to compare against the reference and assert FP32 output, which is appropriate.

## 56. [[Bugfix][MoE] Reject non-contiguous routing tensors](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57167)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57167
- **Author:** [NikitaLogvinenko](https://github.com/NikitaLogvinenko)
- **Merged By:** [mgoin](https://github.com/mgoin)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 15:03:53
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57167/files) (6):**
  - `csrc/moe/moe_align_sum_kernels.cu`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_moe.py`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_moe_align_block_size.py`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_routing.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/fused_moe.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/router/fused_moe_router.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds fail-fast contiguity validation for MoE routing tensors (topk_ids and topk_weights) at three layers: the moe_align_block_size C++ wrapper, the Triton launcher/dispatch boundary, and the shared FusedMoERouter.select_experts boundary. This fixes a silent correctness bug where sliced routing tensors with non-unit row stride were accepted by the old stride(1)==1 checks but read incorrectly by kernels that assume dense buffers. Regression tests cover each validation point.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior change: previously accepted non-contiguous routing tensors now raise ValueError/RuntimeError instead of producing silently wrong results. The router-level check covers all modular backends (Triton, FlashInfer CUTLASS, etc.), not just Triton. No hidden copies are introduced, so callers must ensure contiguity themselves; all in-tree routers already return contiguous tensors.

**Potential risks**  
Any out-of-tree custom routing function or downstream caller that returns non-contiguous routing tensors will now hard-fail rather than silently miscompute, which is intended but could surface as a breaking change for such users. The C++ check adds a per-call contiguity test on a hot path (minor overhead). The CUDA regression test was not run locally in the original submission, though the update reports it passing on B300; one unrelated test failure (flashinfer trtllm backend) is noted as pre-existing.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was an insufficient stride check (stride(1)==1) that missed non-unit row strides from column slices of packed tensors, as hit by ZAYA1. Placing the check at the shared router boundary is the key design choice, ensuring coverage across all backends rather than patching each kernel individually. The fix prioritizes fail-fast correctness over silent fallback, which is appropriate for a numerical-correctness bug.

## 57. [[KV Offload] Adapt to the new KVCR key adapter API](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65455)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65455
- **Author:** [mkhazraee](https://github.com/mkhazraee)
- **Merged By:** [khluu](https://github.com/khluu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 15:01:51
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65455/files) (3):**
  - `.buildkite/scripts/install-kv-offload.sh`
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/tiering/test_kvcr_tier.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/tiering/kvcr/manager.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adapts vLLM's KV offload tiering code to a new KVCR key adapter API that splits framework and router key conversions. The _VllmKeyAdapter now implements encode_to_framework, decode_from_framework, encode_to_router, and decode_from_router, with unused methods stubbed via NotImplementedError. Callers in lookup, submit_load, submit_store, and _align_sequence were updated to use decode_from_framework, and the kvcr dependency pin was bumped to a new commit.

**Technical impact**  
Renames and restructures the key adapter interface, changing method semantics from encode/decode to directional framework/router conversions. The dependency pin update in install-kv-offload.sh ties CI to a specific kvcr commit, and the test was updated to exercise encode_to_router instead of decode. Behavior should remain equivalent for the used paths, but the interface contract is now stricter.

**Potential risks**  
The two stubbed methods raise NotImplementedError, so any future or hidden caller invoking encode_to_framework or decode_from_router will fail at runtime. The dependency pin bump to an unreleased git commit could introduce instability if that commit changes or is force-pushed. Test coverage only validates encode_to_router, leaving the framework conversion path less directly asserted.

**Key insights**  
This is a mechanical API migration driven by an upstream KVCR change, with no intended behavioral change in vLLM. The directional naming (to/from framework vs router) clarifies intent but requires careful auditing of all call sites. The pinned commit hash is the main external coupling risk worth monitoring.

## 58. [[CI][Test] Validate manual NVFP4 fusion in NVIDIA Qwen mixed-precision test](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65495)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65495
- **Author:** [johncalesp](https://github.com/johncalesp)
- **Merged By:** [arpera](https://github.com/arpera)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 14:37:47
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65495/files) (2):**
  - `tests/compile/fusions_e2e/models.py`
  - `tests/compile/fusions_e2e/test_tp1_quant.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The mixed-precision Qwen NVFP4 test previously asserted that the compiler's ActivationQuantFusionPass produced four act_quant_fusion matches, but the NVIDIA Qwen model uses CompilationMode.NONE and performs fusion explicitly via maybe_fused_act_quant, so the counter stayed at zero and the test failed. This PR rewrites the test to drive the model through a worker extension that inspects each Qwen2MoeMLP, counts _C.silu_and_mul_nvfp4_quant dispatches with a TorchDispatchMode, validates the packed QuantizedActivation passed to down_proj, and compares outputs against the unfused chain. It also corrects a misleading fixture comment in models.py.

**Technical impact**  
The test now exercises the real runtime fusion path rather than a compiler pass counter, adding a worker extension class, TorchDispatchMode-based op counting, forward hooks on gate_up_proj and down_proj, and a numerical comparison against the unfused activation/projection chain using the existing 1e-1 NVFP4 tolerance. It switches from the shared run_e2e_fusion_test helper to a direct vllm_runner invocation with explicit CompilationConfig, attention backend, and environment overrides, and runs for both 1 and 17 token inputs across the four MLPs. No production code is touched.

**Potential risks**  
The test depends on internal APIs (Qwen2MoeMLP, QuantizedActivation, get_input_quant_key, kNvfp4Dynamic, _C.silu_and_mul_nvfp4_quant) and on the exact op being dispatched, so refactors of the fusion layer or op naming could break it. The loose 1e-1 atol/rtol tolerance may mask subtle numerical regressions, and the assertion that compilation mode is NONE could fail if the model later adopts compilation. Environment overrides like VLLM_USE_DEEP_GEMM=0 and spawn multiprocessing may interact with other CI configurations, and the test is GPU-specific (Rubin/GB200 validated) with no CPU fallback.

**Key insights**  
The original failure was a stale test expectation, not a runtime bug: the model intentionally bypasses the compiler pass and fuses manually, so validating the compiler counter was the wrong signal. The new approach validates the actual contract (one NVFP4 quant op per MLP, correct QuantizedActivation metadata, and numerical equivalence to the unfused chain), and the reported negative control disabling fusion in one layer confirms the test genuinely detects missing fusion rather than passing vacuously.

## 59. [[Perf][Qwen3.5] (NVIDIA path) Fused AR+norm+quant](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64723)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64723
- **Author:** [gau-nernst](https://github.com/gau-nernst)
- **Merged By:** [gau-nernst](https://github.com/gau-nernst)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 14:36:10
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64723/files) (4):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/models_basic.yaml`
  - `tests/models/qwen3_5/test_fused_allreduce_norm_quant.py`
  - `vllm/models/qwen3_5/nvidia/model.py`
  - `vllm/models/qwen3_5/nvidia/ops/fused_allreduce_norm_quant.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new model-private op (vllm/models/qwen3_5/nvidia/ops/fused_allreduce_norm_quant.py) that calls FlashInfer's allreduce_fusion with kARResidualRMSNormOutFP8Quant/kARResidualRMSNormOutFP4Quant to fuse the TP all-reduce, residual add, Gemma RMSNorm, and consumer input quantization. Qwen3_5RMSNorm gains a reduce_inputs flag and a forward_fused entry point that replaces the previous forward_quant plus separate fused_allreduce_gemma_rms_norm calls in the decoder layer, with a Triton norm-quant fallback above the FlashInfer workspace budget. A new TP2 test and a CI num_devices=2 bump accompany the change. The goal is decode-step TPOT reduction on the Qwen3.5 NVFP4 NVIDIA path.

**Technical impact**  
Removes one standalone FP8/NVFP4 quant kernel per input norm (92 per decode step) by folding it into the all-reduce+norm kernel, which the PR reports as ~0.34 ms/step at c1 (~4% TPOT). Behavior is gated by dispatch conditions (TP>1, FlashInfer AR, static FP8 or NVFP4 with K%64==0 and no padding, within workspace budget); all other paths retain prior behavior. The fused kernel writes the new residual in place into the hidden_states buffer, so callers must not reuse that tensor. A module-level _workspace_unsupported flag permanently disables the fused path after one ValueError, which is a process-wide side effect.

**Potential risks**  
Bitwise-identity claims depend on FlashInfer kernel internals and the SWIZZLED_128x4 layout matching scaled_fp4_quant; any FlashInfer version drift could silently change numerics. The global _workspace_unsupported flag is not keyed by TP group or device, so a single failure (e.g. trtllm backend across nodes) disables fusion for all subsequent calls in the process, including potentially valid configurations. In-place residual_out=hidden_states aliasing requires careful auditing of all call sites. The fallback path (explicit all-reduce + Triton norm-quant) is only tolerance-matched, not bitwise, so accuracy can shift slightly at large batch. GSM8K dropped from 0.9773 to 0.9735, which is within noise but not explicitly analyzed. CI now requires 2 GPUs for the Qwen3.5 B200 step, increasing resource cost.

**Key insights**  
The design is well-scoped as model-private with no shared-code or torch.compile changes, and the dispatch table clearly documents which consumers get fusion versus fallback. The test strategy is strong: it validates bitwise identity against the exact chain being replaced using real ModelOpt FP8/NVFP4 linears, and explicitly exercises the workspace-budget fallback and the no-all-reduce path. The main review focus should be the process-global _workspace_unsupported flag semantics, the in-place residual aliasing contract, and confirming the small GSM8K delta is noise rather than a regression.
