## 60. [[ROCm][CI] Untrusted base builds wait for main's images instead of rebuilding them](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63949)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63949
- **Author:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 14:19:28
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63949/files) (2):**
  - `.buildkite/scripts/rocm/build.sh`
  - `tests/tools/test_rocm_build.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The ROCm CI build script now computes image keys at an arbitrary revision via a new keys_at() helper, and untrusted base builds whose keys match their merge base with main wait for main's CI to push those images instead of rebuilding them in parallel. A trusted base build publishes a heartbeat mark (rocm/vllm-ci:building-<tag>, refreshed every 5 minutes) so waiting builds can detect a live main build, with a start grace, staleness window, and overall deadline. Tests were extended with fake git/docker/date/sleep shims to cover waiting, reuse, grace, registry errors, and heartbeat behavior.

**Technical impact**  
Reduces duplicate multi-hour base image rebuilds on the amd-cpu queue when main's image keys change, by serializing PR base builds behind main's trusted build. Introduces new registry artifacts (label-only heartbeat images), new tunable env vars (ROCM_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS, ROCM_HEARTBEAT_STALE_MINUTES, ROCM_TRUSTED_START_GRACE_MINUTES, ROCM_TRUSTED_WAIT_MINUTES, ROCM_TRUSTED_POLL_SECONDS), and a background heartbeat process in ensure(). Key computation is refactored into keys_at() so HEAD and merge-base keys are derived consistently.

**Potential risks**  
Waiting builds depend on main's heartbeat and push timing; a hung main build or a planted mark can hold waiters until the 300-minute backstop, after which a rebuilt stack may exceed the step's 540-minute timeout. PR hosts can still push to rocm/vllm-ci until the PR-host split, so heartbeat marks are not fully trusted. The change touches ensure(), which PR #64131 also edits, so a rebase conflict is likely for whichever merges second. Background heartbeat process cleanup and subshell/trap interactions (NO_MAIN_BUILD temp file) add subtle failure modes.

**Key insights**  
The design correctly scopes waiting to untrusted base builds whose keys equal main's merge-base keys, so branches that change image inputs still build immediately and trusted builds never wait. The once-per-build start grace and heartbeat staleness window bound wasted waiting, and registry errors are distinguished from cache misses to fail fast rather than silently rebuild. Test coverage is broad (17 new tests, 38 total, stable across 10 runs) and HEAD's image keys are unchanged from main's, limiting blast radius.

## 61. [[ROCm][CI] Bake the fast-exit plugin into the test image and fix the ray-compat pin strip](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64132)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64132
- **Author:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 14:18:45
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64132/files) (3):**
  - `.buildkite/scripts/check-ray-compatibility.sh`
  - `.buildkite/scripts/hardware_ci/run-amd-test.sh`
  - `docker/Dockerfile.rocm`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR bakes the amd_ci_fast_exit pytest plugin into the ROCm test image by copying it into site-packages in Dockerfile.rocm, and removes the per-job copy from run-amd-test.sh. It also changes check-ray-compatibility.sh so the local TheRock stack pins (torch, triton, amdsmi) are stripped on every ROCm torch build, not only when download.pytorch.org lacks a rocm<ver> index. The motivation is to avoid a future ray-compat failure once a rocm7.15 index appears and to eliminate redundant per-job plugin copies.

**Technical impact**  
Plugin installation moves from runtime to image build time, so all jobs using the test image inherit the plugin without copying it, and commands that override PYTHONPATH still find it in site-packages. The ray-compat script now unconditionally sets LOCAL_BUILDS=1 for ROCm torch, making pin stripping independent of index availability. Changes touch CI infra and Docker image contents, affecting all AMD CI jobs built on this image.

**Potential risks**  
The image build now depends on the plugin file existing at the COPY path and on python3 sysconfig resolving correctly during build; a failure there breaks the image. Removing the runtime copy means any job using an older or different image without the baked plugin will fail to load the plugin via -p amd_ci_fast_exit. The unconditional LOCAL_BUILDS=1 could alter dependency resolution behavior for cases previously relying on the index-present path. None of this has run in AMD CI yet, per the description.

**Key insights**  
The change is a reasonable consolidation that reduces per-job duplication and hardens the ray-compat check against a latent failure. The dropped AITER nightly workaround is justified by the manylinux_2_28 migration. Main residual risk is the coupling between image contents and run-amd-test.sh, which should be validated in AMD CI before merge.

## 62. [[CI][Tests] Remove timing dependence from Engram prefetch synchronization test](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65439)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65439
- **Author:** [johncalesp](https://github.com/johncalesp)
- **Merged By:** [arpera](https://github.com/arpera)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 13:54:27
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65439/files) (1):**
  - `tests/kernels/test_engram.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The test test_engram_shared_prefetch_stream_waits_per_layer was flaky in full shards on Rubin and GB200 because it relied on a fixed GPU sleep delay to assume the second lookup was still incomplete. The change replaces that timing assumption with a monkeypatched observer around torch.cuda.Stream.wait_event that records which events the consumer waits on, then asserts the consumer waits only on its own layer's prefetch_done event while forwarding to the real CUDA implementation.

**Technical impact**  
The test now verifies the actual synchronization contract (per-layer event dependency) rather than inferring it from timing, making it deterministic across hardware and shard contexts. Real GPU execution and the exact numerical comparison (rtol=0, atol=0) are preserved, and the monkeypatch is scoped via monkeypatch.context() so it does not leak. Validation shows the previously failing shard now passes on both Rubin and GB200.

**Potential risks**  
The assertion waits == [(torch.cuda.current_stream(), modules[0]._prefetch_done)] is strict: any additional wait_event call during embed (e.g., from unrelated framework internals or future code paths) would break the test. It also couples the test to the internal _prefetch_done attribute and to wait_event being the sole synchronization mechanism, so refactors to the prefetch implementation could require test updates. Patching a torch.cuda.Stream method is somewhat invasive but is scoped and forwards to the original.

**Key insights**  
The original test conflated 'second lookup still running' with 'consumer waited for it', which is not equivalent and is timing-sensitive. Observing the actual wait_event dependency is a more robust way to assert per-layer stream synchronization. This is a test-only change with no production behavior impact, but it is classified SUBSTANTIAL because it alters test logic and coverage semantics rather than just formatting or comments.

## 63. [[Bugfix][MiniMax-M3] Don't emit MXFP8 from the input norm under sequence parallelism](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65496)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65496
- **Author:** [xsxszab](https://github.com/xsxszab)
- **Merged By:** [zyongye](https://github.com/zyongye)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 13:51:19
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65496/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/models/minimax_m3/nvidia/model.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
In MiniMax-M3's NVIDIA model, forward_input_norm now disables MXFP8 output from the input norm when sequence parallelism is enabled, by gating the mxfp8 flag on not self.use_sequence_parallel. This fixes a startup crash (AttributeError on QuantizedActivation.contiguous) where SP all-gathers the norm output before attention and cannot handle the QuantizedActivation dataclass; qkv_proj now quantizes after the gather instead.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes only when SP is active: the fused norm+MXFP8-quant path is skipped and BF16 is all-gathered, matching the dense MLP's existing SP data flow. With SP off the condition evaluates identically to before, so no regression is expected there. The change is small and localized to one file, but it disables a fusion under SP, which may slightly increase all-gather bytes and add a separate quantization step.

**Potential risks**  
The fix relies on the assumption that only the plain norm branch is reachable under SP (MegaMoE never defers finalize, SP layers never defer FFN all-reduce), which is not enforced by tests. No unit test is added because the failure requires 4 SM100 GPUs and the M3.1 checkpoint, so CI cannot catch regressions in this path. The performance tradeoff of gathering BF16 instead of MXFP8 data plus scales is acknowledged but unmeasured here.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between the norm's MXFP8 output assumption (direct norm-to-qkv_proj flow) and SP's intervening all-gather that only supports plain tensors. The minimal gating fix is reasonable and consistent with how the dense MLP handles SP. A follow-up could gather MXFP8 data and unswizzled scales and swizzle after the gather to halve all-gather bytes, but that requires norm-kernel changes and is correctly deferred.

## 64. [[ROCm][Perf] Fix Kimi-K2.6 int4 MoE prefill regression on Triton 3.8 (gfx950 tuned config)](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62565)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #62565
- **Author:** [afriedri](https://github.com/afriedri)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 13:32:08
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62565/files) (4):**
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/configs/E=384,N=256,device_name=AMD_Instinct_MI350X,dtype=int4_w4a16.json`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/configs/E=384,N=256,device_name=AMD_Instinct_MI350_OAM,dtype=int4_w4a16.json`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/configs/E=384,N=256,device_name=AMD_Instinct_MI355X,dtype=int4_w4a16.json`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/configs/E=384,N=256,device_name=AMD_Instinct_MI355_OAM,dtype=int4_w4a16.json`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Updates the "8192" tuned-config entry in four identical E=384,N=256 int4_w4a16 JSON files for MI350/MI355, changing BLOCK_SIZE_N from 256 to 128 and num_stages from 2 to 1. This works around a Triton 3.7.1 to 3.8.0 codegen regression in fused_moe_kernel_gptq_awq that made the previous 256x256x32 tile 3-8x slower, causing a ~150% TTFT regression for Kimi-K2.6 int4 MoE prefill at c32.

**Technical impact**  
Only Kimi-K2-family int4 W4A16 checkpoints at TP8 on MI350/MI355 load these files, and only batches of roughly 6k+ tokens hit the "8192" entry, so the blast radius is narrow. Kernel benchmarks show the new tile at 6129 us versus 44180 us for the current config, and serving TTFT at c32 drops from 1981 to 829 ms with modest TPOT/E2EL/tok-s improvements.

**Potential risks**  
The change is tuned specifically for Triton 3.8.0; on other Triton versions or future codegen fixes the new tile may no longer be optimal, and there is no version gating. The c1 TPOT delta is attributed to run-to-run noise, so the reported numbers carry some measurement variance. Files also lack a trailing newline, a minor hygiene issue.

**Key insights**  
This is a targeted config-only workaround for an upstream Triton codegen regression rather than a code fix, so it should be revisited once Triton 3.8 is patched. The evidence is strong: isolated kernel microbenchmarks plus end-to-end serving results both confirm the improvement, and the change is scoped to a single batch-size entry in device-specific configs.

## 65. [[Bugfix][Tool Parser] Keep raw text for string parameters with union/enum/$ref schemas (Muse, MiniCPM5, Poolside)](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64518)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64518
- **Author:** [simon-veitner-redhat](https://github.com/simon-veitner-redhat)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 13:04:29
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64518/files) (10):**
  - `rust/src/parser/src/schema.rs`
  - `rust/src/parser/src/unified/muse.rs`
  - `tests/tool_parsers/test_minicpm5xml_tool_parser.py`
  - `tests/tool_parsers/test_poolside_v1_tool_parser.py`
  - `tests/tool_parsers/test_schema_types.py`
  - `tests/tool_use/test_muse.py`
  - `vllm/tool_parsers/minicpm5xml_tool_parser.py`
  - `vllm/tool_parsers/muse_tool_parser.py`
  - `vllm/tool_parsers/poolside_v1_tool_parser.py`
  - `vllm/tool_parsers/schema_types.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a bug where the Muse, MiniCPM5, and Poolside V1 tool parsers only preserved raw text for parameters whose schema was exactly {"type": "string"}, causing Optional[str] values like "1.10" or "1984" to be wrongly decoded to 1.1 or 1984. A new shared module vllm/tool_parsers/schema_types.py computes the JSON types each parameter admits (type, const, enum, local $ref, anyOf/oneOf, allOf, additionalProperties) and convert_value keeps raw text when a string is allowed unless the value decodes to another admitted type. The Rust Muse parser gets an equivalent fix via SchemaRoot::options, and tests were added across Python and Rust.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a reusable schema-type analysis layer used by three Python parsers and the Rust Muse parser, with a ParameterTypesCache to avoid recomputing per request (the renderer builds a throwaway parser per request). Behavior changes for schemas that admit strings through unions, enums, refs, or allOf, while plain string, non-string, and untyped parameters are unchanged. Rust and Python implementations intentionally diverge on a few edge cases (nested allOf in anyOf, deep $ref chains, OpenAPI nullable, NaN/Infinity handling).

**Potential risks**  
The new schema walk is recursive and handles $ref cycles via memoization, but complex or adversarial schemas could still be costly; the test includes a 2**60-path case to guard this. Rust/Python divergence on edge-case schemas and the pre-existing split where Rust Glimmer gets new rules while Python Glimmer always decodes could cause inconsistent behavior across frontends. Poolside V1 streaming now buffers nullable-string arguments until the closing tag, changing delta timing. The cache keys on tools list identity, so callers passing fresh list objects per call would lose caching benefits.

**Key insights**  
The fix is well-scoped: it centralizes schema-type logic rather than duplicating per-parser checks, preserves each parser's own decoder, and explicitly leaves extract_types_from_schema/ParserEngine coercion untouched (deferred to #63385). Validation is thorough, covering unit tests, streaming and non-streaming paths, Rust tests, and end-to-end serving on multiple models and both frontends, with documented limitations and Rust/Python differences.

---

## 66. [[Quantization] Block Hadamard input rotation for linear layers: online `mxfp4_hadamard32` and Quark online-rotation checkpoints](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62750)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #62750
- **Author:** [mjkvaak-amd](https://github.com/mjkvaak-amd)
- **Merged By:** [mgoin](https://github.com/mgoin)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 12:47:40
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62750/files) (12):**
  - `docs/features/quantization/online.md`
  - `tests/quantization/test_input_hadamard.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_quantization_config_args.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_quark.py`
  - `tools/pre_commit/test_tethering_allowlist.txt`
  - `vllm/config/quantization.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/base_config.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/input_hadamard.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/online/base.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/quark/quark.py`
  - `vllm/platforms/rocm.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds InputHadamardLinearMethod, a format-agnostic wrapper that rotates linear-layer inputs (and optionally weights) by an orthonormal block Hadamard matrix before the wrapped quantization method runs, to reduce block-scaled quantization error. It is wired into two producers: an online path via a new QuantSpec.hadamard_size field and the mxfp4_hadamard32 shorthand, and a Quark path that detects and validates int8 input_rotation tensors in MXFP4 checkpoints exported with online rotation. Supporting changes touch config validation, the ROCm quantization allowlist, docs, and tests.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a new quantization wrapper class, a new QuantSpec field with power-of-two validation, a new shorthand in the quantization method list, and Quark config discovery of input_rotation tensors from safetensors metadata. The wrapper preserves v1 weight loaders for wrapped methods not in WEIGHT_LOADER_V2_SUPPORTED, guards against double weight rotation via the _already_called_process_weights_after_loading flag, and raises NotImplementedError for unvalidated paths (non-MXFP4 online methods, MoE, requantization, non-Sylvester rotations). The rotation is an fp32 matmul in PyTorch, adding serving overhead (~6-12% throughput loss per the PR's measurements) until the follow-up kernel fusion lands.

**Potential risks**  
The PyTorch rotation adds measurable throughput cost on rotated layers, and the PR notes CI gaps: MX paths are only validated on MI355X outside CI, and the CUDA path is untested beyond CPU tests. The Quark wikitext perplexity shifted by 0.09 versus an older nightly with unexplained cause, which is a small but unverified numerical discrepancy. The metadata-based detection of input_rotation depends on safetensors metadata availability and correct hf_to_vllm_mapper application, and the wrapper's reliance on internal flags like _already_called_process_weights_after_loading couples it to online method internals. Compile/fusion passes must preserve the rotation op, which could be fragile if future fusions assume RMSNorm feeds directly into activation quant.

**Key insights**  
The design deliberately avoids reusing compressed-tensors' HadamardTransform because it lacks TP>1 support, requires checkpoint-loaded matrices, and depends on a CUDA-only kernel, so a small format-agnostic wrapper is a reasonable trade-off. The PR is well-scoped with explicit NotImplementedError guards and TODOs for unvalidated paths, and includes meaningful tests (orthonormality, single weight rotation, checkpoint rotation validation, layerwise reload, and MXFP4 kernel equivalence). The main open concerns are the serving overhead (addressed by follow-up #63272) and the limited hardware/CI coverage for the MX and CUDA paths.

---

## 67. [[Bugfix][Spec Decode] Muse Spark DSpark: initialize _context_kv_proj so the draft loads](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65125)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65125
- **Author:** [fanxingran](https://github.com/fanxingran)
- **Merged By:** [mgoin](https://github.com/mgoin)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 12:41:32
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65125/files) (2):**
  - `tests/transformers_utils/test_muse_spark_config.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/common/dspark.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a startup regression from #64357 where Muse Spark DSpark draft loading raised AttributeError on _context_kv_proj. MuseSparkDSparkModel skips the parent DFlashQwen3Model.__init__ but inherits its load_weights, which now reads self._context_kv_proj, so the fix explicitly sets self._context_kv_proj = None in __init__ to preserve pre-regression behavior since Muse projects context K/V via _project_muse_context_kv. A new unit test builds the real draft model and exercises load_weights.

**Technical impact**  
Restores Muse Spark + DSpark startup on both CUDA and ROCm with acceptance length (3.900) matching pre-regression (3.913) and the model card (3.94). The change is a minimal, targeted attribute initialization that avoids touching the parent class or other DFlashQwen3Model subclasses (Laguna DFlash and Gemma4 DSpark never reach the inherited load_weights). The added test provides real coverage of the draft weight-loading path that CI previously missed.

**Potential risks**  
The new test lives in tests/transformers_utils, which is currently not run by any Buildkite step (commented out in .buildkite/test_areas/misc.yaml), so the regression guard is only exercised locally until that step is re-enabled. Setting _context_kv_proj to None relies on Muse never invoking the parent's MXFP8 projection path; any future refactor that routes Muse through that path would silently break. Acceptance delta vs pre-regression is within run-to-run noise (~0.05) but not exactly identical.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is an inheritance mismatch: MuseSparkDSparkModel bypasses the parent __init__ but inherits load_weights, so any attribute the parent now initializes in __init__ must be mirrored. CI missed this because the Muse DSpark registry entry is is_available_online=False and the init test uses dummy weights, so the real load_weights path was never exercised. Re-enabling the transformers_utils Buildkite step would close the coverage gap.

## 68. [[Bugfix][Frontend] Support min_tokens in the Anthropic Messages API](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65459)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65459
- **Author:** [nliu365](https://github.com/nliu365)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 12:40:27
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65459/files) (3):**
  - `tests/entrypoints/anthropic/test_messages.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/anthropic/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/anthropic/serving.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a min_tokens field (default 0) to AnthropicMessagesRequest and forwards it into the ChatCompletionRequest built by AnthropicServingMessages._build_base_request, so /v1/messages now honors min_tokens like Chat Completions. A regression test verifies that a stop sequence ends the response early without min_tokens but is delayed until at least 16 tokens when min_tokens=16.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change limited to the Anthropic Messages endpoint: min_tokens now reaches SamplingParams, so EOS and stop sequences cannot terminate generation before the configured minimum. Range validation is delegated to existing SamplingParams checks (0 <= min_tokens <= max_tokens), avoiding duplicated validation logic. No changes to core engine or Chat Completions paths.

**Potential risks**  
min_tokens is a non-standard vLLM extension not in Anthropic's spec, so SDK users must pass it via extra_body; this could confuse spec-conformant clients. The new test depends on a specific model (Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B) and greedy decoding, so it may be sensitive to model or sampling changes. P/D proxies are claimed to already handle min_tokens, but that behavior is not exercised by this PR's tests.

**Key insights**  
The fix is small and targeted, reusing existing SamplingParams validation rather than adding a second range check. Test coverage is meaningful because it demonstrates the failure mode (early stop) and the corrected behavior. The PR is well-scoped and explicitly excludes the Responses API counterpart, which is handled separately.

## 69. [[Perf][Mamba] Use the shared checkpoint exporter for Mamba2 and drop ssd_checkpoint](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63742)

  - `tests/kernels/mamba/test_mamba2_checkpoint_state.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/mamba/checkpoint.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/mamba/mamba_mixer2.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/mamba/ops/ssd_checkpoint.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Mamba2 prefill checkpoint storage is migrated from its dedicated ssd_checkpoint.py Triton kernel (deleted, -118 lines) to the shared MambaPrefillCheckpointExporter used by GDN/KDA, consolidating three model families onto one checkpoint-store kernel. The shared kernel's block size is raised from 256 to 512, its eight strides become runtime arguments instead of tl.constexpr, and the conv-window section is guarded so programs beyond the conv columns skip it. export() also gains two host-side asserts (offsets cover the conv window, recurrent_rows sized per checkpoint row).

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change in the shared kernel affects all callers (GDN, KDA, Mamba2, ROCm Kimi-K3 KDA, Qwen4Exp PLE), not just Mamba2. Mamba2 now passes x.transpose(0, 1) as raw_qkv and uses recurrent_rows for chunk-state selection. The shared kernel skips null destination blocks, fixing a latent bug where the removed kernel wrote skipped prefill checkpoints into block 0. Benchmarks show Mamba2 faster in 39/48 cases and GDN/KDA 13-37% faster at 4+ rows on TP1/2, but 25 of 96 GDN/KDA cases are slower (up to 1.14x, +15 us/step), mostly TP4/8 at 1-2 rows.

**Potential risks**  
The 512 block size and conv guard are global to the shared kernel, so non-Mamba2 callers inherit them; Qwen4Exp PLE is 2-6% slower and some GDN/KDA TP4/8 low-row cases regress. Validation is B200-only microbenchmarks with no end-to-end serving throughput measurement, and the ROCm Kimi-K3 KDA path (gfx950) was not run. The new asserts are defensive and cannot fire today, but if a future caller violates them it would crash rather than silently misbehave. The null-block skip changes behavior for prompts longer than the token budget spanning multiple Mamba blocks; no reader of block 0 was found, but that analysis is by inspection. PR #62232 (Mamba2 checkpoints on CPU) adds a path to the deleted store_prefill_checkpoint, so whichever lands second must reconcile.

**Key insights**  
The swap is well-justified: after #60050 added HAS_RECURRENT_ROWS the two kernels were near-duplicates, so consolidation removes maintenance burden and fixes a real null-block write bug. The PR is unusually thorough in its benchmarking, ablating each kernel change (block size, runtime strides, conv guard) separately and reporting regressions honestly. The main review concern is that a Mamba2-motivated kernel tuning (512 blocks, conv guard) is applied to all callers, and the GDN/KDA regressions at TP4/8 with 1-2 rows are acknowledged but not fully resolved. The two new asserts are cheap and safe but should be documented as invariants rather than load-bearing checks.

---

## 70. [[Bugfix][DSv4] Skip empty compressed indexer prefill chunks](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65046)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65046
- **Author:** [hlin99](https://github.com/hlin99)
- **Merged By:** [zyongye](https://github.com/zyongye)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 12:21:29
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65046/files) (2):**
  - `tests/model_executor/layers/test_mla_short_prefill_indexer.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/sparse_attn_indexer.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The sparse_attn_indexer kernel previously decided whether prefill work existed by checking num_prefills > 0, which could be true even when the compressed indexer produced no prefill chunks (all prefills shorter than the compression ratio). That led to workspace setup calling max() on an empty chunk list and raising ValueError. The fix derives has_prefill from the actual prefill metadata chunks, and the dispatch tests were updated to construct real nonempty chunk metadata so they still exercise the scoring path.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes in the indexer dispatch: requests with prefills but zero compressed KV chunks now skip prefill workspace setup and scoring/top-k selection entirely, avoiding a crash. The test helper now builds DeepseekV32IndexerPrefillChunkMetadata/DeepseekV32IndexerPrefillMetadata instead of a SimpleNamespace with an empty chunk list, making the tests exercise realistic metadata and the nonempty-chunk code path.

**Potential risks**  
The new has_prefill condition assumes prefill_metadata.chunks is the authoritative signal; if any other code path populates prefill work without chunks, that work would be silently skipped. The test chunk construction uses synthetic values (e.g., cu_seqlen_ke=ones, token_to_seq derived by integer division) that may not reflect real layouts, so coverage of edge cases like multiple chunks or uneven token distribution is limited. Only two files changed, so broader interactions with other consumers of num_prefills were not verified from the diff alone.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between the coarse num_prefills counter and the finer-grained chunk metadata that actually drives workspace sizing; the fix aligns the dispatch decision with the data it consumes. The test change is important because the old empty-chunk stub would have masked the bug and now would have bypassed the scoring sentinel. Reviewers should confirm no other call sites rely on num_prefills to gate prefill logic and that empty-chunk prefills are correctly treated as no-ops downstream.

## 71. [[Bugfix][HiSparse] Release temporary device KV bindings after graph profiling](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64989)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64989
- **Author:** [zhenwenqi2024](https://github.com/zhenwenqi2024)
- **Merged By:** [MatthewBonanni](https://github.com/MatthewBonanni)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 12:19:29
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64989/files) (7):**
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_sparse_mla_backends.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_attn_utils.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_utils.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/hisparse/worker.py`
  - `vllm/v1/hisparse/binding.py`
  - `vllm/v1/hisparse/runtime.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/cudagraph_utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR fixes a HiSparse memory leak where temporary device KV bindings created during CUDA-graph memory profiling were retained by live attention handles and runtimes after profiling teardown, keeping the throwaway KV slab and per-binding tables alive. It introduces a shared _reset_binding_state() on both HiSparseRuntime and HiSparseCacheHandle, adds unbind_cache() methods, replaces release_hisparse_profiling_cache with unbind_hisparse_caches, and removes the runtimes argument from release_pinned_state so host-pool shutdown is separated from per-binding detachment. It also fixes an adjacent partial-initialization failure path in init_hisparse_kv_cache and updates connector shutdown/error paths to detach handles.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change in HiSparse lifecycle management: profiling cleanup now detaches device views and metadata while preserving the engine-scoped registered host pool for the real KV cache, and rebinding resets stale hot-cache state. The refactor centralizes binding-state reset logic, removes redundant deduplication and bind-time hot-state resets, and changes the release_pinned_state signature, requiring callers in the connector, binding, and tests to be updated. Persistent index-group/draft membership, streams, and shared workspaces are intentionally retained.

**Potential risks**  
The PR explicitly states no fresh CUDA/model validation was run for the latest follow-up revision, and full-package pytest collection was blocked by a missing tblib dependency, so regressions in device paths or other tests may be undetected. The signature change to release_pinned_state and the new unbind ordering in connector shutdown/error paths could affect other callers or shutdown semantics if any path still assumes runtimes are cleared by release_pinned_state. NPU and ROCm hardware were not validated, and the standalone startup wrapper hit a pidfd EINVAL during cleanup, leaving some runtime teardown behavior unverified.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is that profiling teardown cleared runner caches but not handle-owned PagedCacheView and Runtime.hot references, so the temporary device backing survived; separating per-binding detachment from host-pool shutdown is the correct fix. The added regression test using weakrefs and torch.accelerator memory accounting directly targets the leak, and the migrated host-pool reuse test now exercises real runtime/handle/pool objects rather than fakes. Reviewers should note the validation gap for the latest revision and confirm all release_pinned_state callers and shutdown ordering are consistent.

## 73. [[HiSparse] Support the extensible KV cache](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65214)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65214
- **Author:** [MatthewBonanni](https://github.com/MatthewBonanni)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 11:50:18
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65214/files) (13):**
  - `tests/v1/core/test_kv_cache_planning.py`
  - `tests/v1/e2e/general/test_hisparse.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_attn_utils.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_gpu_warmup_blocks.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_mixed_warmup_gate.py`
  - `vllm/config/vllm.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/warmup/flashinfer_sparse_mla_warmup.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/kv_cache_planning.py`
  - `vllm/v1/hisparse/binding.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/extensible_kv_cache.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/attn_utils.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/model_runner.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/warmup.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Removes the gate that excluded HiSparse from the extensible KV cache and adapts the extensible-cache machinery to coexist with HiSparse's separate host pool. Host-resident tensors are skipped by ExtensibleKVCache, committed_views/committed_kv_cache_tensors, and shrink_kv_cache_configs; the post-extend cache dict is now preserved and passed to connectors; committed_views gives raw-buffer layers the committed prefix; and a new rebind_hisparse_device_caches rebinds resident/hot views over the committed blocks. Two warmup crashes exposed by removing the gate are fixed: the FlashInfer HiSparse autotune now commits the hot blocks it reads, and warmup reservations are clamped to the spec's block-table row length (Mamba exempt).

**Technical impact**  
HiSparse now runs on the extensible KV cache by default, growing GPU+host KV capacity (~16% at default flags) with throughput and GSM8K accuracy matching main. The runner retains kv_caches_by_layer so connectors see the same committed views as the fixed-size path, and CUDA graphs captured before extend remain valid because the base address does not move. NIXL P/D and Mooncake store connectors now work with HiSparse, and the warmup clamp bounds fixed-size hot block-table rows.

**Potential risks**  
The NIXL/Mooncake registration has no permanent CI test (no HiSparse P/D job), and the FlashInfer autotune fix is only guarded on SM100 since H200 CI does not exercise that path. Sleep/wake with HiSparse on the extensible cache is explicitly not covered. The warmup row-length clamp changes reservation behavior for all specs, so any spec whose max_num_blocks_per_req diverges from actual manager behavior could under-reserve; Mamba is special-cased but other hybrid specs are not obviously audited. The rebind path mutates kv_caches in place and relies on ordering/assertions, which is fragile if layouts change.

**Key insights**  
The core design choice is to keep HiSparse's host pool entirely outside the extensible paths via the existing generic host_resident flag rather than adding feature-owned branches, which keeps the change small but couples extensible-cache code to HiSparse semantics (rebind call, raw-buffer prefix handling). Preserving kv_caches_by_layer and swapping in committed views is what makes connectors and the fixed/extensible paths agree, and the new test asserting identical caches and greedy outputs against a fixed-size cache of the same block count is the strongest guard. The two warmup crashes were latent bugs only masked by the gate, so their fixes are independently valuable.

---

The PR removes the DEP4 c96 and c128 benchmark points from the gb300_agentx_dep4 preset in the MiniMax-M3.1-Flash recipe, keeping only c64, because those two points exceed the 10 s TTFT limit in InferenceX#4662 and are no longer run. It also rewords the DEP4 runbook for a single point and bumps the source revision for all three presets (TP4, DEP4, TP2) to the current InferenceX head.

**Technical impact**  
The preset now exposes a single DEP4 concurrency point (c64) instead of three, changing the benchmark matrix and any downstream tooling that enumerates or compares points. The revision pin update affects provenance tracking for all three presets even though only DEP4 points changed, so TP4 and TP2 now reference a newer InferenceX commit.

Validation was limited to YAML parsing; validate-source and npm run test:presets were not run, so preset schema or reference integrity issues could slip through. Pinning TP4 and TP2 to a new revision without changing their points could introduce mismatches if the upstream commit altered those configurations. Removing points may break consumers expecting c96/c128 to exist.

**Key insights**  
The change is small in diff size but behaviorally meaningful since it alters the benchmark point set, so it is not trivial despite the Doc title. The revision bump across all presets is a side effect worth verifying against upstream InferenceX#4662. Running the preset validation tests before merge would reduce risk.

## 76. [[Bugfix] Keep the extensible tensor's PyCapsule_New prototype private](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65457)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65457
- **Author:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged By:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 11:33:21
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65457/files) (2):**
  - `tests/utils_/test_extensible_tensor.py`
  - `vllm/utils/extensible_tensor.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The extensible tensor DLPack helper previously mutated the process-wide ctypes.pythonapi.PyCapsule_New function object by setting its restype and argtypes. Because that object is shared global mutable state, FlashInfer's MNNVL helpers overwriting restype to c_void_p caused PyCapsule_New to return an int address instead of a capsule, breaking torch.from_dlpack on ExtensibleTensor views. The fix binds a private PYFUNCTYPE prototype to the same symbol so the helper is immune to external mutation, and adds a regression test that simulates FlashInfer's restype change in a spawned process.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior is intended to be identical for the extensible tensor path: the same C symbol is invoked with the same signature, but through a function object whose restype/argtypes are not shared with ctypes.pythonapi. This removes a cross-library global-state coupling and makes ExtensibleTensor views robust to third-party libraries that reconfigure PyCapsule_New. The change is localized to vllm/utils/extensible_tensor.py plus a test.

**Potential risks**  
The private PYFUNCTYPE object is created at import time and relies on ctypes.pythonapi exposing the PyCapsule_New symbol, which is standard but could theoretically vary across Python builds. The regression test mutates a global in a spawned process, so isolation depends on the spawn fixture working correctly; if that isolation regresses, other tests could be affected. The fix addresses only PyCapsule_New, not other shared ctypes.pythonapi functions that could be similarly mutated elsewhere.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is global mutable state on a shared ctypes _FuncPtr, where the last writer wins across libraries in the same process. Using PYFUNCTYPE to create a separate function object bound to the same symbol is a clean, minimal way to decouple from that shared state. The added test correctly reproduces the FlashInfer scenario and validates the fix in an isolated process, and the PR clearly documents root cause, validation, and non-duplication.

## 78. [[Bugfix][Mistral] Carry token ids in stream state for byte-fallback tokens](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64743)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64743
- **Author:** [cjackal](https://github.com/cjackal)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 11:28:12
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64743/files) (4):**
  - `docs/serving/online_serving/derenderer.md`
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/derender/test_derender_stream.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/renderers/online_derenderer.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Streaming derender state previously carried decoded token strings (prev_tokens) in DerenderStreamState, which is JSON-serialized between chunks. MistralTokenizer.convert_ids_to_tokens returns raw bytes for byte-fallback tokens, and incomplete UTF-8 pieces cannot survive JSON transport, corrupting the decode window. This PR replaces prev_tokens with prev_token_ids (NonNegativeInt list), reconverts ids to tokens on each call, filters out-of-vocab ids and skipped special tokens, and seeds the window from the prompt tail by id. It also makes the parser path for MistralTokenizer honor the request's skip_special_tokens, matching serving behavior after #65118.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the public stream_state schema (prev_tokens -> prev_token_ids), so any client persisting or replaying old stream_state payloads will break or need migration. Detokenization now performs an extra convert_ids_to_tokens call per chunk over the bounded window, which is O(window) and should be negligible. The Mistral-specific skip_special_tokens branch in _decode_params alters parser-path behavior for Tekken tokenizers, aligning derender with serving. Tests are updated and new byte-fallback round-trip and Mistral decode-param tests are added.

**Potential risks**  
The stream_state field rename is a breaking wire-format change for existing clients; no backward-compatibility shim or versioning is shown. The new test fixture loads a real Mistral tokenizer (mistralai/Pixtral-12B-2409) via network, which may be flaky or slow in CI. The is_mistral_tokenizer special-case in _decode_params adds tokenizer-specific branching that could diverge from serving if serving logic changes. Filtering ids by reconverting each prompt-tail id is a small per-seed cost and assumes convert_ids_to_tokens is consistent with the delta path.

**Key insights**  
Carrying ids instead of decoded strings in client-side stream state is the correct fix for byte-fallback tokenizers, since ids are JSON-safe and lossless while partial UTF-8 byte pieces are not. The bounded-window invariant is preserved by trimming and rebasing both prev_token_ids and prev_tokens in lockstep. The PR is well-scoped to derender endpoints and includes targeted regression tests, but the schema change and network-dependent fixture warrant attention before merge.

---

## 79. [[Frontend] Add granite_toolcall_parser for Granite 4.2 tool calling](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65216)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65216
- **Author:** [yousafshah](https://github.com/yousafshah)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 11:26:01
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65216/files) (6):**
  - `docs/features/tool_calling.md`
  - `tests/tool_parsers/test_granite_toolcall_parser.py`
  - `vllm/parser/engine/registered_adapters.py`
  - `vllm/parser/granite_toolcall.py`
  - `vllm/tool_parsers/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/tool_parsers/granite_toolcall_parser.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new granite_toolcall_parser tool-call parser for Granite 4.2, which emits tool calls in an XML format. The parser is a thin subclass of NemotronV3Parser (not GraniteThinkingParser, despite the PR description claiming otherwise) to reuse the shared grammar, argument converter, and streaming logic while avoiding reasoning-side content stripping. It is registered in the tool parser registry, wired into the adapter generation, documented in tool_calling.md, and covered by a new parity test suite.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a new public parser key (granite_toolcall_parser) usable via --tool-call-parser, plus a new adapter pair generated from GraniteToolCallParser in registered_adapters.py. The structural_tag_model is set to qwen_3_coder for strict tool-calling enforcement. No existing parser behavior is modified, so the change is additive and low-blast-radius, but it does expand the parser registry and adapter surface.

**Potential risks**  
The PR description states the parser subclasses GraniteThinkingParser, but the actual code subclasses NemotronV3Parser; this documentation/code mismatch could confuse reviewers and future maintainers. The parser depends on NemotronV3Parser's grammar matching Granite 4.2 output exactly, so any divergence in the shared engine could silently break Granite 4.2 tool calls. Tests require fetching the granite-4.2-8b tokenizer from Hugging Face, which may make CI flaky or network-dependent. The structural_tag_model mapping to qwen_3_coder is an implicit coupling that could drift.

**Key insights**  
The design correctly separates reasoning-side content cleanup (leading newline stripping) from tool-call extraction by not inheriting GraniteThinkingParser, and the test suite explicitly guards this regression. The parity testing against qwen3_coder across streaming modes, thinking modes, and malformed inputs is thorough. The main actionable item is reconciling the PR description with the actual base class, and confirming the NemotronV3 grammar is a stable contract for Granite 4.2.

## 80. [[CI] Pin existing dependency hashes and enforce immutable references](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64241)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64241
- **Author:** [jperezdealgaba](https://github.com/jperezdealgaba)
- **Merged By:** [khluu](https://github.com/khluu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 11:23:30
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64241/files) (19):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/misc.yaml`
  - `.github/workflows/recipes.yml`
  - `.pre-commit-config.yaml`
  - `.readthedocs.yaml`
  - `docker/Dockerfile`
  - `docker/Dockerfile.cpu`
  - `docker/Dockerfile.rocm`
  - `docker/Dockerfile.rocm_gfx1250`
  - `docs/contributing/README.md`
  - `docs/contributing/ci/dependency_hashes.md`
  - `docs/mkdocs/gen_files/generate_argparse.py`
  - `requirements/docs.txt`
  - `requirements/test/cpu.txt`
  - `requirements/test/cuda.txt`
  - `requirements/test/rocm.txt`
  - `requirements/test/xpu.txt`
  - `tests/tools/test_dependency_pins.py`
  - `tools/pre_commit/check_github_actions_pins.py`
  - `tools/use_existing_torch.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR initializes SHA-256 archive hash pinning across the five compiled requirement locks (docs, test cpu/cuda/rocm/xpu) without changing any package versions, adds --generate-hashes to the pre-commit compile hooks, and adds a new check_github_actions_pins.py hook that rejects mutable tags/branches/short SHAs in external Actions and reusable workflows. It also adapts hashed-requirement consumers: use_existing_torch.py now removes complete continued requirement blocks, the CPU Dockerfile joins continuations before filtering, both ROCm Dockerfiles extract the full continued fastsafetensors requirement and rebuild with --require-hashes, CUDA nightly/arm64 recompiles emit hashes, and Read the Docs installs with --require-hashes. Supporting changes include a docs version-parser fix, new regression tests wired into Buildkite, and contributor documentation.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes in CI and container builds: installs now verify archive hashes where supplied, the docs install globally requires hashes, and workflows must use immutable 40-char commit SHAs or image digests. The line-oriented parsing fixes prevent orphaned hash lines from being misattributed to other packages and avoid invalid quoted pip requirements from trailing backslashes. The new test suite exercises the actual Dockerfile extraction/install commands against a local sdist, validating both successful and failing hash verification.

**Potential risks**  
Hash coverage is partial: NCCL overrides, handwritten requirements, direct Dockerfile installs, and source build dependencies remain unhashed, so the security guarantee is uneven and future NCCL_VERSION bumps will silently drop hash verification. The new Actions pin check could break workflows if Dependabot or contributors introduce tags, and the pinned recipes reusable workflow now requires manual/Dependabot refresh. Full Linux CPU/GPU container builds and hosted CI/docs runs are still pending, so integration regressions in real images are not yet ruled out. The large generated lock diffs are hard to review manually.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-scoped as a prerequisite for the automated updater in #59667 and includes targeted regression tests for the tricky continuation-line cases (Torch removal, ROCm fastsafetensors, docs version parsing). The main residual concern is that hash enforcement is not global, and the documentation explicitly acknowledges this limitation, which is good practice. Merge should wait for the pending full container and hosted CI validation before relying on the pinning guarantees.

## 81. [[Quantization] Remove QuTLASS and fp_quant, run QuIP NVFP4 on Humming](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65230)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65230
- **Author:** [mgoin](https://github.com/mgoin)
- **Merged By:** [mgoin](https://github.com/mgoin)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 11:12:00
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65230/files) (24):**
  - `.buildkite/hardware_tests/ascend_npu.yaml`
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/kernels.yaml`
  - `CMakeLists.txt`
  - `benchmarks/kernels/benchmark_mxfp4_qutlass.py`
  - `benchmarks/kernels/benchmark_nvfp4_qutlass.py`
  - `cmake/external_projects/qutlass.cmake`
  - `csrc/qutlass_registration.cpp`
  - `docs/features/quantization/README.md`
  - `setup.py`
  - `tests/kernels/quantization/test_mxfp4_qutlass.py`
  - `tests/kernels/quantization/test_nvfp4_qutlass.py`
  - `tests/models/quantization/test_nvfp4.py`
  - `vllm/_custom_ops.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/nvfp4/humming.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/linear.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/compressed_tensors/transform/linear.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/compressed_tensors/transform/schemes/linear_humming_nvfp4.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/compressed_tensors/transform/schemes/linear_qutlass_nvfp4.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/fp_quant.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/qutlass_utils.py`
  - `vllm/platforms/cuda.py`
  - `vllm/v1/metrics/perf.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Removes the QuTLASS backend (CMake external project, _qutlass_C extension, custom ops, benchmarks, tests, qutlass_utils) and the deprecated fp_quant quantization method (~1800 lines deleted), and replaces the QuTLASS-based NVFP4 Hadamard transform path with a new HummingHadamardNvFp4LinearKernel plus a HummingNvFP4HadamardLinearMethod scheme. The motivation is that QuIP NVFP4 checkpoints crash on SM100+ at main (QuantizedActivation passed to transforms, missing backward_hadamard_matrix registration) and QuTLASS wheels only ship sm_100 code, so the fix routes supported Hadamard+W4A4 cases to Humming and fixes the generic transform path to receive unquantized activations.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral routing change: compressed-tensors W4A4 NVFP4 checkpoints with a Sylvester Hadamard input transform (head_dim 16/32/64/128), no output transform, and backend auto/humming now use a new Humming kernel that fuses the Hadamard into input quantization and honors the checkpoint's FP4 activation dtype without A16 fallback. A new layer.requires_unquantized_input flag is set whenever input transforms exist, fixing the generic transform path. Build system, setup.py precompiled artifacts, CUDA platform imports, CI test lists, and the quantization method registry all drop QuTLASS/fp_quant. fp_quant checkpoints no longer load (already broken on main).

**Potential risks**  
Breaking removal of fp_quant and QuTLASS with no migration shim beyond a docs note; any external user relying on those paths loses support. New Humming path is only validated on B300/SM103 with TP=1; SM120 and TP>1 are explicitly untested, and HadamardTransform still raises NotImplementedError for TP>1. Accuracy is a statistical tie with QuTLASS on GSM8K but lower in every prototype row, and A16 fallback removal means checkpoints declaring FP4 activations cannot silently degrade. Non-Sylvester dense transforms now take the slower generic path, which was not benchmarked. The new routing test mocks is_supported and _get_linear_backend, so it validates selection logic but not real kernel behavior.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was that NVFP4 layers expose input_quant_key, so producers hand transforms a QuantizedActivation that transform methods cannot handle; the requires_unquantized_input flag addresses this generally, not just for QuIP. The prior QuIP perplexity test was silently disabled (commented-out CI job), which is why the regression went unnoticed; new tests live in test_nvfp4.py whose Blackwell job runs. Humming's block-size 16-256 output matched the dense Sylvester matrix to bf16 rounding, justifying skipping the checkpoint matrix. The PR is well-documented with explicit loss/not-validated sections, but reviewers should scrutinize the SM120/TP>1 gaps and the accuracy deltas before merge.

## 82. [[Perf][HiSparse] Prefetch speculative groups with bounded copy workers](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63801)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63801
- **Author:** [zhenwenqi2024](https://github.com/zhenwenqi2024)
- **Merged By:** [MatthewBonanni](https://github.com/MatthewBonanni)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 10:55:27
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63801/files) (7):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/attention.yaml`
  - `benchmarks/kernels/benchmark_hisparse_gather.py`
  - `csrc/hisparse_kernels.cu`
  - `docs/design/hisparse.md`
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_sparse_mla_backends.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_utils.py`
  - `vllm/v1/hisparse/runtime.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Extends HiSparse's shared-index group prefetch from single-token decode to multi-token speculative verification (including MTP) by adding a new is_hisparse_group_prefetch_batch eligibility predicate and a prefetch_decode_group flag on the cache handle, so follower layers' historical KV is staged on the group copy stream with per-layer completion events. It also bounds the CUDA compact-gather kernel to at most 64 thread blocks by having workers loop over rows, adds a gather benchmark, docs, and native/graph regression tests.

**Technical impact**  
Changes GPU kernel launch geometry (grid.x capped at 64 with an in-kernel row loop) and expands prefetch eligibility to verification batches, altering copy/compute overlap behavior. Profiling shows exposed gather time dropping ~64.5% and 8K throughput +2.35-3.00% with TPOT -4.10 to -5.41%, though total gather duration increases. Prefetch eligibility is deliberately decoupled from resident-write and host-mirror policy, and current verification KV stays resident and is written per-layer.

**Potential risks**  
Bounding the grid trades dense-copy bandwidth for overlap and does not reserve specific SMs, so behavior may regress on workloads with many rows and little compute to overlap. The GSM8K regression shows -0.2274pp accuracy (3 fewer correct) and lower acceptance (85.83% -> 84.94%), which the PR does not attribute or rule out as noise. Several validation items remain outstanding (reduced-model E2E, long-history offload accuracy, concurrency/MTP sweeps), and measurements were not rerun on the rebased main base.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped and reuses existing group ownership, copy streams, and completion events without new backend flags or CPU readback. The PR is unusually candid about measurement limits (single-pass accuracy, synthetic workload, no confidence intervals, diagnostic-only profiling), which is good practice. The main review concerns are the accuracy/acceptance delta and the outstanding E2E and long-history validation before merge.

## 83. [[ROCm] [RDNA] [Perf] Add tuned configs for TRITON_ATTN kernel and swap places of TRITON_ATTN and ROCM_ATTN in priority list](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60418)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #60418
- **Author:** [nemanjaudovic](https://github.com/nemanjaudovic)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 10:43:54
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60418/files) (3):**
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_rocm_attention_backends_selection.py`
  - `vllm/platforms/rocm.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/ops/triton_unified_attention.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
On gfx1201 (RDNA4) and gfx1100 (RDNA3), ROCM_ATTN and TRITON_ATTN are swapped in the attention-backend priority list so TRITON_ATTN becomes the default, with an exception for fp8_e5m2 KV caches. The PR also adds extensively tuned TRITON_ATTN launch configs (BLOCK_M/TILE_SIZE/warps/stages, split-KV segment counts, and a 2D/3D decode threshold) for both GPUs, plus a new on_gfx1201() helper and selection tests. The motivation is measured speedups (roughly 1.1x-20x across regions) and avoiding upstream ROCM_ATTN correctness bugs (#62697).

**Technical impact**  
Changes the default attention backend for two widely used RDNA GPUs, which affects every model served on them, not just performance but also numerics and memory behavior. The kernel file gains substantial heuristic logic (head-size padding lookup, prefill bands, split-KV segment sizing, fp8-query tile constraints, sliding-window 2D switching) that only alters launch parameters, not kernel math. Split-KV segment counts are derived from batch size and model shape so they stay fixed per CUDA graph, and batch-invariant mode is explicitly excluded. Tests pin the new arch helpers to False in existing default-order tests and add coverage for the swap, fp8_e5m2, explicit backend, and non-target archs.

**Potential risks**  
The tuning heuristics are complex and shape-dependent, with documented regressions (about 2.1-2.2% of batches below 0.97x, worst cases down to 0.30x-0.45x in some fp8/sliding-window/high-concurrency clusters). The split-KV scratch reuse via view() and manual row accounting is fragile and could break if the metadata builder's allocation changes. The backend swap is a behavior change for all users on these GPUs, and the fp8_e5m2 carve-out is subtle. gfx1100 kernel tests were still running at PR time, and the PR notes possible rebase conflicts with #56232 and #61646 touching the same function. Some end-to-end benchmark cells are still TODO.

**Key insights**  
This is a high-effort, well-measured performance PR with strong methodology (fp32 reference validation, CUDA-graph timing, A/B against upstream), but it is far from trivial: it changes default backend selection and adds a large heuristic layer to a core kernel launcher. Reviewers should focus on the split-KV scratch/segment logic, the fp8 query tile constraints, the interaction with batch-invariant mode and CUDA graphs, and the documented regression clusters. The correctness argument (upstream ROCM_ATTN produces wrong outputs on some layouts) is a strong additional justification beyond raw speed.

---

## 84. [[Perf][Kernel][DSv4.1] Swap-AB CuTe DSL decode attention for small TP2/TP4 steps](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62633)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #62633
- **Author:** [roborluo](https://github.com/roborluo)
- **Merged By:** [wzhao18](https://github.com/wzhao18)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 10:39:57
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62633/files) (5):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/models_basic.yaml`
  - `benchmarks/kernels/benchmark_dsv41_mega_attn.py`
  - `tests/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/test_swapab_decode.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/flash_mla_mega_attn.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/ops/swapab_decode_attn.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new CuTe DSL swap-AB decode attention kernel (vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/ops/swapab_decode_attn.py, ~1263 lines) and wires it into DeepseekV4MegaAttnAttention so that small decode steps (<=16 tokens) with 16 or 32 live heads on SM10x take it instead of the FlashMLA mega kernel, which pads heads to 64. The motivation is that the mega kernel wastes half to three quarters of its MMA work on padding at TP2/TP4, and the PR claims 1.4-2.15x faster per-layer decode attention. Supporting changes include dispatch logic and JIT warmup registration in flash_mla_mega_attn.py, a new benchmark arm, a new test file, and a CI step rename/extension.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a large, low-level tcgen05 kernel with inline PTX, SMEM/TMEM layout math, thread-block clusters, DSMEM merges, and PDL, plus a new dispatch path and JIT compile-key registration in the attention layer. Behavior changes only for SM10x layers with a compressed cache, 16/32 heads, <=16 decode tokens, non-batch-invariant runs, and a CuTe DSL built with CUDA 13.2+; all other paths (prefill, other TP sizes, other platforms, DSpark widened SWA steps) keep the mega kernel. Adds two JIT compiles per worker at warmup for V4.1-Flash and a new nightly CI test step.

**Potential risks**  
The kernel is complex and maintenance-heavy (inline PTX, cluster/DSMEM synchronization, manual dequant and MXFP8 rounding), so subtle correctness or race bugs are plausible despite tests. Output is not bit-identical to the mega kernel, so results depend on step size, which could complicate debugging or reproducibility; batch-invariant mode is excluded to mitigate this. The dispatch depends on host-side shape checks (token count, SWA list width) and on toolchain detection (has_cutedsl, CUDA 13.2+), so misdetection could silently fall back or, worse, select the kernel with an incompatible build. The 16-token cutoff is tuned to current benchmarks and may regress on different hardware or future shapes. CI coverage is nightly-only and skips without SM10x plus the right CuTe DSL build, so regressions may not be caught per-PR.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is sound: FlashMLA's decode maps query heads to MMA M (>=64), so small-head TP shards waste most MMA work; swapping A/B to put KV slots on M and live heads on N removes that padding. Choosing a separate CuTe DSL kernel rather than modifying FlashMLA avoids a C++ release/bump cycle and keeps the kernel next to its only caller, at the cost of a large Python-level kernel and JIT warmup. The PR is well-documented and tested against an fp64 reference and the mega kernel, but reviewers should scrutinize the inline PTX, cluster synchronization, dispatch conditions, and warmup-key correctness, and confirm the fallback paths are truly unchanged.

## 85. [[Bugfix][Model] Share one RoPE cache across Muse Spark NVIDIA layers](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65206)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65206
- **Author:** [simon-veitner-redhat](https://github.com/simon-veitner-redhat)
- **Merged By:** [mgoin](https://github.com/mgoin)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 10:28:41
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65206/files) (3):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/models_basic.yaml`
  - `tests/models/test_muse_spark_nvidia_shared_rope.py`
  - `vllm/models/muse_spark/nvidia/model.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The NVIDIA Muse Spark model previously constructed a private fp32 RoPE table in each of its 46 RoPE attention layers, costing roughly 11.5 GiB per GPU. This PR routes non-draft target layers through get_rope, which caches one instance per key, so all target layers share a single table, and sizes that table by max_model_len. The DSpark draft path keeps its private table with its original dtype and sizing logic.

**Technical impact**  
Model loading memory drops by about 11.25 GiB per GPU (81.0 to 69.75 GiB on 8x B200 TP8), which grows the KV cache by roughly 14.5 percent. Numerics are unchanged because each table row is computed identically regardless of table length and sharing does not alter values; the unfused path now converts one shared table instead of 46 copies. A new CPU-only unit test asserts identity and fp32 dtype of the shared table and is wired into the Basic Models CI step.

**Potential risks**  
The smaller max_model_len sizing for target tables was not validated end to end, so an edge case where target positions exceed max_model_len could be a latent risk. The get_rope cache is process-global, so the test's monkeypatched empty _ROPE_DICT is essential to avoid leaking CPU tables into later GPU tests, and future tests must follow the same pattern. GSM8K showed a 96 vs 95 difference attributed to batch-order noise, and the DSpark spec-decode path has a separate pre-existing load bug (_context_kv_proj unset) that this PR does not fix.

**Key insights**  
This is a clean, well-scoped memory optimization that mirrors an existing ROCm fix (#62757) and is backed by strong validation: bit-identical greedy output, token-identical sanity runs, and near-identical speculative acceptance rates. The main review focus should be the max_model_len-based sizing assumption and ensuring the global get_rope cache does not cause cross-test contamination. Performance was not measured but is expected to be neutral since kernels read the same values.

## 86. [[ROCm][Perf] Keep 32-bit buffer addressing for per-token-head KV and AITER cross-attention fallback](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64317)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64317
- **Author:** [umarkovi-amd](https://github.com/umarkovi-amd)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 10:26:13
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64317/files) (2):**
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/rocm_aiter_unified_attn.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/triton_attn.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds six lines across two ROCm attention backends to call the existing set_triton_ptr_range helper on KV-cache views that were previously missing the hint: the per-token-head scale caches and K/V views in TritonAttentionImpl, and the K/V views used by the non-causal AITER cross-attention fallback. This lets Triton's AMD backend keep 32-bit buffer addressing instead of falling back to 64-bit, yielding large throughput gains (up to +60%) on RDNA3 for per-token-head KV caches and a small gain for Whisper cross-attention.

**Technical impact**  
Purely a code-generation hint: it changes pointer-range metadata seen by Triton, reducing VGPR pressure and address arithmetic in kernel_unified_attention and related kernels. No new configuration, no API change, and the helper only reads shapes, strides, and data_ptr(). Behavior is gated by the existing _set_kv_ptr_range ROCm flag, and the AITER causal kernel and INT4 per-token-head path are deliberately untouched.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness depends on the computed bound (distance from each view's data_ptr to the end of the layer region) being a valid upper bound; the PR argues offsets are non-negative and in-layer, and Triton re-checks the 2 GiB limit at launch. The hint is ineffective when a layer's reservation exceeds 2 GiB (e.g., gpt-oss-20b INT8 with extensible KV cache on), so gains are workload-dependent and measured only on RDNA3/gfx1100. No new tests were added, and CI lacks RDNA runners, so regressions on other AMD architectures would not be caught automatically.

**Key insights**  
The change is a targeted follow-up to #60084 extending 32-bit addressing coverage to two overlooked view-construction sites, with strong empirical validation (stable multi-run throughput, token-identical Whisper output, GSM8K within noise, VGPR reductions confirmed via Triton cache inspection). The interaction with the extensible KV cache (#56492) is well analyzed: eager launches benefit automatically post-extend, while CUDA-graph-captured kernels only benefit when the per-layer reservation is under 2 GiB, a limitation explicitly deferred to a separate PR.

## 87. [[MM] Mistral vision Encoder CG support](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56277)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56277
- **Author:** [NickLucche](https://github.com/NickLucche)
- **Merged By:** [NickLucche](https://github.com/NickLucche)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 10:25:28
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56277/files) (7):**
  - `docs/design/cuda_graphs_multimodal.md`
  - `tests/models/multimodal/generation/test_pixtral.py`
  - `tests/models/multimodal/generation/test_vit_cudagraph.py`
  - `tests/v1/cudagraph/test_encoder_cudagraph.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/mistral3.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/pixtral.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/encoder_cudagraph.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds encoder CUDA graph support for Mistral multimodal models using the Pixtral vision encoder, covering both PixtralForConditionalGeneration and Mistral3ForConditionalGeneration. The change introduces a PixtralEncoderCudaGraphMixin that packs variable-resolution image patches into fixed-capacity graph buffers, precomputes attention metadata, rotary embeddings, and patch-merger indices, and falls back to eager execution for unsupported attention configs. It also adds a guard in the encoder CUDA graph manager to skip capture when no supported modalities are configured, plus tests and docs updates.

**Technical impact**  
Substantial performance improvement: the PR reports roughly 4.6x faster mean encoder execution and about 78% lower encoder latency on Ministral 3B, with end-to-end latency roughly halved. It touches core model code (pixtral.py, mistral3.py), the encoder CUDA graph manager, and adds new packed forward paths (forward_packed, project_packed_image_features) that must stay numerically equivalent to the eager paths. New helper functions (_make_merge_indices, _merge_patches_by_index, _patch_grid_positions, pack_image_patches) are covered by unit tests.

**Potential risks**  
The packed/graph path relies on assumptions that image patch grids are divisible by the spatial merge size and that processors resize images accordingly; violations raise errors. Correctness depends on the merge-index gather matching the unfold order of the patch mergers, which is tested but only for limited shapes. Graph capture is disabled for TORCH_SDPA, dynamic FP8 scales, and precomputed embeddings, so coverage is partial. Keeping max_seqlen on CPU and using async_tensor_h2d introduces device/host synchronization subtleties. The change overlaps with PR #54214, creating potential merge conflicts. The diff is truncated, so parts of the implementation could not be fully reviewed.

**Key insights**  
The design cleverly achieves fixed-shape graph buffers by padding the packed sequence with zero-length sequences up to max_batch_size and precomputing all metadata, so replays only copy fresh values. The mixin abstraction lets both Pixtral and Mistral3 share the encoder CUDA graph logic while keeping model-specific projection in forward_packed. The added manager guard prevents capture attempts when no modalities are supported, which is a sensible defensive fix. Overall a well-scoped, performance-focused change with good test coverage, though the truncated diff limits full verification.

---

## 88. [[Perf][Startup] Keep openai out of import vllm.config via lazy ToolServer import](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65423)

- **PR Number:** #65423

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Moves the module-level import of ToolServer in vllm/reasoning/abs_reasoning_parsers.py under TYPE_CHECKING and quotes the single annotation that uses it, since ToolServer is only referenced in a type hint. This breaks the import chain vllm.config -> vllm.reasoning -> entrypoints.mcp.tool_server -> openai, keeping openai and openai_harmony out of every process that imports vllm.config. The lazy_imports test is extended to block openai and to explicitly import vllm.config so regressions are caught.

**Technical impact**  
Reduces startup import cost for all vLLM processes (EngineCore, workers, API server), with the PR reporting vllm.config.reasoning cumulative import time dropping from roughly 310-440 ms to 6-9 ms and openai no longer appearing in sys.modules after import vllm.config. Behavior is unchanged at runtime because ToolServer was never used at runtime in this module. The change is low-risk and localized to two files.

**Potential risks**  
The new lazy_imports assertion only runs in the (CPU) Params/Env/Tokenizers/Parser Buildkite job, which is currently commented out, so the regression guard is not active in CI until #64414 restores it. Any future runtime use of ToolServer in this module would now fail with a NameError instead of an import error, though the quoted annotation and TYPE_CHECKING placement make this unlikely. Minor rebase conflict expected with #57310 in lazy_imports.py.

**Key insights**  
This is a clean, well-validated import-layering fix that removes a heavy frontend dependency (openai) from the shared config import path. The main gap is CI coverage: the test that enforces the invariant is disabled, so the protection depends on a follow-up PR. The measurements and reasoning test results support the claimed benefit.

## 89. [[ROCm][Perf] W4A16: retune int4 skinny GEMM decode tuples for gfx115x](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65322)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65322
- **Author:** [roberteg16](https://github.com/roberteg16)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 10:18:11
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65322/files) (2):**
  - `csrc/rocm/skinny_gemms_int4.cu`
  - `tests/kernels/quantization/test_rdna_hybrid_w4a16.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR retunes the wvSplitK_int4_g int4 skinny GEMM decode dispatch for batch 1-5 on gfx1151 and gfx1150/1152/1153, replacing the previous generic tuple-selection chain with per-target macros (WVSPLIT_INT4G_GFX1151 and WVSPLIT_INT4G_GFX115X) that pick YTILE/UNRL/A_CHUNK/WvPrGrp based on batch, M, K, dtype, and sym/asym mode. It also adds an on_gfx1151_int4() helper, narrows some tile instantiations (batch-1-only tiles built for N=1, symmetric-only UNRL=8 tile), and adds new test shapes covering the new tuples. The goal is measurable decode throughput gains (up to +62% on gfx1150) while eliminating register spills (151->1 on gfx1151, 144->0 on gfx1150).

**Technical impact**  
Behavior of the int4 skinny GEMM dispatch changes for a large set of decode shapes on gfx115x targets, with reported mean gains of roughly +4% to +23% per batch and best cases up to +60%. The refactor into constexpr-guarded macros keeps tuple selection compile-time per batch while runtime-guarding on M/K/dtype, and the narrowed tile instantiation reduces compiled kernel count and spill pressure. Fallback to generic tiles is preserved for uncovered cases and non-gfx115x targets, so other architectures should be unaffected.

**Potential risks**  
The new rules are tuned on a 38-shape sweep and may regress shapes outside that set; the PR itself reports small worst-case regressions (down to about -1.9%) on some points. gfx1152/gfx1153 reuse gfx1150 rules without being measured, so their performance is unverified. The macro-heavy dispatch is harder to read and maintain, and the truncated diff means the full fallback path and the narrowed tile instantiation logic could not be fully reviewed. Correctness depends on the new test shapes adequately covering each tuple branch.

**Key insights**  
This is a well-scoped, measurement-backed performance tuning change with clear before/after data and spill-count evidence, plus targeted test additions. The main review focus should be on the full (untruncated) dispatch macro to confirm fallback correctness and that the narrowed tile instantiations still cover all reachable N values, and on whether unmeasured gfx1152/gfx1153 warrant at least a smoke benchmark. The small negative deltas are within acceptable noise for a net-positive tuning change.
