## 90. [[Bugfix][Benchmark] Preserve inactive expert IDs in synthetic forward routing](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65253)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65253
- **Author:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged By:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 10:16:37
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65253/files) (4):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/quantization.yaml`
  - `docs/benchmarking/cli.md`
  - `tests/benchmarks/test_forward_cli.py`
  - `vllm/benchmarks/forward/routing.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a bug in vllm bench forward's synthetic routing hook where substituting the Zipf/balanced fixture overwrote the entire topk_ids tensor, reactivating inactive -1 expert slots. The fix adds a torch.where(ids == -1, ids, replaced) mask so inactive sentinels are preserved while active slots get synthetic IDs. Also parametrizes the Muse CLI test for balanced and zipf routing, adds a focused regression test across positional/keyword and int32/int64 variants, updates docs, and renames the CI test selector.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change confined to the benchmark's generic expert-ID hook; production serving and the gate-logits hook are untouched. The added comparison/selection runs inside the timed forward but introduces no host synchronization. The fixture tensor is not mutated, so subsequent replays can correctly activate previously masked rows. Test coverage now spans both routing modes and dtype/argument-passing variants.

**Potential risks**  
The extra torch.where adds minor overhead to the timed benchmark path, which could slightly affect measured throughput though not correctness. The fix only addresses the expert-ID hook; the gate-logits path relies on downstream model padding masks, so padded EP performance is not validated end-to-end. CI selector rename could miss coverage if the old test name is referenced elsewhere.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was whole-tensor replacement ignoring the inactive sentinel, a subtle correctness bug that could inflate benchmark results by routing padded rows. The regression test is well-designed, verifying both sentinel preservation and non-mutation across replays. Documentation appropriately clarifies exact-shape benchmark graphs versus serving capture-bucket padding, scoping the claim honestly.

## 91. [[Fast Start] Support ModelOpt CUTLASS FP8 weight-cache restarts](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63754)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63754
- **Author:** [liusy58](https://github.com/liusy58)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 10:06:34
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63754/files) (3):**
  - `docs/features/preload.md`
  - `tests/model_executor/model_loader/test_weight_cache.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/modelopt.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Enables the weight-cache daemon to reuse serialized ModelOpt FP8 (per-tensor and per-channel-per-token) linear weights via CUDA IPC when the CUTLASS linear backend is selected. The change opts in only those two weight schemes in supports_pre_processed_weights, restores processed_weight_dims=(0,1) for the transposed layout without re-transposing or requantizing, and adds a real-checkpoint IPC test plus documentation.

**Technical impact**  
Narrows the capability opt-in to kFp8StaticTensorSym/kFp8StaticTokenSym with a CutlassFP8ScaledMMLinearKernel and a plain FormatScheme, so other ModelOpt FP8 backends and block FP8 remain unsupported. The early return in process_weights_after_loading avoids double-processing daemon-shared tensors, and relies on the prerequisite PR for CUTLASS logical output width so padded shared weights still yield correct output dimensions. Adds a new SM89+ gated test and updates the preload feature guide.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness depends on the daemon and engine using matching kernel configuration (CUTLASS) and on the prerequisite #63672 for output-width restoration; mismatches could silently produce wrong shapes. The isinstance check on the concrete kernel class is brittle if kernel selection or class hierarchy changes. Validation is limited to B300 TP1 BF16 eager smoke tests with no multi-GPU, ROCm, performance, or accuracy benchmarks, and GitHub/Buildkite checks were pending at publication.

**Key insights**  
The fix is a narrow, well-scoped capability opt-in rather than a new loading path, reusing existing kernel and metadata interfaces. The main review focus should be the kernel/format guard conditions and the processed_weight_dims restoration, plus confirming the prerequisite dependency is merged. Test coverage is reasonable for the feature but relies on a single pinned checkpoint and a specific GPU class.

## 92. [[ROCm][Perf] Encoder prefill attention on gfx1151: split-D head dims, alignment hint, launch config](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56450)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56450
- **Author:** [roberteg16](https://github.com/roberteg16)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 08:52:09
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56450/files) (3):**
  - `benchmarks/kernels/benchmark_vit_prefill_attn.py`
  - `tests/kernels/attention/test_triton_prefill_attention.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/ops/triton_prefill_attention.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a gfx1151-only fast path to the encoder prefill attention kernel (triton_prefill_attention.py) that splits non-power-of-2 head dims into a power-of-2 main block plus a power-of-2 tail (e.g. 72 -> 64+16), adds a tl.multiple_of alignment hint on head-axis offsets derived from real strides, and narrows the KV tile (BLOCK_N=16, 4 warps) for the 64+16 case. Also adds a microbenchmark script and expands tests to cover head dims 64/72/80/96 plus new tile-config and correctness tests. Motivation is a reported 2.19x kernel speedup at head_dim=72 and ~21% TTFT improvement on Gemma3-4B.

**Technical impact**  
The kernel gains a new constexpr BLOCK_DMODEL_TAIL that is dead code when zero, so all non-gfx1151 and power-of-2 paths compile to the prior single-block kernel. The wrapper computes launch args as before and only reassigns inside an on_gfx1151() branch, gated on bf16/fp16 dtype and stride alignment. The alignment hint is unconditional on gfx1151 but only informative when head stride is 8 mod 16. New tests exercise the split path and the non-contiguous fallback.

**Potential risks**  
The split-D path is only exercised for real on ROCm (test skips otherwise), so coverage on non-ROCm CI is limited to config capture. The alignment hint relies on runtime stride checks; a bug there could mis-vectorize loads, though the non-contiguous test guards this. The narrow tile is gated on the hint, but the interaction with future BLOCK_N changes or other RDNA parts is untested. Benchmark script monkeypatches _fwd_kernel, which is fragile if the launch signature changes. Performance claims are single-device (gfx1151) and not independently verified in this review.

**Key insights**  
Well-scoped, device-gated optimization with constexpr dead-code fallback and stride-derived (not head_dim-derived) alignment, which is the correct soundness choice. The PR is careful to show the hint is neutral except at head_dim=72 and that the narrow tile regresses without vectorized loads, justifying the combined gating. Test additions meaningfully cover the new tail widths and the fallback path. Main residual concern is that real-kernel correctness for the split path only runs on ROCm hardware.

## 93. [[ROCm][Kernel] New "hip" GDN backend for gfx1151](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56307)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56307
- **Author:** [roberteg16](https://github.com/roberteg16)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 08:51:41
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56307/files) (13):**
  - `CMakeLists.txt`
  - `benchmarks/kernels/benchmark_gdn_chunk.py`
  - `csrc/rocm/ops.h`
  - `csrc/rocm/rdna35_gdn_chunked.cu`
  - `csrc/rocm/torch_bindings.cpp`
  - `tests/kernels/mamba/test_gdn_chunked_rocm.py`
  - `tests/kernels/mamba/test_gdn_rocm_layout_dispatch.py`
  - `tests/kernels/quantization/test_rdna3_compile_guards.py`
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_gdn_aiter_flydsl.py`
  - `vllm/engine/arg_utils.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/mamba/gdn/qwen_gdn_linear_attn.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/mamba/ops/hip_gdn_chunk.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/gdn_attn.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new HIP (ROCm) GDN prefill backend for RDNA3.5 (gfx1150-gfx1153) that fuses the chunked delta rule (WY/UT transform) into a single kernel, keeping state in registers and per-chunk intermediates in LDS to avoid DDR round-trips. It wires the backend into CMake, torch bindings, backend resolution (auto on gfx1151, explicit 'hip' elsewhere), CLI args, and the ChunkGatedDeltaRule forward path, plus a benchmark and tests. The Triton/FLA path is left untouched and unsupported calls fall back per-call.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a new compiled CUDA/HIP source (csrc/rocm/rdna35_gdn_chunked.cu) gated by VLLM_ROCM_GFX115X, a new torch op rdna35_gdn_chunked, and a Python wrapper (hip_gdn_chunk.py) with availability/support checks and a fake registration. Backend selection logic in qwen_gdn_linear_attn.py gains a 'hip' branch with fail-closed behavior for explicit requests and Triton fallback for unsupported models. Reported speedups are 1.0x-9.9x over Triton with improved fp32 accuracy and unchanged GSM8K accuracy.

**Potential risks**  
Only gfx1151 has been validated on hardware; gfx1150/gfx1152/gfx1153 are compile-tested only, so runtime correctness there is unverified. The kernel relies on architecture-specific intrinsics (DPP, exp2f, cumode attribute, register/LDS budget assumptions) that are fragile across compiler and hardware revisions. The auto-selection on gfx1151 changes default behavior for existing users, and the per-call fallback path adds branching complexity. Benchmark and accuracy gains are measured on a single model/hardware, so generalization is uncertain.

**Key insights**  
The design avoids materializing h, w, and u by rewriting v_new = Tinv(V beta - (K beta exp(g_cs)) S), fitting a 32-token chunk in 64 KB LDS, and keeps intermediates in fp32 for better accuracy than the bf16 Triton chain. Backend resolution is carefully layered: auto is conservative (gfx1151 only), explicit 'hip' fails closed if the op is missing, and unsupported shapes/dtypes degrade to Triton. Tests cover numerics against an fp64 reference, dispatch/selection, and guard placement, which is appropriate for a hardware-specific kernel addition.

---

## 94. [[Bugfix][ROCm] Raise the extensible KV cache margin floor to 3 GiB](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65418)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65418
- **Author:** [djramic](https://github.com/djramic)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 08:45:05
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65418/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/v1/worker/extensible_kv_cache.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR raises the ROCm margin floor for the extensible KV cache from 2 GiB to 3 GiB by changing KV_CACHE_MARGIN_FLOOR_BYTES in vllm/v1/worker/extensible_kv_cache.py. The intent is to give torch more room for memory fragmentation on MI355, fixing OOMs observed in the Spec Decode Eagle and DPX nightly CI jobs where reserved-but-unused memory (1.3-1.7 GiB) plus a new large allocation exceeded the post-warmup limit.

**Technical impact**  
This is a one-line configuration constant change that alters runtime memory budgeting behavior on ROCm only, reducing the memory vLLM allows torch to reserve and thereby lowering KV cache capacity slightly on ROCm devices. Non-ROCm platforms are unaffected since the floor remains 1 GiB. The change trades a small amount of usable KV cache memory for increased headroom against allocator fragmentation.

**Potential risks**  
The fix is a heuristic bump rather than a structural fix, so fragmentation behavior could still exceed 3 GiB under different workloads or future model sizes. Reducing the torch memory limit may slightly lower achievable KV cache capacity or throughput on ROCm, and the validation appears limited to MI355X with specific CI images, so other ROCm hardware or configurations are not explicitly covered.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is well documented: fragmentation varies 0.2-0.3 GiB run to run while prior headroom was only 0.05-0.08 GiB, making the original floor too tight. Raising the floor to 3 GiB is a pragmatic mitigation, but the underlying fragility of a fixed margin against variable fragmentation suggests a more adaptive margin strategy could be worth considering later.

---

## 95. [[ROCm][Perf][Bugfix] wvSplitK: launch tiles of their own for gfx1151 and gfx1150; fix bigType for A_CHUNK 16](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63835)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63835
- **Author:** [roberteg16](https://github.com/roberteg16)
- **Merged By:** [dllehr-amd](https://github.com/dllehr-amd)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 08:41:13
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63835/files) (2):**
  - `csrc/rocm/skinny_gemms.cu`
  - `tests/kernels/quantization/test_rocm_skinny_gemms.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR tunes the ROCm wave32 wvSplitK skinny GEMM kernel for gfx1151 and the gfx1150/gfx1152/gfx1153 class by introducing per-architecture launch tile configurations selected from a sweep of non-spilling configs, and fixes a correctness bug in bigType sizing for A_CHUNK=16. It also switches bf16 DOT2C on gfx11/gfx12 to use v_dot2_f32_bf16 instead of widening operands to f32, and adds test cases for K % 16 != 0 and N=5.

**Technical impact**  
The WVSPLITK_CFG macro is refactored into WVSPLITK_CFG_AC that takes A_CHUNK explicitly, with WVSPLITK_CFG preserving A_CHUNK=8 for other archs. half4 is now fixed at 4 bf16 elements (the operand of its only consumer) and a new floatx4 type replaces scalar8 for sum4 accumulators, which is required for the new A_CHUNK=16 path. The gfx1151 and gfx1150-class launch logic is rewritten with simpler, arch-specific tile rows, and A_CHUNK falls back to 8 when K % 16 != 0 to avoid incorrect results.

**Potential risks**  
The new launch rows are tuned on a limited shape set, so untested shapes or future hardware could regress; the PR reports at most 3 of 150 cells slower by >2% per dtype, which is acceptable but not zero. The bf16 v_dot2_f32_bf16 change alters summation/rounding order, producing small GSM8K differences (2-3 of 1319 answers), which is within noise but worth monitoring. The A_CHUNK=16 path depends on K % 16 == 0 being correctly enforced; any missed fallback would silently produce wrong results. The gfx1150-class detection uses substring matching on gcnArchName, which is consistent with existing on_gfx1151 but could match unintended future archs.

**Key insights**  
The bigType fix is a genuine correctness bug: half4 was sized as (A_CHUNK/2) bf16 (32 B at A_CHUNK=16) but the consumer reads 4 bf16 (64 B), causing reads past the activation row and overlapping LDS writes; no prior launch used A_CHUNK=16, so the bug was latent. The PR is well-validated with interleaved A/B benchmarks, cold caches, held-out shapes, bias cases, GSM8K accuracy, and new edge-case tests. The refactor cleanly separates A_CHUNK from the launch macro, making future per-arch tuning easier.

---

Removes the backend="openai-chat" argument from the get_samples() call in the NixlConnector PD + Spec Decode acceptance test. **Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between the declared chat backend and the actual completions endpoint used, which #65245 exposed by changing get_samples() templating behavior. The bisect and before/after CI validation are thorough and well-documented, and the one-line fix is minimal and targeted. ## 97. [[Bugfix][Spec Decode] Use vllm.config.replace for the MRV1 DFlash draft config](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65350)

  - `vllm/v1/spec_decode/dflash.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The MRV1 DFlash proposer's _create_draft_vllm_config was using stdlib dataclasses.replace on VllmConfig, which broke after a new init=False field (num_hash_lookahead_tokens) was added, causing a Pydantic ValidationError at engine startup. The PR swaps the import and call to vllm.config.replace, which skips init=False fields, aligning with the base proposer and MRV2 DFlash loader.

**Technical impact**  
Restores MRV1 DFlash engine startup by using the project's custom replace helper that correctly handles Pydantic models with init=False fields. Behavior is otherwise unchanged; the diff is a one-line import/call swap in a single file. MRV2 is unaffected per the description.

**Potential risks**  
Low risk given the minimal change and that vllm.config.replace is already used elsewhere. Residual risk is limited to any subtle semantic differences between stdlib replace and vllm.config.replace for other fields, but those are already exercised by the base proposer. Validation is limited to local MI355X runs and nightly CI links rather than a dedicated new test.

**Key insights**  
This is a targeted regression fix for a config-construction incompatibility introduced by a new init=False field. It highlights the importance of using the project's config replace helper consistently across all VllmConfig mutation sites to avoid future breakage when fields are added.

## 98. [[KV Offload] Refactor cpu offloading - calculate gpu pointers and introduce fs ABC](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56076)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56076
- **Author:** [effi-ofer](https://github.com/effi-ofer)
- **Merged By:** [orozery](https://github.com/orozery)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 07:58:46
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56076/files) (12):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/offloading_connector/test_worker.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/offloading_connector/utils.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/cpu/test_canonical_layout.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/cpu/test_gpu_worker.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/fs/__init__.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/fs/test_fs_worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/offloading/worker.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/base.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/cpu/gpu_worker.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/fs/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/fs/worker.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/pointer_utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR refactors the KV offloading worker interface so that logical device block IDs are resolved to device pointers once (via a new resolve_device_pointers helper in pointer_utils.py) before being passed to workers, instead of each worker duplicating tensor layout logic. It also introduces a new FSOffloadingWorker abstract base class (with ThreadPoolFSWorker and StreamCompletion helpers) to support file-based backends like GDS/cuFile, POSIX, and NIXL, and updates the CPU worker, base classes (DevicePointers, IOCompletion protocol), connector worker, and associated tests accordingly.

**Technical impact**  
The OffloadingWorker.submit_store/submit_load signatures change from taking GPULoadStoreSpec to taking a pre-resolved DevicePointers object, which is a breaking interface change for any out-of-tree worker implementations. The CPU SingleDirectionOffloadingHandler no longer holds GPU tensors and instead consumes device pointers, simplifying its constructor and removing duplicated pointer computation. New abstractions (DevicePointers, IOCompletion, FSOffloadingWorker, ThreadPoolFSWorker) lay groundwork for the GDS implementation and decouple I/O dispatch from completion tracking.

**Potential risks**  
The interface change is breaking for external/custom OffloadingWorker subclasses and could silently break downstream integrations. The test utils monkeypatch resolve_device_pointers in the connector worker module and key a map by id(device_ptrs), which is fragile and could mask real pointer-resolution bugs. The new FS worker's file_offset computation and block_indices/skip handling are subtle (spanning files, multi-data-ref layouts) and only lightly covered by the new tests. Pointer resolution correctness depends on tensor.stride(0) and page_size_bytes assumptions that may not hold for all attention backends.

**Key insights**  
Centralizing pointer resolution removes duplicated layout logic across workers and is a sensible prerequisite for the GDS backend. The new FSOffloadingWorker ABC cleanly separates key-to-path resolution and completion tracking from backend-specific I/O, enabling reuse across filesystem backends. Reviewers should focus on the correctness of resolve_device_pointers stride math, the FS worker's offset/skip arithmetic, and the test monkeypatching approach, since these are the highest-risk areas despite the PR reporting all tests passing.

## 99. [[Metrics][Frontend] Expose request render duration metric](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60213)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #60213
- **Author:** [NickLucche](https://github.com/NickLucche)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 07:38:53
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/60213/files) (10):**
  - `tests/entrypoints/openai/chat_completion/test_chat_error.py`
  - `tests/entrypoints/openai/chat_completion/test_serving_chat.py`
  - `tests/entrypoints/openai/completion/test_completion_error.py`
  - `tests/entrypoints/openai/completion/test_lora_resolvers.py`
  - `tests/entrypoints/openai/test_render_parity.py`
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/test_generate_stream.py`

  - `tests/renderers/test_metrics.py`
  - `vllm/renderers/metrics.py`
  - `vllm/renderers/online_renderer.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new Prometheus histogram vllm:request_render_duration_seconds (new vllm/renderers/metrics.py) and instruments OnlineRenderer.render_chat and render_completion with perf_counter timing, observing duration on successful returns. The goal is to make chat templating, tokenization, and multimodal preprocessing latency visible to operators, especially for deployments that call /render separately. Test mocks were updated to add served_model_name, and a new unit test file covers the metrics module.

**Technical impact**  
Purely additive observability on the CPU frontend path: two perf_counter calls and one histogram observe per successful request, with bounded model_name/request_type labels. Lazy registration via lru_cache avoids the startup unregister_vllm_metrics issue noted in the code. The new dependency on model_config.served_model_name required updating several test MockModelConfig classes, indicating the renderer now assumes that attribute exists on the config.

**Potential risks**  
The cast(str, self.model_config.served_model_name) assumes served_model_name is always a non-None string; if it can be None or a list in some configs, labels could be wrong or raise. Label cardinality is bounded but model_name still multiplies series per served model. render_responses is not instrumented, so coverage is partial. Timing includes early-return paths only on success, which is intentional but means error-path render latency is invisible. No end-to-end serving validation was run (no GPU), so real-world overhead and metric exposure under PROMETHEUS_MULTIPROC_DIR were not exercised beyond unit tests.

**Key insights**  
The lazy-registration pattern is a sensible workaround for the global registry unregistration at startup and is well tested. The change is low-risk and additive, but reviewers should confirm served_model_name is reliably a string across all ModelConfig variants and consider whether the responses path and error-path timing warrant follow-up. The bucket range (0.1 ms to 10 s) is reasonable for render-stage latency.

## 100. [[Perf][KV Offload] Publish async lookup results per request group](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57474)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57474
- **Author:** [Alex-ai-future](https://github.com/Alex-ai-future)
- **Merged By:** [orozery](https://github.com/orozery)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 07:22:09
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57474/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/tiering/test_async_lookup.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/tiering/async_lookup.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The async lookup worker previously accumulated results from all request groups in a scheduler step and posted a single combined batch after the last group finished. This PR changes the worker to publish each request group's results immediately after that group's batch_lookup() output is fully consumed, so completed groups no longer wait on slower later groups. Tests were added to verify per-group publication, FIFO ordering, and lazy-iterator atomicity.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change in result-publication timing within the KV offload async lookup path. The worker remains single-threaded and FIFO, and lookup verdicts/promotion policy are unchanged, but the scheduler can now drain a HIT group earlier, potentially starting promotion sooner. The change is localized to async_lookup.py and reuses the existing _pending_results queue and drain path.

**Potential risks**  
The PR description notes test results were recorded on an earlier commit and not rerun on the current head, so current test status is unverified. The replay is a simulated, directional timing study that does not run the full scheduler or promotion transfers, so real serving impact is unproven. Publishing per group increases the number of queue items and drain iterations per step, and correctness depends on the invariant that a group's list is never mutated after enqueue and that partial groups are never published.

**Key insights**  
The change is a small, well-scoped latency optimization that removes inter-group publication serialization without parallelizing lookups. The added tests directly target the key invariants (blocked-group early publication and lazy-iterator atomicity), which is good coverage. The main gap is that validation evidence is stale relative to the PR head and lacks end-to-end serving benchmarks, so reviewers should confirm tests pass on the current commit before merge.

## 101. [[Bugfix][KV Offload][P2P] Clean up both roles on request finish](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64686)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64686
- **Author:** [waizuichougou](https://github.com/waizuichougou)
- **Merged By:** [orozery](https://github.com/orozery)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 07:22:00
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64686/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/tiering/p2p/test_manager.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/tiering/p2p/manager.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a P2P lifecycle bug in on_request_finished() where dual-role requests (remote_kv_source plus remote_decoder) returned early after finishing only the source-side session, leaving the decoder-side _kv_to_session entry stale until teardown. The manager now finishes source and destination roles independently, pops the destination entry from _kv_to_session, and suppresses duplicate finish_request calls when both roles share the same session and request ID. A parametrized regression test covers shared/non-shared session and request ID combinations.

**Technical impact**  
Changes cleanup behavior in the P2P tiering manager so both consumer and decoder sessions are properly finished and the _kv_to_session map is drained for dual-role requests. This reduces stale session entries and delayed server-round cleanup on long-lived peers. The deduplication guard preserves correctness when source and decoder are the same node, avoiding double finish_request calls.

**Potential risks**  
The dedup condition relies on identity comparison of session objects plus equality of request IDs; if a future refactor returns distinct session wrappers for the same underlying peer, duplicate finish_request could occur. Also, popping dest_id from _kv_to_session unconditionally when dest_id is present could affect prefiller-side flows if a dest_id collides with a source-bound entry, though the shared-session guard mitigates the common case. Test coverage is strong but limited to the fake-session harness.

**Key insights**  
The original early return conflated 'source present' with 'only source role to clean up', which is invalid for documented dual-role PD prefiller requests. Separating role cleanup and explicitly handling the shared-session/shared-id case is the minimal correct fix. The parametrized test matrix (shared_session x shared_request_id) is a good pattern for lifecycle edge cases.

## 102. [[Compile] Fix compile warning topk `csrc/topk_histogram_4096_overflow.cuh`](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65185)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65185
- **Author:** [yewentao256](https://github.com/yewentao256)
- **Merged By:** [yewentao256](https://github.com/yewentao256)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 06:57:19
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65185/files) (2):**
  - `csrc/topk_histogram_4096.cuh`
  - `csrc/topk_histogram_4096_overflow.cuh`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR fixes a CUDA compiler warning (#802-D) about specifying default arguments when redeclaring an unreferenced function template. The default template and function arguments for exact_topk_rescan were moved from the definition in topk_histogram_4096_overflow.cuh to the forward declaration in topk_histogram_4096.cuh, so defaults are declared only once.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior is unchanged; only the location of default arguments shifts from the definition to the declaration. This is the standard-compliant way to declare defaults for a template, eliminating the nvcc warning while keeping call sites that rely on defaults working. Builds now complete without the warning.

**Potential risks**  
Low risk. If any translation unit included only the definition header without the declaration header, defaults would be lost, but the diff shows the declaration is in the shared header. No runtime or algorithmic change is introduced.

**Key insights**  
This is a build-hygiene fix rather than a functional change, but it touches C++ template semantics so it is classified SUBSTANTIAL. The correct pattern is to place default template/function arguments on the first declaration, not the definition, which is exactly what this PR does.

## 103. [[Feature] Support batch invariance for ShortConv (LFM2) models](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62367)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #62367
- **Author:** [Manfredss](https://github.com/Manfredss)
- **Merged By:** [yewentao256](https://github.com/yewentao256)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 06:49:36
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62367/files) (2):**
  - `docs/features/batch_invariance.md`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/short_conv_attn.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a supports_batch_invariance classmethod to ShortConvAttentionBackend returning True, and an explicit override returning False on PleShortConvAttentionBackend, plus a docs entry listing LFM2-8B-A1B as validated. This unblocks LFM2/LFM2-MoE models from starting under VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT=1, which previously raised a RuntimeError, addressing issue #61319.

**Technical impact**  
Enables the batch-invariant code path for SHORT_CONV_ATTN, so LFM2 models can produce reproducible greedy outputs across batch shapes and repeated generate() rounds. The change is a capability declaration only; it does not alter kernel math, and behavior is unchanged when VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT is unset. PLE_SHORT_CONV_ATTN remains opted out, preserving its prior behavior.

**Potential risks**  
Validation is limited to Ampere (SM86), Model Runner V2, and non-spec-decoding, non-VL LFM2 configs; other hardware, V1 runner, spec decoding, and LFM2-VL are untested. The correctness argument relies on causal_conv1d being per-sequence with no cross-sequence reduction, which should be verified for all supported conv implementations. No CI test was added, so regressions in this opt-in could go unnoticed.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is that prefix caching shrinks the first prefill step, and without batch invariance kernel selection varies with batch shape, causing ULP-level drift and argmax flips. The fix is a minimal, well-scoped capability flag rather than a global determinism hack, and the author explicitly scopes out PLE_SHORT_CONV_ATTN and documents untested areas. Adding a cheap ~35s LFM2 needle test to CI would strengthen coverage.

## 104. [[Fast Start] Support online FP8 and CUTLASS NVFP4 weight-cache restarts](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63672)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63672
- **Author:** [liusy58](https://github.com/liusy58)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 05:57:10
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63672/files) (7):**
  - `docs/features/preload.md`
  - `tests/model_executor/model_loader/test_weight_cache.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/scaled_mm/cutlass.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/experts/cutlass_moe.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/modelopt.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/online/fp8.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/online/mxfp8.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Extends the weight-cache daemon so online FP8 (per-tensor, per-channel, per-block) and MXFP8 dense linear weights, plus ModelOpt NVFP4 CUTLASS MoE weights, can be reused across engine restarts via IPC instead of being requantized. It adds supports_pre_processed_weights opt-ins to online FP8/MXFP8 methods, a pre-processed early-return in the CUTLASS FP4 MoE expert and ModelOpt NVFP4 path, fixes the CUTLASS FP8 kernel to initialize logical_output_size from the weight shape, and adds tests and docs.

**Technical impact**  
Online quantization methods now short-circuit process_weights_after_loading when weights are pre-processed, restoring runtime state (input_scale=None, quant keys, kernel build) without re-quantizing shared daemon tensors. The NVFP4 CUTLASS MoE skips its in-place activation-scale fusion on pre-processed weights, preventing double fusion of shared scales, and the CUTLASS FP8 kernel preserves the unpadded logical output width when IPC loading skips post-processing. Support is gated by backend type and requantization_source, so unsupported paths still raise.

**Potential risks**  
The early-return paths rely on correctly reconstructing kernel state and quant keys; a mismatch between daemon and engine backend/kernel config could yield silently wrong results rather than an error, though fingerprint checks and explicit RuntimeErrors mitigate this. Validation is limited to single-GPU B300 with specific PyTorch/CUDA builds and output-equivalence smoke tests, not accuracy or performance benchmarks, and no multi-GPU, ROCm, or full dependency-matrix coverage is claimed. GitHub checks were pending at publication and no human review or Buildkite GPU-suite pass is asserted.

**Key insights**  
The change is narrowly scoped to opt-in restore paths and explicitly excludes online MoE and requantization from serialized checkpoints. The logical_output_size fix and the NVFP4 double-fusion guard address concrete regressions (padded CUTLASS assertion failure and scale double-multiplication) that would otherwise corrupt shared daemon tensors. Test coverage adds focused loading regressions and extends the padding test to construct a real kernel, which is appropriate for the risk profile.

## 105. [[Bugfix] Keep default max_tokens in rendered sampling_params](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65389)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65389
- **Author:** [nien-hui](https://github.com/nien-hui)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 05:35:52
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65389/files) (3):**
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/render/test_render.py`
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/test_protocol.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/protocol.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a wrap model_serializer on GenerateRequest in the scale-out token-in-token-out protocol so that when max_tokens was provided (including the default value 16), it is written back into the dumped sampling_params dict. This fixes a bug where SamplingParams' omit_defaults dropped max_tokens=16, causing /inference/v1/generate to treat it as unset and fall back to server defaults (700/40943 tokens). Two tests were added covering the dump round trip and the /v1/completions/render endpoint.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the serialized wire format of GenerateRequest dumps: sampling_params now includes max_tokens when it was provided, even if equal to the default. This affects every render endpoint (chat, completions, messages, responses) and any code path that dumps a GenerateRequest. Bodies that omitted max_tokens still omit it, preserving server-default behavior. The serializer runs on every dump, adding a small per-request overhead.

**Potential risks**  
Version skew: a render server on main talking to a generate server with this PR still drops 16, and vice versa the fix only helps when the serializing side is patched. The change relies on is_sampling_param_provided semantics, so in-process requests are treated as fully provided, which could surface other default-valued fields later. Coverage gaps are acknowledged: Rust frontend, gRPC render, Cohere v2 render, RequestLogger body logging, and streaming/n>1 were not validated on this base.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between msgspec omit_defaults serialization and generate's key-presence-based is_sampling_param_provided check, which cannot distinguish a resolved default from an unsent limit. The fix is correctly placed on the serializing side since generate cannot recover the information once the key is absent. Only max_tokens is written back because it is the only field consumed by is_sampling_param_provided, keeping the change minimal and targeted.

## 106. [[Bugfix][Frontend] Pass the caller's prompt_token_ids to the chat derender parser](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63434)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63434
- **Author:** [Alex-Wengg](https://github.com/Alex-Wengg)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 05:18:05
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63434/files) (5):**
  - `docs/serving/online_serving/derenderer.md`
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/derender/test_derender.py`
  - `tests/parser/test_response_template.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/renderers/online_derenderer.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Non-streaming chat derender previously passed only generate_response.prompt_token_ids to the parser, which is unset unless return_token_ids is enabled, so parsers never saw the prompt and misclassified reasoning state (e.g. Qwen3 output after a closed </think> returned as reasoning). The fix falls back to the caller's prompt_token_ids, preferring the engine's full IDs when present, and updates docs and the DerenderChatRequest docstring to require full prompt IDs when a parser is configured.

**Technical impact**  
Changes behavior of the non-streaming chat derender path in vllm/renderers/online_derenderer.py by supplying prompt context to tool/reasoning parsers, aligning derender with the parser behavior introduced in #61857. Adds an E2E test via the render server and a response-template suffix test. No API surface changes beyond documentation; the fallback is a single expression with clear precedence.

**Potential risks**  
Callers sending only a prompt suffix may still misparse when the suffix starts after the last reasoning marker, which the docs acknowledge but cannot detect. The `or` fallback treats an empty list as falsy, which is benign but worth noting. New E2E test depends on downloading Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B and tokenizer availability, adding some CI resource cost despite being CPU-only.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between where prompt IDs are sourced (generate response vs. caller) across streaming and non-streaming paths. Preferring engine IDs over caller IDs is the correct precedence since the caller may send a suffix. The added suffix test documents the graceful degradation boundary (parses like full prompt while the opener is present, like no prompt afterward), which is a useful invariant for future parser work.

## 107. [[Perf][Model] DeepSeek V4/V4.1 Vision: encode a call's images in packed passes](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65030)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65030
- **Author:** [subhashpolisetti](https://github.com/subhashpolisetti)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 05:08:08
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65030/files) (4):**
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v4/common/vision.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v4/common/vl_model.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/amd/vl_model.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/vl_model.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds an encode_images helper in deepseek_v4/common/vision.py that packs a call's multiple images into a single forward_packed ViT+aligner pass when FlashAttention is used and there are 2+ images, falling back to the per-image loop otherwise. The V4 and V4.1 (NVIDIA and AMD) VL models are refactored to call this helper instead of looping per image, with the perm reordering moved to a split-based post-step. The goal is to cut per-image kernel-launch overhead and speed up multi-image encoding.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes for multi-image FlashAttention calls: encoding now runs as one varlen pass with per-image attention via cu_seqlens, giving reported 2.0-2.6x (V4) and 1.6-2.1x (V4.1) encoder speedups and large TTFT/throughput gains in serving benchmarks. Single-image and non-FlashAttention paths are unchanged. Peak encoder memory per call now scales with image count (up to ~1.7-2.0 GB more for 32-image calls), and packed output is not bit-identical to the per-image path due to different matmul row counts.

**Potential risks**  
Output is not bit-identical, and the PR disagrees with main on more answers than main does with itself (200 vs 96 of 1730), though scores are within noise; the two causes (rounding vs batching) are not separated. Peak memory growth on many-image calls could pressure memory in some configurations, and the startup KV-cache profile is only validated in the tested setup. V4.1 serving, MMMU-Pro for V4.1, AMD, thinking mode, and speculative decoding were not tested, and the unit test was removed, leaving accuracy covered only by an external MMMU-Pro run. There is also a merge conflict with #58782 in amd/vl_model.py.

**Key insights**  
The change reuses the existing forward_packed path already used by V4.1 encoder CUDA graphs, keeping the design minimal and gated to FlashAttention with 2+ images. The main trade-off is speed versus per-call peak memory and non-bit-identical output; the accuracy evidence is reasonable but limited (direct-answer setup, no finish-reason capture, untested V4.1 serving). The speedup is host-sensitive since the per-image path is kernel-launch bound, so benchmark variance should be considered when interpreting the headline numbers.

---

## 108. [[Bugfix][Kernel] hadacore: fix skipped wait_group on partial-chunk warp for odd row counts (#63514)](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63614)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #63614
- **Author:** [bandham-manikanta](https://github.com/bandham-manikanta)
- **Merged By:** [arpera](https://github.com/arpera)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 04:57:32
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63614/files) (2):**
  - `csrc/quantization/hadamard/hadacore/hadamard_transform_cuda.cu`
  - `tests/kernels/quantization/test_hadacore.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a correctness bug in the hadacore Hadamard transform CUDA kernel used by INT4 per-token-head KV cache. The diff_num_chunks sign was inverted, producing a negative switch index for the tail warp when the row count is odd, which silently skipped cp.async.wait_group and caused reads of incomplete shared memory (data corruption and illegal memory access). The fix corrects the sign and adds a default case that always waits on group 0, plus a parameterized regression test covering odd and large row counts.

**Technical impact**  
Restores correct synchronization semantics for partial-chunk warps: the tail warp now waits on the intended barrier count (wait_group 0 for a single chunk) instead of falling through the switch. The default fallback makes the switch total, preventing any future silent skip of the async wait. Behavior for even/full-chunk cases is unchanged since diff_num_chunks is 0 there. The new test exercises hidden_dim 64/128/256 across many odd and large row counts against a float64 reference.

**Potential risks**  
The default case uses wait_group 0, which is the most conservative wait; if a future refactor expects a different barrier count for an unhandled index, this could over-synchronize (minor perf) but not corrupt data. The test tolerance (atol=0.05, rtol=0.02) is loose relative to the claimed bit-exact match, so it may not catch subtle regressions as tightly as the reproduction script. Only validated on A100 per the description; other architectures with different cp.async behavior are not explicitly covered.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was a sign error combined with a non-exhaustive switch lacking a default, which turned a synchronization bug into silent corruption rather than a compile-time or obvious runtime failure. Adding a default fallback is a good defensive pattern for inline-asm switch dispatch. The regression test is valuable but could be strengthened by tightening tolerances or asserting bit-exactness against the float64 reference to match the validation claims.

---

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR retargets the AuxOutput indexer-replay feature branch (same commits as #49555) onto rl/aux-output, adding capture of ordinary sparse-attention TopK selections and exposing them as CompletionOutput.indexer_topk and a base64 .npy field on Python Chat/Completions, token-in/token-out, and Rust /inference/v1/generate responses. It generalizes the routed-experts AuxOutput path into a shared artifacts/buffer abstraction, adds a new IndexerCapturer with model adapters for DeepSeek V3.2/V4/GLM-5.x DSA and the MLA reference path, and wires a new enable_return_indexer_topk flag plus indexer_topk_prompt_start sampling parameter through Python and Rust.

**Technical impact**  
Touches 62 files across config, scheduler, worker connector, model layers, output processing, OpenAI/TITO protocols, and the Rust engine-core/llm/server/text crates. The AuxOutput SHM buffer now packs routed-expert and indexer bytes per token, capture is bound at connector construction (before KV capacity is known) with storage initialized later, and both capturers gain explicit close/unbind lifecycles with rollback on partial binding failure. Rust gains a new WireNdArray field, a generalized take_aux_output_rows concatenation helper, and independent prompt-start validation.

**Potential risks**  
The diff includes merged main updates since rl/aux-output is behind main, so review scope is larger than the feature itself. The docs explicitly state model support is an implementation claim pending validation, and CUDA Graph, real TP/DP, model quality, and performance still require later validation; Rust Chat/Completions artifact delivery is not implemented. Packing two artifacts into one SHM object changes byte accounting and namespace identity, and the new close/unbind ordering around CUDA graphs is subtle enough to risk use-after-free or leaked buffers if any path skips _release_capture_resources.

**Key insights**  
The design reuses the existing AuxOutput block-hash, async-copy, and prefix-restoration machinery rather than adding a parallel path, which keeps indexer and routed-expert return independently toggleable. Notable correctness details are DBO-aware capture offsets, forcing DeepSeek V4 backbone scoring so short contexts still produce fresh TopK, rejecting ROCm monokernel with indexer replay, and validating all capture targets before installing any callback. Test coverage is broad (new indexer capture tests, connector store tests, Rust fixture/round-trip tests, and CI job additions), but the PR's own notes flag that several validation dimensions remain outstanding.

## 110. [[Mamba][MRV2] Plan Triton ReplaySSM decode on the GPU and support Model Runner V2](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65033)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65033
- **Author:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged By:** [ZJY0516](https://github.com/ZJY0516)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 04:34:38
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65033/files) (11):**
  - `tests/kernels/mamba/test_replayssm_plan.py`
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_replayssm_metadata_builder.py`
  - `tests/v1/e2e/test_replayssm_decode.py`
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_utils.py`
  - `vllm/config/vllm.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/mamba/mamba_mixer2.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/mamba/ops/ssu_dispatch.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backend.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/mamba_attn.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu_input_batch.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu_model_runner.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Moves Triton ReplaySSM decode planning from CPU to a new GPU kernel (plan_replayssm_decode) driven by per-cache-slot ring counters shared within a KV-cache group, enabling Model Runner V2 support. Removes the V1-only replayssm_decode_base_cpu plumbing from CommonAttentionMetadata, the V1 input batch, and the V1 runner, and drops the config rejection of Triton ReplaySSM on MRV2. Also changes one-token prefill-to-decode rows to append to the ring instead of forcing a flush.

**Technical impact**  
Eliminates per-step CPU planning (~70 lines) and two .tolist() H2D copies in the Mamba metadata builder, replacing them with one small Triton kernel per KV-cache group per step. write_pos/is_flush become persistent buffers written inside CUDA graphs rather than copied in before replay. Align-mode block-boundary semantics (ring restart at block start, forced flush on block completion) are preserved in the kernel, and tracker sharing is extended from FlashInfer-only to all ReplaySSM layers.

**Potential risks**  
The behavior change for one-token final prompt chunks (append vs forced flush) is claimed mathematically equivalent but is not deliberately exercised by e2e tests, only by the new unit test. Correctness now depends on GPU ring counters staying consistent with prefill resets and on align-mode seq_lens being available; stale or misaligned counters could silently corrupt SSM state. TP2 parity and FlashInfer e2e tests were not run locally, relying on CI. The known mamba_cache_mode='all' limitation with Triton ReplaySSM remains unaddressed.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly centralizes planning in the first layer of each cache group and reuses the FlashInfer tracker infrastructure, which is a good consolidation. The new kernel test is thorough, checking every decode step against the removed CPU closed form across buffer lengths, align block sizes, padding rows, preemption-resume, and stale-slot cases. The main residual concern is the untested-in-e2e prefill_to_decode behavior change and the reliance on CI for TP2 and FlashInfer paths.

## 111. [[Bugfix][Watermarking] Skip incomplete opening contexts](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61194)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #61194
- **Author:** [TQCB](https://github.com/TQCB)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 04:30:09
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61194/files) (12):**
  - `.buildkite/test_areas/lm_eval.yaml`
  - `docs/features/watermarking.md`
  - `tests/watermarking/golden_candidates.py`
  - `tests/watermarking/test_engine_compatibility.py`
  - `tests/watermarking/test_parallel_compatibility.py`
  - `tests/watermarking/test_watermarking.py`
  - `tests/watermarking/test_watermarking_e2e.py`
  - `tests/watermarking/watermarking_goldens.json`
  - `vllm/v1/watermarking/gpu_sampler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/watermarking/spec_decode.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/sample/watermark.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/rejection_sampler.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR changes watermarking generation so that the first context_width output tokens are sampled with ordinary randomness instead of being watermarked against a -1-padded context. Previously, incomplete response-local contexts were padded with -1, which with a fixed key reused the same opening watermark randomness across prompts and reduced opening diversity. The fix adds an incomplete-context mask (contexts < 0) to the existing sampling skip masks across the GPU sampler, speculative draft kernel, and rejection sampler, and updates goldens, tests, and docs accordingly.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes for the opening tokens of every watermarked generation: the first context_width tokens now use ordinary sampling, which can cause later tokens to diverge since they depend on earlier choices. The change is applied consistently across Gumbel and dual-key Gumbel, standard and speculative sampling, and adds no host read or device synchronization. Detection preprocessing and PRF mappings are unchanged, but unwatermarked opening positions can dilute detector signal on short responses, as reflected in updated goldens and the relaxed repetitive-fixture p-value ratio guard (0.5 to 0.6).

**Potential risks**  
Engine-level GPU validation is still pending, and the retained model outputs only establish detection compatibility, not generation correctness with this patch. Short responses may now fail detection because unwatermarked openings dilute the signal, and the loosened p-value ratio guard slightly weakens a test assertion. The mask logic is duplicated across three code paths (gpu_sampler, spec_decode kernel, rejection_sampler), so future changes must keep them in sync. Tests now pin seeds because openings use ordinary randomness, which could mask nondeterminism elsewhere.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is that -1-padded opening contexts caused repeated watermark randomness across prompts under a fixed key, so skipping watermarking until a full context exists is a reasonable correctness fix. The implementation is careful to preserve deduplication semantics (prompt tokens still excluded, 'all' scope still searches prompt) and to avoid synchronization. The main open item is GPU engine validation, and reviewers should confirm the three mask implementations stay consistent and that the relaxed golden guard is justified.

## 112. [[Spec Decode] Support XPress + Acceptance Estimator](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65175)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65175
- **Author:** [tomasruizt](https://github.com/tomasruizt)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 04:27:11
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65175/files) (6):**
  - `docs/features/speculative_decoding/adaptive_verification.md`
  - `tests/v1/e2e/spec_decode/acceptance_rates/xpress/test_xpress.py`
  - `tests/v1/e2e/spec_decode/draft_model/test_draft_model.py`
  - `tests/v1/spec_decode/test_xpress_head.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/qwen3_xpress.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/spec_decode/xpress/speculator.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
XPress speculative decoding never fed the online acceptance estimator, so adaptive verification read an uninitialised confidence buffer and pruned all drafts after the first, making AV ~3x slower. This PR makes jacobi_refine_greedy optionally return the logits its argmax ran on (summed bias over vocab, or top-C candidate scores) and has XPressSpeculator pass them to _maybe_predict_acceptance, wiring XPress into the estimator. It also adds an AV variant to the XPress e2e test, a new enable_adaptive_verification field to the draft-model test harness, and extends the unit test to cover both topc paths.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes only when adaptive verification is enabled: the AV budget now receives real per-position acceptance estimates and verifies a sensible number of drafts instead of one. When AV is off, return_logits is False and no extra work or allocation happens, so the draft is bit-identical. The logits buffer in the non-topc path reuses the bias scratch buffer (bias.add_(base)), which is safe only because the next call overwrites it, and the topc path allocates a fresh tensor. Reported results show AV on going from a 26-71% throughput loss to an 8% cost at low concurrency and a 21-40% gain at c32+, with GSM8K accuracy unchanged.

**Potential risks**  
The returned logits alias the scratch bias buffer, so any future caller that retains them across calls would read corrupted data; this is documented but fragile. The row ordering assumption (req, draft slot) matching sample_idx_mapping and sample_col must hold, and a mismatch would silently feed the estimator wrong features. The topc path allocates a new [N*(B-1), C] tensor per step when AV is on, adding memory pressure at high batch sizes. Test coverage relies on the nightly XPress job and a GPU-gated unit test, so regressions may not surface in standard CI.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was a silent contract gap: draft_token_confidence_probs is allocated with torch.empty and only written by _maybe_predict_acceptance, which DFlash and DSpark call but XPress did not, so AV consumed uninitialised survivals. The fix is minimal and gated on acceptance_estimator being present, preserving the AV-off fast path. The performance data shows that a shorter, better-targeted verification budget can beat no AV at high concurrency, which is a useful signal that AV's value is load-dependent rather than uniformly positive.

---

## 113. [[Bugfix][KV Offload] Preserve SWA null-page offsets after store retry](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65279)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65279
- **Author:** [waizuichougou](https://github.com/waizuichougou)
- **Merged By:** [orozery](https://github.com/orozery)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 04:15:33
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65279/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/offloading_connector/test_scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/offloading/scheduler.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a KV offload store-retry bug where SWA could release and reallocate a GPU block, causing the scheduler to mark the old logical position as NULL_BLOCK_ID. Previously _build_store_jobs dropped null blocks from the source spec, shifting later pages into wrong CPU chunk offsets and corrupting loaded KV data. The fix keeps null blocks as placeholders in the source spec and excludes them from the fence set, plus adds a scheduler regression test.

**Technical impact**  
Changes source-spec construction in OffloadingConnectorScheduler._build_store_jobs so GPULoadStoreSpec.block_ids preserves full logical page layout including null placeholders, keeping source and destination chunk offsets aligned. start_gpu_block_idx is now set from the chunk base rather than the first non-null block. Null blocks are still copied (block 0 is zeroed) but not fenced as live allocations. Admission, eviction, retry, and partial-tail behavior are unchanged.

**Potential risks**  
Null pages are now included in the copy set, so correctness depends on block 0 being a permanently reserved, zeroed GPU null block; if that invariant ever changes, garbage data could be copied. The change is narrow and validated by a regression test and manual CUDA transfer checks, but the V1 KV Connectors GPU CI job is disabled, so automated coverage is limited and no model-level evaluation was performed.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is an offset-alignment bug: dropping null blocks from the source spec desynchronizes source pages from the CPU chunk slots that still reserve space for every page. Preserving placeholders is the minimal fix that maintains the chunk layout contract. The regression test correctly demonstrates the failure on the parent revision (8 vs 9 pages) and passes with the fix.

---

## 114. [[Bugfix][Scheduler] Don't split a multimodal item when a prefix cache hit ends inside it](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65271)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65271
- **Author:** [Anieca](https://github.com/Anieca)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 04:06:41
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65271/files) (3):**
  - `tests/models/multimodal/generation/test_mm_prefix_lm.py`
  - `tests/v1/core/test_scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a scheduler bug where, with disable_chunked_mm_input enabled, a multimodal item could still be split when a prefix cache hit ended inside the item and the current step lacked budget for the remainder. The guard in _try_schedule_encoder_inputs previously assumed chunks never start inside an item; it now distinguishes chunks starting before the item (roll back as before) from chunks starting on or inside it (schedule nothing until the rest fits). Adds a scheduler unit test and an end-to-end Gemma3 mm-prefix-LM test verifying hidden states match a standalone run.

**Technical impact**  
Changes scheduling behavior for requests whose prefix cache hit terminates inside a multimodal item: such requests now wait for a step that can cover the remaining item tokens in one chunk, preserving bidirectional attention correctness. The refactored condition removes the old `<=` comparison and splits the rollback logic into two branches, avoiding negative token counts. Touches core scheduler logic but is narrowly scoped to the disable_chunked_mm_input path.

**Potential risks**  
Requests may be delayed (stalled) if max_num_batched_tokens or long_prefill_token_threshold is smaller than the item, as the description acknowledges (pre-existing on main). The change overlaps with PR #62782 on the same lines, so merge-order conflicts are possible. The new e2e test relies on specific token offsets, block sizes, and environment flags, making it somewhat brittle to model/tokenizer changes.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a faulty invariant assumption: prefix cache hits are block-aligned and can land inside a multimodal item, so the guard must handle chunks starting inside items, not just before them. The fix correctly schedules zero tokens rather than a negative rollback in that case. Test coverage is strong, with both unit-level and end-to-end validation demonstrating the bug on base and the fix's correctness.

---

## 115. [[Bugfix] Disable CUTLASS FP4 MoE backend on sm_110](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62275)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #62275
- **Author:** [UsamaKenway](https://github.com/UsamaKenway)
- **Merged By:** [arpera](https://github.com/arpera)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 03:08:36
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62275/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/experts/cutlass_moe.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The MoE device-support predicate in CutlassExpertsFp4._supports_current_device() no longer claims device capability family 110, leaving only 100 and 120. This prevents NVFP4 MoE auto-selection from picking VLLM_CUTLASS on sm_110 (Jetson AGX Thor), where the grouped FP4 kernel faults during profile_run, so selection falls through to Marlin instead.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes only for sm_110 devices: NVFP4 MoE now routes to Marlin rather than CUTLASS, while sm_100 and sm_120 evaluate identically and dense NVFP4 (cutlass_scaled_mm_supports_fp4) is untouched. The change is a one-line predicate edit with no new code paths. On sm_110, small-shape MoE workloads that previously worked on CUTLASS now go to the slower W4A16 Marlin backend, a deliberate tradeoff since selection happens before shapes are known.

**Potential risks**  
The fix is hardware-validated only; there is no sm_110 CI runner and no added test, so regressions on Thor would not be caught automatically. The [nvfp4] padded-routes test case shifts from pass to skip on sm_110, reducing coverage there. If the underlying grouped kernel is later fixed for sm_110, this exclusion must be reverted, and the comment-only justification could be missed. Performance on sm_110 prefill may degrade for shapes that did work on CUTLASS.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is an ordered backend list where VLLM_CUTLASS precedes MARLIN and sm_110 passed a capability check inherited from a prior normalization PR that was never validated on Thor. The fault occurs after selection in profile_run with no retry, and is shape-dependent, so auto-selection must be correct because the global --moe-backend flag cannot express per-layer choice. Scoping the fix to the MoE predicate rather than the global FP4 helper is correct since dense FP4 works on sm_110.

---

## 116. [[Bugfix][LoRA] Account for CLIP CLS rows in LLaVA-NeXT-Video tower mappings](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62097)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #62097
- **Author:** [liulanze](https://github.com/liulanze)
- **Merged By:** [jeejeelee](https://github.com/jeejeelee)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 02:36:06
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62097/files) (2):**
  - `tests/models/multimodal/processing/test_llava_next_video.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/llava_next_video.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a LoRA tower-connector bug in LLaVA-NeXT-Video where get_mm_lora_token_counts() undercounted vision-encoder tokens per frame (576 vs 577) because it ignored the CLIP CLS token, causing the tower LoRA buffer to be undersized and triggering an assertion at startup or incorrect adapter mapping at runtime. The fix computes tower_tokens_per_frame in __init__ via get_num_image_tokens() (matching llava_next.py) and uses it in the hook instead of patch_grid_length**2. Tests are updated to reflect the new field and CLS-inclusive counts.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the encoder-token count returned by get_mm_lora_token_counts() for CLIP-based towers (now includes CLS), which correctly sizes the tower LoRA buffer and aligns the runtime token-to-adapter mapping. SigLIP-based configs are unaffected since get_num_image_tokens() returns the same value there. The new attribute is computed once at init, avoiding per-call recomputation.

**Potential risks**  
Behavior depends on get_num_image_tokens() correctly reflecting the tower's actual token output for all supported vision encoders; any encoder whose token count diverges from this helper would still mismatch. The change is scoped to llava_next_video.py, and the PR notes llava.py has a separate fix (#61799), so similar bugs may persist in other models. Validation is on a single A100 and one model, so broader encoder coverage is limited.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a subtle off-by-one between placeholder token counts and actual tower output due to CLS tokens, which only surfaces under tower-connector LoRA. The PR provides strong empirical validation (startup, runtime batching, bit-exactness without LoRA, and merged-vs-LoRA logprob deltas), and the test refactor to use tower_tokens_per_frame makes the CLS assumption explicit and testable.

---

## 117. [[Bugfix][MRV2][LoRA] Disable KV-sharing fast prefill for LoRA batches](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61162)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #61162
- **Author:** [waizuichougou](https://github.com/waizuichougou)
- **Merged By:** [jeejeelee](https://github.com/jeejeelee)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 02:35:11
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/61162/files) (3):**
  - `tests/v1/worker/test_attn_utils.py`
  - `vllm/config/cache.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/attn_utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a correctness bug in MRV2 KV-sharing fast prefill where the optimization reduced the batch to logits rows while LoRA mappings still described the full batch, causing wrong outputs or CUDA illegal memory access. The fix adds a guard in FastPrefillHelper.prepare() to return None when num_active_loras > 0, so LoRA and mixed batches fall back to regular prefill while base-only batches keep fast prefill. The PR also removes prior compact-metadata/graph-capture machinery and replaces the old dispatch test with a simpler fallback-and-recovery test, plus a docstring update in cache config.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change in the prefill dispatch path: any batch containing active LoRAs now bypasses fast prefill entirely, preserving full-batch attention metadata and LoRA mappings. Base-only batches, including those following LoRA requests, still use fast prefill. The change is small and localized (a single added condition), and the test verifies both the fallback and the recovery to fast prefill for subsequent base batches.

**Potential risks**  
Mixed base/LoRA batches lose fast-prefill savings for the whole batch, which is a performance tradeoff rather than a correctness issue. The new test relies on a MagicMock dispatch and a fixed PIECEWISE descriptor, so it may not exercise real graph-capture or padding interactions as thoroughly as the removed parametrized test. Real-weight E2E validation was done outside CI, so regression coverage in CI for the CUDA illegal memory access scenario is limited.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between logits-only batch reduction and full-batch LoRA metadata; the chosen batch-level fallback is simpler and safer than adding LoRA metadata and graph-capture machinery. The guard is placed correctly at the single eligibility decision point, and the test confirms state does not leak across batches. The main residual concern is the loss of fast-prefill performance for mixed batches and the reduced breadth of the new test compared to the removed one.

---

- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/62470/files) (2):**
  - `tests/lora/test_layers.py`
  - `vllm/lora/layers/vocab_parallel_embedding.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a LoRA bug where embed_tokens adapters on Transformers-backend scaled embeddings (VaultGemma, Gemma with --model-impl transformers) only received 1/embed_scale of their intended effect. The fix multiplies the slot's lora_b by the base layer's embed_scale in set_lora, matching PEFT behavior, and adds test coverage with a scaled embedding subclass and an embed_scale parametrization.

**Technical impact**  
Changes LoRA output for scaled embeddings to match PEFT 0.21.1, with validation showing delta ratio going from 0.0625 to 1.0 and max diff vs PEFT dropping from 1.11 to 2.4e-07. The scale is applied once at adapter load time on the device copy, so there is no per-step cost. Embeddings without embed_scale (all native vLLM models) are unaffected since the getattr returns None.

**Potential risks**  
Adapters trained with PEFT 0.17 or earlier saw an unscaled delta in the unmerged training forward, so this change may alter behavior for those older adapters, as the PR itself notes. The fix relies on getattr for embed_scale, which is a duck-typed convention rather than an explicit interface, so future scaled embedding implementations must follow the same attribute name. No GPU end-to-end serving run was performed, and the full test_layers.py suite has pre-existing failures on the author's machine, limiting confidence in broader regression coverage.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is that the Transformers backend rebases a HF ScaledWordEmbedding onto VocabParallelEmbedding but keeps its forward, which scales the lookup while the LoRA delta is added unscaled. Applying the scale to lora_b at load time is a clean, zero-runtime-cost fix that mirrors PEFT's merged-adapter semantics. The test design using a _ScaledVocabParallelEmbedding subclass is a reasonable proxy for the real ScaledWordEmbedding and meaningfully exercises the new path.

## 119. [[LoRA] Add DeepSeek V4.1 language-model adapter support](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57245)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57245
- **Author:** [HollowMan6](https://github.com/HollowMan6)
- **Merged By:** [jeejeelee](https://github.com/jeejeelee)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 02:34:26
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57245/files) (11):**
  - `tests/lora/test_layers.py`
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_deepseek_v4_swa_visible.py`
  - `vllm/lora/layers/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/lora/layers/custom_layer.py`
  - `vllm/lora/utils.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/attention.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/common/grouped_wo_a.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/compressor.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/model.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/ops/o_proj.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/vl_model.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds LoRA adapter support for DeepSeek V4.1 language-model layers, including multimodal checkpoints. Introduces a new DeepseekV4GroupedWoA layer and DeepseekV4CompressorKVScoreLinear, plus custom LoRA wrappers (DeepseekV4GroupedWoAWithLoRA, DeepseekV4CompressorKVScoreWithLoRA) that apply per-group adapter deltas to the grouped output projection and the FP32 compressor projection. Also registers packed module mappings and multimodal module mapping, disables GEMM-RS and mega-attention paths when LoRA is enabled, and fixes a weight-loading edge case.

**Technical impact**  
Refactors wo_a from a plain ColumnParallelLinear into a dedicated grouped projection class and routes the compressor's fused wkv/wgate through a new linear that preserves FP32 output. LoRA wrappers are registered ahead of generic wrappers in from_layer ordering, and attention class selection now falls back to the split-KV FlashMLA path when LoRA is active. Behavior changes for LoRA-enabled configs (GEMM-RS and mega-attn disabled) and for the grouped projection call path even without LoRA.

**Potential risks**  
The grouped LoRA wrapper asserts BF16 input and manually loops over groups, which could be a performance regression and may break if input is quantized. Fully-sharded handling keeps full LoRA-A per rank, which is subtle and could be misconfigured. Disabling GEMM-RS and mega-attention under LoRA changes performance characteristics and may surprise users. The compressor wrapper assumes exactly two packed modules and relies on dtype-specific delta construction. Weight-loading change to skip None name_mapped could mask mapping failures.

**Key insights**  
The core challenge is that fused GEMM kernels bypass adapter application, so the PR adds explicit LoRA epilogues and disables fusion paths that cannot apply adapters. Tests cover grouped projection, fully-sharded LoRA, and FP32 compressor output across fp8/gate/aux_stream variants. Tower/connector LoRA is intentionally deferred, keeping vision frozen. The PR is well-scoped but touches attention dispatch and kernel selection, warranting careful review of the LoRA-disabled path for regressions.

## 120. [[Rust Frontend] Add the stateless Responses API (streaming and non-streaming)](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/63902)


### Base Information

- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 02:18:06
