  - `rust/src/chat/src/stream.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/middleware/offload.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/http_client_tests.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/chat_completions/convert.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/mod.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/responses.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/responses/convert.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/responses/streaming.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/responses/types.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/responses/validate.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/utils/types.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/tests/responses.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/lib.rs`
  - `rust/src/text/src/lower.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new stateless OpenAI Responses API (POST /v1/responses, streaming and non-streaming) to the Rust frontend, built on the existing ChatLlm pipeline. It introduces new route handlers, wire types, request lowering/validation, an OutputItemStreamer state machine that maps ChatEvent into Responses SSE events, non-streaming as a fold over the same state machine, plus supporting changes: EffectiveSampling exposure on ChatEventStream/PreparedTextRequest, a generate_prepared split in TextLlm, a ChatTool From<Function> impl, offload-path registration, and ~1.2k lines of route tests.

**Technical impact**  
Touches the chat and text crates (new public sampling() accessor, refactored generate path) and adds a sizable new server module with typed DTOs, validation, and streaming conversion. Reuses existing chat lowering, tool handling, and error envelopes, and registers the new routes and offload path. Behavior is additive for existing endpoints, but the text/chat refactor changes internal call flow and the new endpoint adds a large public surface that must stay aligned with the Python frontend.

**Potential risks**  
Large diff (+4478) with a complex streaming state machine and many compatibility no-ops (reasoning.summary, text.verbosity, encrypted reasoning, service_tier) that could silently diverge from Python semantics. The deliberate difference of emitting response.failed with partial output instead of a generic error frame may surprise clients. Store-dependent features are rejected rather than supported, and the refactor of TextLlm::generate/generate_raw could affect existing callers if not fully covered. Validation notes mention 27 pre-existing test failures and that model-backed E2E was not repeated after the final rebase, so runtime confidence rests largely on deterministic route tests.

**Key insights**  
The design is streaming-first: non-streaming is a fold over the same OutputItemStreamer, ensuring output items and IDs are consistent across modes, which is a clean architectural choice. Echoing engine-resolved sampling via EffectiveSampling matches Python's from_request behavior and is well tested. The PR is thorough on validation and test coverage but is large and introduces compatibility shims that warrant careful human review against the Python implementation before merge.

## 121. [[Recipes Tool][CPU] Remove obsolete TP/DP NUMA binding workaround](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64797)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64797
- **Author:** [louie-tsai](https://github.com/louie-tsai)
- **Merged By:** [bigPYJ1151](https://github.com/bigPYJ1151)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 02:06:49
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`

  - `tools/recipes/hardware_detection.py`
  - `tools/recipes/recipe_json_to_vllm_config.py`
  - `tools/recipes/sweep/scheduler_fallback.py`
  - `tools/recipes/sweep/scheduler_sweep_prepare.py`
  - `tools/recipes/sweep/sweep_generation.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Removes the temporary TP/DP NUMA-binding workaround from the Recipes tooling now that vLLM PR #62867 provides native per-DP-rank NUMA affinity. This deletes the --tp-dp-numa-bind-workaround CLI flag, the build_numa_omp_threads_bind() helper and its CPU-list compression utility, and the env override logic in recipe_json_to_vllm_config.py, while updating SWEEP_TUNING.md. The PR also adds a new scheduler_fallback.py script and reorders scheduler sweep candidates, which is unrelated to the stated NUMA cleanup.

**Technical impact**  
Removes roughly 120 lines of obsolete binding logic and relies on vLLM's automatic CPU NUMA/OpenMP binding, preserving recipe-provided VLLM_CPU_OMP_THREADS_BIND values. The new scheduler_fallback.py preserves a previously validated SLA-feasible concurrency baseline when scheduler tuning fails, and sweep_generation.py now wires it into run_full_sweep.sh. Scheduler candidate ordering changes to test isolated parameters before combinations, altering sweep behavior.

**Potential risks**  
Correctness depends on the deployed vLLM image containing PR #62867; older images will regress to multi-DP ranks sharing NUMA nodes. The scheduler fallback is a behavioral addition bundled into a cleanup PR, expanding review scope and risk, and it assumes concurrency-recommendation.json exists with a valid status. The fallback also silently swallows scheduler failures, which could mask real tuning regressions.

**Key insights**  
The NUMA workaround removal is a clean, well-justified deletion, but the PR mixes in an unrelated scheduler fallback feature and candidate reordering that deserve separate review and testing. Validation claims rely on manual Xeon sweeps and log inspection rather than automated CI coverage. Consider splitting the scheduler changes into their own PR and adding tests for scheduler_fallback.py error paths.

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
In KimiVLConfig.__init__, pad_token_id is now assigned directly to the instance (self.pad_token_id = pad_token_id) and removed from the super().__init__ keyword arguments. This fixes a TypeError raised when the base PreTrainedConfig is dataclass-based and does not accept or forward the undeclared pad_token_id field.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior is preserved for callers passing pad_token_id= since the constructor signature is unchanged, and the attribute is still set on the config instance. The change decouples KimiVLConfig from the base class's handling of unknown kwargs, making it robust across different PreTrainedConfig implementations (e.g., upstream vs. MoonshotAI-modified base).

**Potential risks**  
If the base PreTrainedConfig previously relied on receiving pad_token_id to register it as a serialized field or to propagate it to sub-configs, setting it only on the instance could subtly alter serialization or downstream behavior. The PR claims serialization is unaffected, but this is not backed by an added test in the diff, so regression risk is unverified.

**Key insights**  
The fix is a minimal, targeted workaround for base-class incompatibility rather than a broader refactor. The main gap is the absence of a regression test in the changed files; the validation described appears to be manual reproduction only, so CI coverage for the dataclass-base scenario should be confirmed.

## 124. [[Model Loader] Direct reload: lay the checkpoint out where a relocated weight now starts](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65281)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65281
- **Author:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged By:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 01:19:57
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65281/files) (2):**
  - `tests/model_executor/model_loader/test_direct_reload.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/model_loader/reload/direct.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a correctness bug in VLLM_RELOAD_MODE=direct where weights relocated by post-load processing (e.g. set_ into a fused buffer) were reloaded at the stale recorded storage_offset, overwriting neighboring weights. record_checkpoint_layout now also stores the storage data_ptr, and _checkpoint_views falls back to the tensor's current storage_offset() when the storage has changed. A regression test moves proj.weight into a new buffer at an offset and verifies reload matches a cold start with stable addresses.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the internal _ckpt tuple shape from 3 to 4 elements, which is a private contract but is consumed by tests and any code touching _ckpt. The fix is localized to reload/direct.py and only alters behavior when a tensor's storage identity changes between layout recording and reload, otherwise behavior is unchanged. Restores DeepSeek-V4-Pro GSM8K accuracy after reload (0.885 back to ~0.96) and eliminates the 30 moved-tensor mismatches.

**Potential risks**  
The _ckpt tuple format change could break external or out-of-tree consumers that unpack it as a 3-tuple, though it appears private. Using data_ptr() as a storage-identity proxy is generally sound but could theoretically collide if a storage is freed and a new one reuses the same address; the fallback to current storage_offset() is also a heuristic that assumes the relocated tensor's own offset is correct, which holds for the set_ fusion case but may not cover all relocation patterns. Test coverage depends on the toy model faithfully reproducing the fusion behavior.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is that storage_offset is only meaningful relative to a specific storage, so recording it without recording storage identity is unsafe once post-load processing can rebind tensors. The data_ptr comparison is a pragmatic, low-cost way to detect rebinding. The strong end-to-end validation on MI355X (cold vs reload parity, perturbed reload correctly degrading to 0.035) gives high confidence, but the fix is heuristic and the private tuple format change warrants a quick check for other _ckpt consumers.

---

## 125. [[Model Loader] DeepSeek-V4 ROCm: keep the preshuffle scales out of the module's parameters](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65296)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #65296
- **Author:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged By:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 01:18:13
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/65296/files) (2):**
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v4/amd/model.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v4/amd/rocm.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a crash in DeepSeek-V4 on ROCm under VLLM_RELOAD_MODE=direct where prepare_attn_preshuffle and prepare_gateup_preshuffle assigned scale nn.Parameter objects directly as module attributes, causing nn.Module.__setattr__ to register them as duplicate parameters that reload could not find (KeyError). The fix stores ws.detach() plain tensors and assigns them via refill_derived so reloads refill them in place at the addresses captured CUDA graphs read, and applies the same treatment to _wo_a_e8m0_scale, _wo_a_cos_cache, and _wo_a_sin_cache. Imports of replace_parameter and refill_derived were also hoisted to module top.

**Technical impact**  
Prevents duplicate parameter registration on the attention and gate-up preshuffle modules, eliminating the reload KeyError and keeping derived tensors stable across reloads so captured CUDA graphs remain valid. The test table shows reload of original weights now matches cold accuracy (0.9650 vs 0.9675) with zero moved tensors out of 3480, and perturbed-weight reload correctly degrades to 0.0350, confirming weights are actually reloaded rather than silently stale.

**Potential risks**  
The fix depends on refill_derived semantics correctly handling None values (e.g. when _prep returns None for a missing weight) and on detach() sharing storage so in-place refill reaches the graph-captured address; any divergence in those assumptions could reintroduce stale-tensor bugs. The change was validated only on MI355X with TP8 and specific flags, so other ROCm configs or non-gfx950 paths (where _prepare_fp8_wo_a is skipped) are less exercised. It also relies on the companion PR #65281 for full correctness of the compressor-fusion offset on reload.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a subtle nn.Module.__setattr__ side effect: assigning an existing Parameter to a new attribute name silently registers a duplicate parameter, which breaks weight-reload bookkeeping. Using detached plain tensors plus refill_derived is the right pattern for derived tensors that must stay at fixed addresses for CUDA graph capture, and the data_ptr report (0/3480 moved) is strong evidence the approach preserves graph-captured buffers.

## 126. [[Bugfix][KV Offload][P2P] Settle buffered terminal messages after disconnect](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64299)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64299
- **Author:** [jiuyueQ](https://github.com/jiuyueQ)
- **Merged By:** [orozery](https://github.com/orozery)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 00:53:13
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64299/files) (4):**
  - `docs/features/kv_offloading_usage.md`
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/tiering/p2p/test_sessions.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/tiering/test_p2p_lifecycle_e2e.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/tiering/p2p/session/session.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a P2P KV offload bug where a poll batch containing both a disconnect and a terminal confirmation (TransferDone or AbortAck) for a completed transfer would drop the terminal message, causing a successful load to be reported as a failure. The session dispatch guard now allows buffered terminal messages to settle their matching loads after a peer disconnect or socket-monitor death, while still dropping work-starting messages and disabling the exception for local protocol faults, send errors, and other non-peer failures. Tests and docs are updated accordingly.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a new _accept_buffered_terminals flag on the session that is set false whenever _mark_dead is called with a reason other than 'peer disconnected', so only genuine peer/socket-monitor disconnects permit buffered terminals to be dispatched. Terminal messages still pass existing validation and match by request ID and round, and AbortAck settles the deferred release without producing a second failed outcome. Data-plane teardown and slot-release contracts are unchanged, so the change is localized to the P2P session dispatch path.

**Potential risks**  
The correctness hinges on the string comparison reason != 'peer disconnected' in _mark_dead; any future caller that marks death with a different reason string for a legitimate peer disconnect would silently disable the buffered-terminal exception. A malformed terminal in the same batch disables subsequent terminals, which is intentional but could interact subtly with ordering. The new e2e test relies on monkeypatching ZMQ internals and a 64 MiB KV cache budget to avoid memory profiling overlap, which is somewhat brittle and environment-dependent; upstream GPU CI on H200 MIG was still pending at submission.

**Key insights**  
The fix is a narrow, well-scoped change to the dead-connection dispatch guard, distinguishing peer-initiated disconnects from local faults so that already-received terminal confirmations are not lost. Validation is strong: component tests, a real-weight LLM regression that fails on main and passes with the fix, and explicit coverage of protocol-fault rejection including malformed terminals after peer disconnect. The PR is a deliberate split from a larger change and documents its independence from related PRs.

## 127. [[Bugfix][ROCm][MoRIIO] Wait for MoRIIO WRITE mode completions in short chunks](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64330)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64330
- **Author:** [kliuae](https://github.com/kliuae)
- **Merged By:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 00:46:58
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64330/files) (3):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_moriio_tp_ack.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_moriio_transfer_completion.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/moriio/moriio_engine.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The MoRIIO WRITE/READ completion wait previously issued a single MoRI wait_all call with the full transfer_timeout. On MoRI's xGMI backend that call polls statuses once and then sleeps until its deadline, so a still-running copy held the wait for the entire timeout (30s default) even when all statuses had already succeeded, and under load the deadline could expire while statuses were still IN_PROGRESS, failing the transfer and leaving decode requests hanging. This PR replaces the single call with a loop of wait_all slices capped at 10ms, checking statuses via poll_transfer_batch between slices, honoring fail_fast, and preserving the same timeout and error messages.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the core completion-wait path in MoRIIOWrapper.waiting_for_transfer_complete used by both the WRITE producer before write_done and the READ barrier. Behavior now returns promptly on success, raises immediately on failure when fail_fast=True (WRITE) or once no status is in flight when fail_fast=False (READ), and still enforces the overall transfer_timeout. This fixes a real correctness/latency bug: reported xGMI waits drop from ~30s to ~0.2s, and P/D serving throughput at concurrency 16 improves from 2.28 to ~7.1 req/s with no failed waits. Adds a small per-slice polling overhead and a new module constant _NATIVE_WAIT_SLICE_S.

**Potential risks**  
The 10ms slice is a fixed heuristic; on very slow or heavily loaded backends it could add polling overhead or, conversely, still miss completions if MoRI's per-call polling semantics differ from assumptions. The fail_fast=False path now waits until all statuses are terminal, which could extend latency if a status never reaches a terminal state, though the overall deadline still bounds it. Tests rely on mocked wait_all and a fake clock, so real xGMI/scatter-gather behavior is only validated by the PR's manual benchmarks, not CI. Edge cases around sub-millisecond timeouts and the ceil rounding of slice_ms deserve scrutiny.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between vLLM's assumption that wait_all blocks until completion and MoRI xGMI's actual poll-once-then-sleep-until-deadline behavior, which silently converted a fast transfer into a full-timeout stall and, at the deadline, into a spurious IN_PROGRESS failure with no notification to decode. Slicing the wait and re-polling statuses is a pragmatic workaround that also restores fail_fast semantics that the native path previously ignored. The PR is well-evidenced with a reproducer and serving benchmarks, but the fix is a workaround for backend semantics rather than a change to MoRI itself, so it should be revisited if MoRI's wait_all behavior is corrected upstream.

---

## 128. [[Test][KV Offload] Update stale partial-tail scheduler expectations](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64993)


### Base Information

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Updates stale test expectations in tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/offloading_connector/test_scheduler.py to match current scheduler/event-tracker contracts. The Mamba partial-tail test now asserts the checkpoint block size (4) and predecessor-linked parent hash instead of placeholder values, and the EAGLE round-trip test now compares load-job keys as an ordered tuple in prefix order rather than a set. These changes fix three tests that fail on main after PRs #62613 and #63249.

**Technical impact**  
Test-only change with no production code impact. It aligns assertions with the event tracker emitting checkpoint block size and predecessor hash, and with the scheduler preserving ordered key tuples for load jobs. This restores coverage of the partial-tail COW and EAGLE round-trip behaviors and makes the suite pass on current main.

**Potential risks**  
Low risk since only test assertions change. The main risk is that the new expectations are tightly coupled to internal details like block_hashes indices (5, 6) and block size 4, so future scheduler or event-tracker changes could require further updates. No CI config or markers are touched, so no broader pipeline impact is expected.

**Key insights**  
The failures stemmed from contract drift rather than product bugs: #62613 changed Mamba event reporting and #63249 changed load-job key ordering. Using maybe_convert_block_hash and make_offload_key keeps assertions consistent with the helpers used elsewhere. Validation shows 3 focused tests and the full 467-test file passing, confirming the fix is scoped and effective.

---

## 129. [[Bugfix][KV Offload] Count each key once per request toward store_threshold](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64558)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #64558
- **Author:** [sohom-cs](https://github.com/sohom-cs)
- **Merged By:** [orozery](https://github.com/orozery)
- **Merged time:** 2026-11-03 00:02:00
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/64558/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/cpu/test_manager.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/cpu/manager.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a bug in CPUOffloadingManager where each retry of a failed prepare_store() call, or a re-offer after preemption, incremented a key's store_threshold count again, letting a single request admit a cold chunk via its own retries. Adds a counted_keys set to _RequestCacheAccess so each key counts once per request, and makes the STORES_SKIPPED metric count a key's skip once per request as well. Tests are updated to use distinct request contexts where they previously simulated multiple requests through one context, and two new tests cover the retry and preemption cases.

**Technical impact**  
Changes admission behavior for the CPU offloading tier when store_threshold >= 2: keys offered repeatedly by the same request (retries, resumed preempted requests) no longer accumulate toward the threshold, while offers from other requests still do. The counted_keys set lives alongside store_miss_keys and seen_keys on the per-request access record, so it is scoped to the request lifetime and reset on cache reset. No change to how chunks are written or read, and no behavior change when store_threshold < 2.

**Potential risks**  
The fix relies on the scheduler preserving a request's ReqContext across preemption and dropping it on finish; if any path creates a fresh context for a resumed request, counting would restart and the bug could resurface. Existing tests that reused one context to stand for multiple requests had to be rewritten, which suggests some callers may have implicitly relied on the old per-call counting semantics. Validation was CPU-only with no end-to-end GPU run, so real-world admission behavior under pressure is not directly exercised.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is that prepare_store() counted offers before allocation, so a None return (allocation failure) left the count incremented while the scheduler re-offered the same keys. The PR aligns the threshold counter with existing per-request deduplication patterns (store_miss_keys, seen_keys) and correctly ties the skip metric to the same once-per-request semantics. The added tests are well-targeted, parametrized over thresholds 2 and 3, and explicitly verify that other requests' offers still count.
